  transcript is keyed to the **session's launch directory**, which for every
  manager session in this run was a different repository. The predecessor never
  hit it because it ran the command from its own cwd, where the default happens
  to be right. **A default that is correct for the author and wrong for the
  reader is the shape that survives review.** The failure is loud, which is the
  only reason it is a finding rather than a hole in the ceiling.
- **F-23 — the environment pin went stale mid-run with nobody touching the
  project.** Both the plugin commit and the `claude` CLI moved between two tasks.
  The plugin move was proved inert by **hashing** the scripts rather than reading
  the diff; the CLI move could not be proved inert at all. **The plugin is a
  symlink into a working repository other sessions commit to**, so "plugin
  commit" is not a version this project selects and holds — it is a live pointer
  to somebody else's HEAD and can move *in the middle of a dispatched step*. The
  ENV pin is **a record of what the tools were, not a lock on what they are.**
- **The guard's `chmod` false positive has two instances in two different
  scripts**, and the record reads as though it covers both when it covers one:
  `guard.py` (recorded via T-2's close) and `root_guard.py` (the `+x` *flag*
  parsed as a filename at a repository root). Both belong to the family F-6
  names. **A finding recorded once against a family with two implementations
  will be read as covering both.**

### The estimate defect, and the first thing that would repair it rather than measure it

**Self-reported budgets across this run: 20.6×, 41×, 21×, 6.4× wrong.** Two
workers on different tasks independently reported the identical figure
`tokens=45000`, against metered 928,376 and 1,675,103 — it is **close to a
constant guess rather than an estimate**, and several workers disclosed in their
own notes that they cannot read their own counters.

**Then one worker anchored.** T-2's S-3 implementer wrote, unprompted, that its
estimate was *"anchored on this task's own metered history rather than guessed
from nothing"*, cited S-1's and S-2's real metered figures, and predicted its own
error direction. **It came in at 1.38×.**

**Hypothesis, n=1: the problem is not that workers estimate badly, but that they
are asked to estimate with nothing to anchor on.** Round three candidate: the
dispatch template carries prior metered figures for the task as a field. *This is
already being tested* — T-4's brief carries T-2's and T-3's metered figures down
and requires each step worker to say what it anchored on, which should give n=2,3,4.

**Task sizing, three independent samples, all near 2×:** T-5 2.3×, T-3 2.43×
(6,080,116 tokens, `$6.10`, against 2,499,600 estimated), T-2 1.92× on workers
alone and 2.03× including its three step verifiers. Different models, different
step counts, different per-step costs — and they agree on the multiple. **The
`plan` skill's refusal to smooth the round count into a per-step constant looks
better than it did at the start of the run.**

**F-9 was beaten on one task and not the other.** T-3's supervisor read every
`meta/budget.json` before every close and reported three complete pairs — the
first task in the project's life to do so. T-3.S-1's figure was still lost the
same way on the other.

### The verification layer rejects things the record cannot see

`grep -coE "verify [A-Za-z0-9-]+ FAIL" RECORD.md` returns **0**. The task files
hold **two genuine FAILs**, both on T-2:

- **S-1 FAIL** — a `leak` in the supervisor's own transcription of a worker's
  verbatim report.
- **S-2 FAIL**, and this is the instructive one: **the shipped test suite was
  correct and the worker's prose about it was false.** Its self-reported mutation
  table claimed a mutant left the price-decrease case green. It does not,
  confirmed independently three times.

**C-1 called the ratio misleading in the project's favour and could not say why.
C-2 can: step verdicts live in task files and only task-level ones reach the
record, so the grep measures the wrong population.** No better regular expression
repairs it.

**The generalisation is worth more than the count.** This run has now caught a
worker's own prose wrong about **its budget** and wrong about **its
verification**. Self-reported evidence is unaudited in exactly the way
self-reported cost is, and only an adversary reconstructing the claim found it.

**What worked, and it is a transferable dispatch technique.** Both manager
verifier briefs said *"report gaps as a result, not as an apology"* and both
returned real gaps — including one verifier disclosing **two bugs in its own
harness and a mutation that proved nothing**. And the strongest evidence in the
run came from one clause: *"construct the part-way case directly rather than
trusting a mock"* produced a **kernel-raised `EFBIG`** via `RLIMIT_FSIZE` with an
ignored `SIGXFSZ`, rather than a monkeypatched exception. **Naming the cheap
substitute you do not want beats asking for adversarial effort in general** — an
adversarial instruction without a named alternative leaves the worker to pick its
own cheapest attack.

### Checks that fire correctly on states the protocol requires to exist

- **F-19 — `one-sided-link` fires for the whole duration of every independent
  verification.** A supervisor sets its task `DONE`; the manager must not mark
  the requirement `discharged` until a fresh verifier returns (P-18), and the
  board's own Legend defines `DONE` as *closed, verified, and released*. So the
  tree is necessarily one-sided throughout, and `check_trace` calls it a defect
  the entire time. **Nobody errs. Any project running P-18 meets it at every
  close.** The gate cannot simply exempt the class — the real version, a manager
  who closes a task and forgets the requirement half, is what the check was built
  for. **The distinguishing fact is whether a verifier is outstanding, and
  nothing in the tree records that.**
- **A newly created file declaring a new identifier is invisible to `check_refs`
  until staged**, because it enumerates with `git ls-files`. So a checkpoint
  declaring its own id fails the gate until `git add`. Refinement found later:
  `check_refs` enumerates from the index but **reads from the working tree**, so
  a modification to an already-tracked file needs no staging — only a genuinely
  new file does, and then by explicit path, never `-A`.
- **The forward-reference class fired four times in one day**: a borrowed
  checkpoint id from another project's namespace, the correction that named the
  id while explaining the error, C-2 naming a checkpoint it had not yet filed,
  and a task id named in a recommendation. **A check that scans prose cannot
  distinguish discussing a token from emitting one**, and every author who met it
  here met it while explaining the previous instance.

### The client took the recommended option five times out of five

Across three batches and five decisions — the budget ceiling's unit, its
figures, the attribution convention, the dispatch width, and the cross-repository
write target.

**P-25's recommendation is not advice being weighed here; it is the decision
being ratified.** Two consequences:

- **The drafting of the recommendation IS the judgement.** An option's wording
  deserves the care of a decision rather than of a suggestion.
- **An escalation window measures the manager's own delay, not the client's
  deliberation**, when the client will take the recommendation either way. This
  is persona-dependent rather than general — a client who genuinely weighs
  options is exactly who the window is for.

**And the manager cannot tell the two apart from inside.** A client who agrees
and a client who ratifies produce the identical artifact: a selected option
label. P-24's minuting rule — write *"client selected &lt;label&gt;"* rather than the
client's own words, because the label is the manager's sentence — turns out to be
the only instrument that measures this at all, and only in retrospect.

**P-27 has still never fired**, after two opportunities, both answered inside the
hour. It needs work in flight while the client is genuinely away, which no window
value produces.

### What the two rotations measured

- **`resume` §5's late-write case did not occur at either rotation** — recorded
  as two *measured negatives* rather than two absences. The second was a
  near-miss worth keeping: the predecessor's last write landed **after** the
  pointer was written, so *"the commit before the rotation"* would have been
  wrong and only *"the last commit before the lock commit"* was right. **The
  hazard's shape was present; only its consequence was absent.**
- **Ten record defects across two rotations, five each.** Not one was withheld by
  a predecessor; every one was something no file was shaped to carry. **Four were
  repaired by the outgoing manager while it was still alive** at the first
  rotation, and more at the second — a position a crash does not offer.
- **The session-id-to-name join is unfixable by writing and recurred verbatim.**
  `BOARD.md`, `.run/session/manager` and `handoff-ready` carry a session id;
  `ListAgents` prints a name and a short ref; nothing carries both. A session
  cannot read its own name, so the join exists only by asking a live peer — and a
  crashed predecessor cannot be asked. **It needs a mechanism.**
- **The guard's replaced-manager branch fired for the first time**, correctly
  telling an outgoing manager *"you have been REPLACED … do not take the lock
  back"* — distinct from the stranger recipe, and the reason `handoff-ready` is
  appended to rather than deleted. **But an incoming manager that wrote
  `.run/session/manager` before `BOARD.md` named it got the GENERIC takeover
  recipe** — written for a writer that is gone, delivered to one arriving at a
  planned rotation whose predecessor was demonstrably alive. It did no harm
  because the actions coincide. Ordering is load-bearing and the skill lists the
  steps in the right order **without saying that the order is load-bearing**.

#### The instrument that reaches past the defect list

**Every question logged at a rotation is shaped by what the incoming reader
already suspected was missing.** The ten defects are all of that kind, so the
list is bounded by the successor's imagination and systematically excludes
whatever both parties take for granted.

The only instrument against that is a deliberately **open-ended** question to the
outgoing session — *"what should I know that I have not thought to ask?"* Asked
once at the second rotation, it returned: F-24, F-25, the two-instance `chmod`
family, a stale cost line on an open question, and a list of the predecessor's
**unverified beliefs**. **Round three candidate: make it a step in `resume` §0
rather than a thing a successor thinks of.**

**A related defect the incoming manager filed against itself:** its rotation
report asked the *client* to backfill cross-session continuity — *"if you told
another session something you expect me to know, I do not have it."* **That is
not a client question.** A client is the authority on what to build and on
decisions that are genuinely the owner's; the owner is not a transport for state between two
sessions of a pipeline built to carry its own.

### Two claims still unverified, carried forward deliberately

Recorded because a refuted finding acted on all day is worse than an open
question:

- **`promote.lock`'s stale entry "cannot" refuse a promotion** — asserted by two
  managers, independently, both reasoning from `_apply()` opening the file `"w"`
  under `fcntl.flock`. **Neither ever ran a promote with a stale lock present.**
  Two readings of the same source are one observation, not two.
  *(T-4's brief names this as an untested inference and carries the first real
  test of it.)*
- **T-5's 2.3× overrun** was inherited from C-1 and never re-derived. If the
  near-2× trend across three tasks is leaned on, re-derive the first point.

---

### Candidate items for round three, collected

1. **Scope `promote`'s uncommitted-remainder check to the task's declared
   paths.** Highest value per line changed; three blocked promotions this run,
   one costed at `$1.95`.
2. **A findings index generated from the roadmap rather than maintained.** 88
   findings across eleven subcycle files today; a hand-maintained index is the
   rule a rushed session skips.
3. **A roll-up step with an exit code** moving the target project's findings into
   the subcycle file at close. Done once by hand at C-1 and the gap reopened by
   C-2, because nothing makes it happen.
4. **Provenance on findings — measured vs inferred.** F-10 was cited as
   established on three confounded observations.
5. **A declared residual risk names its watcher or says nobody watches it.**
6. **Escalation cannot be satisfied by prose** — a question is asked when it has
   a row, a class and a window.
7. **Class has no default and cannot be omitted.**
8. **Prior metered figures as a dispatch-template field** (already under test on
   T-4).
9. **The open-ended question as a step in `resume` §0.**
10. **The client-profile interview questions** — phrased as *what do you want to
    happen* rather than *how experienced are you*, with the constraint that
    **what gets recorded never varies**, only whether the client is interrupted.
11. **A client channel for injecting an urgent finding mid-run** — no such route
    exists; the only channel is answering escalations the loop raises.
12. **A final checkpoint readable by a non-developer.** C-2 cites finding
    classes, rule numbers and check names throughout; the artifact best placed to
    let a novice client verify they got what a development team owes them is
    currently written for a successor manager.

<Cycle 1, 2026-09-10 to 2026-09-11 — RECORD.md lines 1–537 — written up by a reader of `pricelog`'s record, every sentence cited to it.>

### The manager made six commits in the `git add -A` form `run` §4.5 forbids, harmless only because no worker existed yet
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:305, :306; rollup §9 (staging refinement only)

Six commits were made as `git add -A devteam/` followed by a bare `git commit`, a form `run` §4.5 forbids "by name, in both halves", because the shared index lets the manager commit a worker's staged work under its own message (RECORD.md:305). The record says every one was harmless "and the reason is luck rather than judgement: no worker existed until minutes ago" (RECORD.md:305). The form felt necessary because the manager believed every change had to be staged before `check_refs` could see it; in fact `check_refs` enumerates with `git ls-files` and reads the working tree, so only a new file needs staging (RECORD.md:306, already in rollup §9).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "From here the form is `git commit -F <file> -- <explicit paths>`": stage new files one by one, gate, then commit by pathspec (RECORD.md:305–306).
- **For v3.** A manager commit helper that stages only named paths and refuses when the index holds anything else (suggested here, not in the record).

### The session-id-to-name join logged twice as unfixable is solvable from transcripts, and the lookup fails silently if it takes the last match
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:136, :256, :509, :510, :511, :512; overtakes rollup §11 (third bullet)

Both earlier rotations recorded the join between a board session id and a `ListAgents` name as fixable only by a mechanism, "because a session cannot read its own name" (RECORD.md:136, :256), and rollup §11 carries that as current. At the third rotation: "any party can read another session's" name, because a transcript at `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl` holds its own `/rename` output, and `grep -o 'Session renamed to: [A-Za-z0-9_-]*' <path>/<id>.jsonl | head -1` maps an id to a name, verified on three sessions (RECORD.md:509–510). `tail -1` returns an empty name once sessions have discussed the method, because the transcript then contains the search pattern: 11 matches in the manager's own transcript where there should be one (RECORD.md:511). The record calls this "a method that gets more broken the more it is discussed" (RECORD.md:512).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The grep above, with `head -1` and never `tail -1` (RECORD.md:510–511).
- **For v3.** Write the session name onto the board when the lock is taken, so recovery needs no transcript search (suggested here, not in the record).

### Taking the writer lock is refused whenever the board edit and the session-pointer write share one command
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (skill fix deferred by the client until the run ends, RECORD.md:528)
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:142, :262, :263, :523, :524, :525, :526, :528; overtakes rollup §11 (fourth bullet)

At the second rotation the guard refused the incoming manager's first write, and the record's diagnosis was "Ordering, not exemption": write `BOARD.md` first (RECORD.md:262; rollup §11). At the third rotation the manager used that order inside one command and was refused again, atomically, with nothing applied (RECORD.md:523). The correction: the earlier diagnosis "is right across commands and wrong within one", and the "BOARD.md is always writable" escape "holds only when `BOARD.md` is written ALONE" (RECORD.md:524). The explanation offered — `guard.py` reads `BOARD.md` from disk when the hook fires, before any part of the command runs — is marked "a SOURCE READING rather than an experiment" (RECORD.md:525), and the first rotation had already seen that "a compound command is judged whole before any part of it runs" (RECORD.md:142). Both managers hit it on their first devteam write while following `resume` §0 step 4 and `run` §7b, which describe the bundled shape (RECORD.md:526); the incoming manager was also handed the stranger's "take the lock" recipe while its predecessor was alive (RECORD.md:263).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The fix is one clause in two skills": say that the two writes must be separate commands (RECORD.md:526).
- **For v3.** Add that clause to `resume` §0 step 4 and `run` §7b (RECORD.md:526), or make lock-taking a single scripted step (the script suggested here, not in the record).

### The predecessor's last-write sha keeps moving for as long as the predecessor is alive
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:252 (rollup §11), :519

`resume` §0 step 5 asks for the predecessor's last-write sha because the record cannot supply it (RECORD.md:519). At the third rotation the answer was `e65a233`, then `3dae8e0`, then `8d10b79` within fifteen minutes, each correct when given, because the predecessor kept recording the defects the successor's questions exposed (RECORD.md:519). The second rotation's near-miss, a last write landing after the pointer, is already in rollup §11 (RECORD.md:252).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Ask last, after the predecessor has said it is finished, or ask again at the moment of the lock commit" (RECORD.md:519).
- **For v3.** Have the successor read the sha from git at the lock commit rather than ask for it earlier (suggested here, not in the record).

### The recovery table sends every dead agent to re-dispatch, though a live session can resume one killed by a transient error
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:352, :353; F-17 (rollup §1)

T-4's supervisor was killed mid-turn by a transient API error (ENOTFOUND) while holding 33 uncommitted lines of its worker's report transcription, and `resume` §4's rule that "an uncommitted change under a claimed task's scope is WORK, not debris" decided what happened next (RECORD.md:352). It was resumed rather than re-dispatched with `TREE: dirty`, an option "the harness supports and which no part of `run` or `resume` mentions" (RECORD.md:352). §3's table "was written for a session that has restarted", and it does not separate a supervisor that died of an API error, which is safe to resume, from one that died of a logic failure (RECORD.md:353). The record also narrows the working rule taken from F-17: "do not resume supervisors" holds for one that is waiting and is "exactly wrong for one that is dead" (RECORD.md:353).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond naming the missing distinction (RECORD.md:353).
- **For v3.** A "resume in place" row in the recovery table, keyed on how the agent died (suggested here, not in the record).

### Indenting the ATTRIBUTION field's second trailer line does not fix F-8; identical text produced different commit histories (F-27)
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (T-6's brief moved both lines into `NOTES:`; the result falls after this slice, RECORD.md:535)
- **Evidence.** F-8 (RECORD.md:66; 0.2.9.md Findings "Four defects in the tooling"), F-27 (RECORD.md:348, :350, :399), RECORD.md:146, :299, :535

F-8 recorded that an unindented second trailer line parses as a new field, so worker commits carried no `Claude-Session` line (RECORD.md:66), and D-6 "explicitly does not repair" it (RECORD.md:146). T-4's dispatch indented the second line to test whether that was the whole problem (RECORD.md:299). One of T-4's first four step commits carried `Claude-Session` (RECORD.md:350), and the tally closed at 2 of 5 after S-5's worker "read 'ATTRIBUTION lines' as plural and stripped the continuation indent itself" (RECORD.md:399). The record judges this worse than a uniform failure: "Indentation does not fix F-8; it relocates the decision from the template to the reader" (RECORD.md:399).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The field needs a shape, not a convention" (RECORD.md:350); T-6's brief wrote both lines as prose in `NOTES:` instead (RECORD.md:535).
- **For v3.** A multi-line trailer field with a defined grammar in the dispatch template (RECORD.md:350), or trailers appended by the supervisor at promotion (the second option suggested here, not in the record).

### A step verifier dispatched before the step's report is committed always fails on `no-report` (F-30)
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-30 (RECORD.md:437, :438, :439), RECORD.md:314; F-19 (RECORD.md:206; rollup §9), overtaken in part at RECORD.md:369, :486

The S-1 verifiers of T-4 and T-8 both failed their first pass on `no-report` alone, with sound work, because the supervisor had not yet committed the transcribed report (RECORD.md:314, :438). "Two occurrences on two consecutive tasks with two different supervisors makes it structural" (RECORD.md:438). The record groups it with F-19 (`one-sided-link` during P-18's verification window) and the unstaged-identifier `cited-undefined`: all three "are resolved by ordering rather than by exemption", two are enforced in the manager's gate, and this one "belongs in the supervisor's" (RECORD.md:439). Rollup §9 says of F-19 that nothing in the tree records whether a verifier is outstanding; RECORD.md:369 corrects that — "The board's in-flight table records it exactly" — and the allowance was later re-keyed to the task named in the requirement's `in-progress (T-n)` after it refused a legitimate commit (RECORD.md:486).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Transcribe and commit the report, then dispatch the verifier" (RECORD.md:438); for F-19, allow `one-sided-link` only while the requirement's `in-progress (T-n)` task is in the in-flight table (RECORD.md:369, :486).
- **For v3.** Put the F-30 ordering into `supervise` (RECORD.md:439) and ship the F-19 allowance in a plugin gate (the gate suggested here, not in the record).

### Supervisors under-filled the step dispatch template, and a worker had to break the discipline to proceed correctly
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:203

For all three of T-3's steps, SANDBOX-ROOT, MODEL-BAND, GATE, VERIFY, AUDIT and NOTES "were dropped as labelled fields, some of their substance folded into prose instead" (RECORD.md:203). S-3's worker noted that the discipline says a missing required input means BLOCKED "and PROCEEDED ANYWAY while recording the deviation" (RECORD.md:203). The record: "That is the right call and the discipline does not license it — which makes it a defect in the discipline, not in the worker"; the worker caught the under-fill, not the supervisor (RECORD.md:203).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond the diagnosis (RECORD.md:203).
- **For v3.** Check a step dispatch's required fields mechanically before it is sent (suggested here, not in the record).

### When a supervisor prescribes the interface or the exact fix, every worker implements its defect faithfully
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:308, :341, :393, :394, :460, :482

T-4's supervisor fixed the module interface before dispatching any step, because three separately-run workers cannot negotiate it (RECORD.md:308); its exception clause was too narrow, and "three workers faithfully implemented a specification whose exception clause was too narrow" (RECORD.md:341). The record names the cost: it "trades three independent chances to notice for one authoritative chance to be wrong", and no step's verify could catch it because each verified against the same specification (RECORD.md:341). The manager's own prescribed `_get` boundary still leaked `http.client.IncompleteRead` (RECORD.md:394), T-8's supervisor's interface note carried the `splitlines` hole (RECORD.md:460), and T-8's S-5 brief gave a literal fix whose "behave identically" argument was false for a raw carriage return, caught only because the worker ran the parametrisation the brief called optional (RECORD.md:482).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Even a structural fix needs its own adversarial pass to prove the boundary has no gap in its own construction" (RECORD.md:393); "testing one representative case tests the argument rather than the claim" (RECORD.md:482).
- **For v3.** Plan an adversarial pass against any interface or fix a supervisor prescribes (following RECORD.md:393; making it a planned step is suggested here, not in the record).

### Committing promptly does not stop F-5: seconds of dirty `RECORD.md` still pinned a worker's overlay (F-26)
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-5 (RECORD.md:63, :70; rollup §2), RECORD.md:144, :162, :168, :194, :195, :196, :224; F-26 (RECORD.md:316, :318, :320), RECORD.md:330, :346; C-1 §4; C-2 §4

The first rotation adopted "the manager commits promptly" as F-5's mitigation (RECORD.md:144). F-26: the manager wrote and committed in one shell invocation, "a dirty window of a few seconds", and `T-4-S-1-000451` was still pinned on `devteam/RECORD.md`, because "promptness reduces the probability and not the possibility" (RECORD.md:318). It was replaced by a mechanism: draft record entries in the session scratchpad and append them only when no sandbox is RUNNING, corrected from "no open sandbox", which could never be true while supervisors keep sandboxes for F-9 (RECORD.md:320, :346). The next promotion was clean, "n=1, and the confound is stated" (RECORD.md:330). No manager discipline touches task-against-task pins (RECORD.md:162, :168).

Corrections to rollup §2. It lists this half's three pinning parties as a supervisor's task file, the manager's `RECORD.md`, and T-3's S-1 against T-2 "recovered without a re-dispatch"; RECORD.md:196 and C-2 §4 list a manager's bookkeeping, a task file held by another supervisor, and the manager's record, and RECORD.md:162 says the T-3/T-2 collision "cost a sandbox and a recovery dispatch". It says the manager "violated it personally within the hour"; RECORD.md:195 says "three hours after", and that "the reason is structural rather than a lapse of care". The record's own count is inconsistent too: RECORD.md:70 gives five hits before C-1 ("both of T-1's steps and three times in T-5"), C-1 §4 and RECORD.md:144 give four, and RECORD.md:162 calls the T-3/T-2 case the fifth.
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Scope `promote`'s uncommitted-remainder check to the task's declared paths, "the only real repair" (RECORD.md:196, :224); meanwhile draft outside the repository and land only when nothing is RUNNING (RECORD.md:320, :346).
- **For v3.** `0.2.9.md`'s Findings names a different repair, re-deriving the check fresh at promote time, but F-5's own text says the edit is "baked into the overlay's upper layer" (RECORD.md:70), which a fresh derivation would still read; test both before choosing (noted here, not in the record).

### A worker that follows the work discipline always leaves an out-of-scope edit that `promote` refuses (F-31)
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-31 (RECORD.md:445, :446, :447, :471, :472, :473), RECORD.md:196, :536; C-3 §4

The work discipline tells every worker to append its report to the task file, and "the task file is never in any step's declared scope, by this design's own construction", so a compliant worker always leaves an uncommitted out-of-scope edit that `promote` refuses (RECORD.md:471). On T-8 it hit three of four successful worker dispatches, each costing a six-command cherry-pick recovery, and "the one worker that did not hit the trap is the one that disobeyed" (RECORD.md:472). T-4 escaped only because its supervisor told workers not to touch the task file, and that instruction did not reach T-8 because "dispatches are not inherited" (RECORD.md:446). C-3 calls it "the largest process finding of this run" (C-3 §4). The worker could write a path outside its scope (RECORD.md:446), while the F-5 scoping repair assumes "the guard already refuses out-of-scope writes" (RECORD.md:196), so the two repairs change the same check on different assumptions (noted here, not in the record).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Either tell workers under `structural` containment to skip the task-file append and rely on the final message alone ... or teach `promote` to tolerate an uncommitted change confined to the task's own task-file path" (RECORD.md:473); T-6's brief told its supervisor to land reports host-side itself (RECORD.md:536).
- **For v3.** Adopt one of the two repairs as the default, and design it together with F-5's scoping repair (the first from RECORD.md:473; the pairing suggested here, not in the record).

### `sandbox.py`'s inventory cannot carry a recovery: spent looks unpromoted, the pid is missing, and residue is invisible
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:95, :245, :260, :303, :322, :327, :333; F-9 (RECORD.md:74; rollup §7); overtakes rollup §12 (first bullet)

The sandbox file records `pid -`, which "silently disables one of P-14b's two recovery readings", because the recovery table turns on whether the pid is live (RECORD.md:303). Since `close` destroys `meta/budget.json` (F-9), supervisors keep sandboxes until they are read, so `status` shows a growing list that is mostly finished work — four open and three spent on T-4 — and "the distinction between spent and unpromoted lives only in the task file's prose" (RECORD.md:95, :333). A recovering session could therefore "re-promote what is already in history" (RECORD.md:327). A leftover overlay workdir with no `upper/` and no `meta/` is invisible to `status`, which keys on `meta/`, while `resume` §3 tells a recovering session to trust that instrument (RECORD.md:245, :260). The stale `promote.lock` that rollup §12 carries as an untested inference did not block S-1's promotion when tried, so that item is now observed (RECORD.md:322).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond naming each gap (RECORD.md:260, :303, :327).
- **For v3.** Keep the pid, a spent or promoted flag and the budget figure in metadata that survives `close`, and have `status` report anything under the sandbox root it cannot classify (suggested here, not in the record).

### Commands run through a sandbox wrapper lose `PIPESTATUS`, because the wrapper is a `#!/bin/sh` script and `/bin/sh` is dash
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:366, :367

An S-4 worker reported `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` reading empty; the manager's own shell returned 0, so the finding was first recorded as environment-dependent (RECORD.md:366). The supervisor found the mechanism: `sandbox.py` generates every dispatched and `run`/`exec`'d command as a `#!/bin/sh` script, and dash "has no `PIPESTATUS` array at all", reproduced as `Bad substitution` (RECORD.md:366). It therefore fails deterministically on Debian- and Ubuntu-derived machines and "silently defeats the exact 'capture the exit code directly' discipline this task's own brief insisted on" (RECORD.md:366). The record credits the narrow first statement of the finding with making the cause findable (RECORD.md:367).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The portable form is a redirect to a file and `$?` on the next line" (RECORD.md:366).
- **For v3.** Generate the wrapper as a bash script, or state the POSIX-only constraint in the work skill (suggested here, not in the record).

### A verifier's PASS does not record what it attacked, so a PASS on one function reads like a PASS on the gate
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:372, :374, :376, :397, :404, :411; C-3 §3c

T-4's S-4 was accepted on a real PASS whose scope was `parse_response` only, "narrower than the Gate it was judging", and the task was later re-opened from DONE (RECORD.md:397). The manager's verifier then found leaks in the request path — `fetch_price("BT C")` raising `http.client.InvalidURL` live — which had survived "four step verifications, a dedicated adversarial REFUTE, and a supervisor's close" because "every reader followed the requirement's framing rather than the Gate's words" (RECORD.md:372, :374). That verifier made the gap visible by passing R-5's literal Acceptance and failing the Gate in one verdict (RECORD.md:376), and the next verifier answered the two separately (RECORD.md:404). "A verifier's coverage is a fact about the verdict and nothing in the pipeline records it" (RECORD.md:397).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond answering acceptance and gate as separate questions (RECORD.md:376, :404).
- **For v3.** A coverage field in every verdict, naming what was attacked and what was not, with acceptance and gate answered separately (suggested here, not in the record).

### Python mutation testing is silently invalid for a same-size edit restored within one second, because of the bytecode cache
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:377, :491

T-4's verifier restored `fetch.py` by copy within the same clock second after a line-reordering mutant, so "the stale `.pyc` from the mutant validated as fresh, and every probe it ran afterwards executed MUTANT bytecode while the source on disk was correct" (RECORD.md:377). It noticed because `inspect.getsource` and a traceback's line disagreed, confirmed by decompiling, and re-ran everything under `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` with a guard that disassembles the functions (RECORD.md:377). The task's three earlier mutation proofs survived only because their mutant changed the file's size (RECORD.md:377). T-8's verifier later proved each mutant was loaded with a module-level sentinel read back from the loaded module, and ran a no-op negative control (RECORD.md:491).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` with a loaded-code guard (RECORD.md:377); a loaded-module sentinel and a negative control (RECORD.md:491).
- **For v3.** Put both into the verify and tester disciplines for Python projects (suggested here, not in the record).

### Tests that could not fail were common, and were found only by building mutants and watching the suite stay green
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:342, :395, :396, :406, :443, :481, :491, :496

T-4's first REFUTE found an HTTPError test that never confirmed its fake was invoked, so a regression to a direct import would "make a REAL NETWORK CALL, and leave the suite green", plus a type-only assertion that both clause orderings satisfy (RECORD.md:342). Later rounds found an unanchored `pytest.raises(..., match=...)` that cannot tell wrapped from unwrapped errors, and socket tests that pass without loopback, "four times in five steps" (RECORD.md:395–396). A removal mutation that turns nothing red can mean the broad boundary absorbed it rather than that the test is blind (RECORD.md:481). T-8's verifier broke all 71 new tests with 42 mutations and a negative control, and still found that raising on a blank line is guarded by no committed test (RECORD.md:491, :496).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The full square — green against a reference implementation, red against the stub, red against each specific mutation (RECORD.md:443); key the probe on a branch-specific signal and try a leniency variant when a removal yields no red (RECORD.md:481); a negative control (RECORD.md:491).
- **For v3.** Make "shown able to fail" a required part of verifying any test step (suggested here, not in the record).

### Bare `pytest` could not import the package while `python3 -m pytest` could, so the convenient form hid the defect (F-16, F-29)
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (D-10)
- **Evidence.** F-16 (RECORD.md:157), F-29 (RECORD.md:417, :419, :420); Q-4 and D-10 (RECORD.md:413); C-2 §2; C-3 §2

F-16 recorded that no test file could import the package and that no live task's scope covered a shared fix, so each task carried its own `sys.path` shim (RECORD.md:157; C-2 §2). F-29, found while checking D-10's fix: from the repository root "the `pytest` BINARY cannot import the package; `python3 -m pytest` can", because `-m` puts the working directory on `sys.path` (RECORD.md:419–420). Every Acceptance and Verify line names the bare form while agents "habitually run `python3 -m pytest`", so "a verifier running the convenient form would certify a suite that the requirement's own literal command cannot collect" (RECORD.md:420). The first test of the fix passed both with and without it and "would have certified the fix on no evidence" until re-run from a neutral directory (RECORD.md:419).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** D-10, `pythonpath = ["."]` in `pyproject.toml`, which closes it for both forms (RECORD.md:413, :420; C-3 §2).
- **For v3.** Require verifiers to run the literal Acceptance command rather than an equivalent (suggested here, not in the record).

### Measurements taken inside the sandbox were extrapolated to the real filesystem, and the gap was never closed
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:84, :202, :215

T-3's REFUTE held, but "'atomic on this filesystem' was measured on the SANDBOX's ext4, and the real unsandboxed data directory was never observable from where T-1's probe ran" (RECORD.md:202). T-3's verifier ran all its mutation work on overlayfs, "the same boundary T-1's digest already names, now confirmed as still un-crossed by anybody" (RECORD.md:215). Durability against power loss was also left unscoped by T-1's digest (RECORD.md:84). "Recorded, not resolved, and nobody has asked for the re-measurement" (RECORD.md:202).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded.
- **For v3.** Where a requirement depends on the host filesystem, plan a probe outside the sandbox or write the limit into the requirement (suggested here, not in the record).

### Membership fixes to one exception boundary did not converge; a structural boundary did, after its own adversarial pass
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:337, :339, :340, :373, :375, :393, :394

T-4's first REFUTE broke the claim that three observed responses suffice for R-5: `RecursionError` and an int-string-limit `ValueError` escaped `parse_response` (RECORD.md:337, :339). The clause had listed expected failures beneath a requirement saying "the known members are a sample and NOT the definition" (RECORD.md:340). The widening went into the wrong function, and the manager's verifier found seven or more further leaks in `_get` and `_url_for`: "each fix was correct, verified, and left the class open" (RECORD.md:373, :375). The structural boundary that replaced the list still leaked, because "an exception raised INSIDE an except clause is never offered to that try's sibling clauses" (RECORD.md:394).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The repair is a translation at the boundary, or an explicit, disclosed decision about what deliberately is not converted" (RECORD.md:375), plus an adversarial pass on the boundary itself (RECORD.md:393).
- **For v3.** Carry "close a class at a boundary, then attack the boundary" into brief and REFUTE guidance (following RECORD.md:375, :393).

### Several checks report clean when they did not look, indistinguishably from a real clean (F-32)
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-32 (RECORD.md:475, :476), RECORD.md:86, :356, :512; F-24 (RECORD.md:283; rollup §6); C-3 §4; 0.2.9.md Findings "`check_trace` reported `clean` having parsed zero goals and zero requirements"

F-32: all four of T-8's workers wrote `tokens=~<n>` because none can read its own counter, and `check_report`'s regex needs a digit after `=`, so none was flagged although they were wrong by 36×, 24×, 15.6× and 16.6× (RECORD.md:476). On T-4, `check_report` turned from red to `clean` because the supervisor closed its sandboxes, and "a check that cannot find its comparator reports clean rather than reporting that it could not run" (RECORD.md:356). `check_scope`'s `foreign-write` fires only while a task title reads RUNNING, so "a check that is silent because nothing is running looks identical to a check that is silent because nothing is wrong" (RECORD.md:86). The record names the family, which includes F-24's `&&` skip: "the instrument returns the shape of 'nothing wrong' when what happened is 'nothing was looked at'" (RECORD.md:512).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None specific for `budget-mismatch` (RECORD.md:356, :476); `0.2.9.md` proposes the same principle for `check_trace`, that parsing nothing from a non-empty file is a finding, not `clean`.
- **For v3.** Give every check a distinct "not evaluated" result with its reason, and read `~n` as an approximate figure rather than skipping it (suggested here, not in the record).

### Checks bound only once commits were gated on their exit codes, and managers had to build that gate themselves
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-12 (RECORD.md:96), F-19 (RECORD.md:206), F-24 (RECORD.md:283); RECORD.md:99, :164, :190, :207, :208, :209, :237, :247, :264, :272, :273, :370, :425, :430, :432, :486, :487

Before any gate existed there were four ungated commits for three reasons: another live task's file red over F-12, twice, because a project-wide gate "deadlocks whenever a concurrent task's file is red" (RECORD.md:99, :164); a semicolon that ran the checks and ignored them (RECORD.md:190); and F-19's mandated window (RECORD.md:209, which counts itself the third). The gates the managers then wrote caught real defects at the moment of writing, such as N-4 in a cell and an identifier named in its own explanation (RECORD.md:246–247, :272–273). They also refused legitimate commits on true findings about states the procedure requires, which the record called "too strict for this phase" (RECORD.md:207–208, :237); RECORD.md:247 nonetheless tallies that gate's three refusals as "a real defect, none a false positive". Each permissive branch was mutation-tested both ways before it was trusted, because each made a check quieter (RECORD.md:264, :370, :487); the parser fails closed (RECORD.md:430); and the gate now refuses untracked files under `devteam/` after the staging trap recurred a fourth time (RECORD.md:425, :432).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The ordering -- stage, then gate, then commit -- has to live in the gate script rather than in anyone's memory" (RECORD.md:425); the F-19 rule at RECORD.md:486.
- **For v3.** Ship a commit gate in the plugin with these rules, scoped so that a concurrent task's red file cannot block an unrelated commit (the scoping suggested here, not in the record).

### The trace walks no chain from a done-means to a gate and allows one discharging task per requirement
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:413 (D-8, D-9), :428, :429, :495, :499; C-3 §1, §5

C-3 found that DM-11 (D-8) has two halves and only one had an owner: detection was built in T-8, but "T-6's gate did not name it, so nothing would have made T-6 build it and `check_trace` would have read clean throughout" (RECORD.md:499). The cause is that "nine of the eleven done-means are named by no requirement at all -- the DM-to-task link is not a chain this project's traceability walks" (RECORD.md:499; C-3 §1). T-8's verifier separately showed, by testing three configurations, that "the model allows exactly one discharging task per requirement", so T-8's re-establishment of R-2 has no term and sits in prose (RECORD.md:495). An earlier claim that the vocabulary lacked a term had been wrong — "'The model cannot express what I mean' and 'I have misunderstood the model' look identical from the inside" — which is why the second claim was tested before it was recorded (RECORD.md:428–429, :495).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** C-3 amended T-6's gate to name DM-11 in its own commit (RECORD.md:499; C-3 §1).
- **For v3.** Have `check_trace` walk each done-means to the gates that discharge it, and add a term for a second task that re-establishes a discharged requirement (suggested here, not in the record).

T-8 wrote its adversarial step into the plan with the claim stated in advance, and the step broke the claim before the task reported DONE (RECORD.md:426, :459). T-8 ran 12,926,279 tokens and `$14.495`: 5.17× on tokens and 1.0045× on minutes, the first time wall-clock landed, and its Estimate line had said such a run would be "the plan working, not failing" (RECORD.md:485; C-3 §4).

### Anchoring self-reports on metered figures cut T-4's error to 1.14–1.97×, but T-8's workers still ran 15.6–36×
- **Area.** A8 planning & estimates
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:223, :358, :359, :400, :401, :434, :476; C-3 §4; extends rollup §7 (n=1)

Rollup §7 carries the anchoring hypothesis at n=1: T-2's S-3 at 1.38× (RECORD.md:223). All five T-4 steps were given metered figures and came in at 1.97×, 1.90× (the only overstatement), 1.14×, 1.57× and 1.18×, against an unanchored 20.6×, 41×, 21× and 6.4× (RECORD.md:358, :400). The record concludes only that "anchoring on any real metered figure collapses the error by an order of magnitude, and the refinements are not yet separable", and that minutes stayed unlearnable (RECORD.md:359, :400–401). T-8's brief carried the series forward (RECORD.md:434), yet its four workers ran 36×, 24×, 15.6× and 16.6× (RECORD.md:476). C-3 §4 attributes this to `check_report` not parsing `tokens=~N` (F-32); that explains why the misses went unflagged, not why they were large, and the record in this slice does not say whether T-8's step workers were given anchors (noted here, not in the record).

- **Area.** A11 budget & cost

The client settled F-14: the ceiling accumulates across rotations and the manager half is counted in tokens (RECORD.md:147), which D-7 set at 400M manager tokens plus `$75` of worker cost (C-2 §2). F-33: a verifier or auditor dispatched through the Agent tool "has no sandbox and writes no budget file, and it is not the manager's session", and T-8 alone ran six such dispatches, 572,082 tokens (RECORD.md:479; C-3 §3d). The spot-price digest said the unknown-coin behaviour was inferred from the API's general error convention and recommended observing it; observed, it was HTTP 404 with a gRPC-style body rather than 400, and a malformed pair returned HTTP 200 with an array of about 400 objects, "which a status-code-only client would read as success and crash on" (RECORD.md:43). ### A relayed cause entered the record unchecked, the manager's own check was blind, and only a prior baseline settled it (F-28)
- **Area.** A13 record & evidence
- **Status at the stop.** open (F-28 was corrected and widened by its own author at RECORD.md:449–452; no new F-n)
- **Evidence.** F-28 (RECORD.md:385, :387, :388, :389), RECORD.md:449, :450, :451, :452, :454, :463, :465, :466

T-4's S-5 kill was attributed to "host memory pressure" by relaying the kill notification's own text; the manager checked `free`, PSI and `dmesg`, found nothing, and had the supervisor record "cause not established" (RECORD.md:387). After a second kill, cgroup counters showed four real OOM kills that "every instrument the manager used is blind to", so the earlier check was a false negative (RECORD.md:450). The author replaced F-28's generalisation: "'I checked and found nothing' is worth exactly as much as whether the instrument can see the thing", and "a negative result needs its coverage stated as much as a positive one needs its evidence, and nothing in this pipeline asks for it" (RECORD.md:452). The "supported" middle position was itself too strong, because the counter had only been read after the kills (RECORD.md:465). A baseline of 4 written before the next kill and unchanged after it established that T-8's S-5 kill was not a kernel OOM, while T-8's S-3 kill, with no baseline, stays un-attributed (RECORD.md:454, :463, :466).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Read the counter before the event, "one command" (RECORD.md:454); state what an instrument can see alongside any negative result (RECORD.md:452).
- **For v3.** A coverage clause for negative results in the finding format (following RECORD.md:452; the field suggested here, not in the record).

- **Area.** A13 record & evidence

T-4's supervisor "silently substituted ASCII hyphens for four of a worker's em dashes while transcribing its report", an unsanctioned edit under P-17 that "no tool anywhere catches"; it found the change itself by a byte-level diff and then extracted reports programmatically (RECORD.md:364). "P-17's verbatim requirement has been enforced by nothing but authorial care for this project's whole life" (RECORD.md:364). On T-8, S-4's verifier verdict added a whole step, but a verifier cannot write, so its verdict "exists only in the dispatch notification and in the supervisor's own upward report", and S-5's worker, told to read it "in full" from the task file, found only a paraphrase (RECORD.md:483).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Programmatic extraction with a byte-for-byte containment check before each commit (RECORD.md:364).
- **For v3.** A check that a transcribed report is byte-identical to the worker's original, and a committed home for verifier verdicts (suggested here, not in the record).

### Dispatched agents were killed with memory cited while the kernel killed nothing, which the record attributes to a harness heuristic firing with 65–135 GiB free
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform

Three step dispatches in this slice were killed with harness text citing memory (T-4's S-5, T-8's S-3 and T-8's S-5), and T-4's supervisor was separately killed by a transient API error, ENOTFOUND (RECORD.md:352, :384, :387, :450, :463). With the kernel's `oom_kill` counter at 4 before T-8's S-5 kill and 4 after it, that kill "was definitively not a kernel OOM" (RECORD.md:454, :463). The record concludes that "the harness has a low-memory heuristic of its own, distinct from the kernel's OOM killer, and it fires with 65-135 GiB free" (RECORD.md:464); what was measured is the unmoved counter, and the heuristic is inferred from it and the harness's wording (noted here, not in the record). T-8's S-3 kill had no prior reading and stays un-attributed (RECORD.md:466).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None; the practice recorded is reading the counter before the event (RECORD.md:454).
- **For v3.** Treat agent kills as expected: count them per task and keep them out of the attempt count (suggested here, not in the record).

<Cycle 1, 2026-09-11 to its close on 2026-09-13 — RECORD.md lines 538–929 — written up by a reader of `pricelog`'s record, every sentence cited to it.>

### The manager relayed one claim to the client and closed its own concern on another without measuring either, and both were false.
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-57 (RECORD.md:795-796), F-58 (RECORD.md:797-798); verify T7-lint-1606 FAIL (RECORD.md:790); RECORD.md:762, 777.

The manager told the client that all four of the blind reader's guesses were "steps the author knew and did not write" without opening the README, and README lines 19-20 answer guess 1 (RECORD.md:777, 796). It accepted a supervisor's wording on the ground that the `MemoryError` exclusion also defeats the non-zero-exit promise, withdrew two open questions, and told the verifier it need not adjudicate them (RECORD.md:762, 798). The verifier measured exit 1 (RECORD.md:798). Both errors were caught by the manager-side verifier's FAIL on T-7, not by the manager (RECORD.md:790, 796, 798). The record calls F-58 "the more serious of the two" because the manager used it to close its own open concern (RECORD.md:798).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond noting that each was checkable with one command: a `grep` for F-57 and one run for F-58 (RECORD.md:796, 798).
- **For v3.** Before relaying a subordinate's claim to the client or withdrawing an open question, the manager re-runs the command that settles it and cites the result (suggested here, not in the record).

### Re-dispatching a claimed task makes the manager write that task's file under a live claim, and check_scope decides liveness from the task title, not the board.
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** refuted or narrowed later — F-39's step-order explanation and its proposed fix were overturned by the correction at RECORD.md:626-627; the underlying plugin defect is open.
- **Evidence.** F-39 (RECORD.md:593-596); RECORD.md:607, 623-629, 674-675; commits `9749e60`, `42425a8`, `17e7811` (RECORD.md:674).

F-39 first blamed `run` §4: step 2 claims the board and step 3 commits task-file edits, so a re-claim after an amendment collides into a `misattributed-write` (RECORD.md:594). The manager predicted that committing the amendment first would avoid this; measured, it did not, and the rule became "the manager cannot write a task file while that task is claimed, full stop", four commits where the skill describes two (RECORD.md:623, 626-627). Its prediction of when the finding would clear was also wrong: check_scope keys on the task file's title (`RUNNING` fires; `DONE` and `NEEDS-DECISION` read clean), so "a supervisor can silence a scope finding by writing `DONE`" (RECORD.md:607, 624, 674). The hazard the check exists for did not happen here, because workers had been told not to touch the task file (RECORD.md:595). At the next claim the manager avoided part of the problem by writing only BOARD.md and leaving the title to the supervisor (RECORD.md:675).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Release the claim and commit; amend and commit; re-claim and commit; dispatch (RECORD.md:627); "the repair lives in `claude-skills`" (RECORD.md:629).
- **For v3.** `run` §4 needs a written re-claim procedure (RECORD.md:627), and check_scope should take liveness from the board's claim rather than from a title the supervisor edits (second half suggested here, not in the record).

### Values the manager typed by hand, and checks chained to commits, failed repeatedly until derivation or structure replaced memory.
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — from T-7 on, the `Since` column was derived from the claim label (RECORD.md:719); gating a write on a check stayed unsolved (RECORD.md:890).
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:553, 587, 676-677, 719 (typed values); RECORD.md:641, 861, 890 (checks and commits); RECORD.md:881, 911 (the record read and not followed).

A board edit sliced a fixed number of characters off a cell holding a multi-byte em dash, and it dropped a space that nothing would flag (RECORD.md:553). Three times the board's `Since` time was typed from the time the manager expected rather than from `date`, twice after written corrections (RECORD.md:587, 676, 719). The record concludes the column "should be derived from the label rather than typed beside it" (RECORD.md:677). Twice a check and the commit it gated ran in one shell command, so a red check printed and the commit landed anyway (RECORD.md:641, 861). Splitting them created a check-then-act race that let a write land in an open overlay, and the record concludes "the gate must be a property of the write itself" (RECORD.md:890). The manager also reproduced defects it had just read about: it read F-26 "four hours before violating it", and the value-cell error N-4 reached its fifth author (RECORD.md:881, 911).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Derive the column from the label (RECORD.md:677); run the checks, read them, and commit as a separate act (RECORD.md:641); draft outside the repository and land writes only in a tree no overlay is reading (RECORD.md:890).
- **For v3.** The claim step generates every board value that restates another, such as the time and the label, and a commit helper refuses to run on a red gate (suggested here, not in the record).

### Rotation handoffs lost the client's reply and a decision's expiry condition, and only the incoming manager's questions caught them.
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:841-842, 852 (client's reply); RECORD.md:854 (expiry); RECORD.md:851 (handoff sha); RECORD.md:856 (write order); C-5 lines 162-168; F-64 (RECORD.md:844).

The C-4 rotation announcement recorded no client reply, the same defect logged at C-3 and reproduced by the manager that had named it (RECORD.md:842, 852). The recorded cause is that "the announcement is written before the reply exists" (RECORD.md:842). The entry explaining why T-10 was left unclaimed gave the reason but not that the reason expired when the lock moved (RECORD.md:854). The incoming manager asked three questions the record could not answer, and C-5's grep for such entries counts 15 across four rotations (C-5 lines 162-168). The handoff sha stayed stable this time, and the rule was refined to "unstable while the predecessor still owes writes it has not made" (RECORD.md:851). Writing BOARD.md alone in one command and the manager file in the next worked, which confirms that the "BOARD.md is always writable" escape holds only for BOARD.md written alone, a rule "still unstated in the skills" (RECORD.md:856).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Leave the announcement entry open until the reply exists or a line records that none came (RECORD.md:852); `resume` §0 step 5 should ask for the sha only after the predecessor confirms it owes nothing (RECORD.md:851).
- **For v3.** The handoff-ready record gets fields for the client's last words and for each deferred decision's expiry condition (suggested here, not in the record), and the skills state the BOARD.md-alone write order (RECORD.md:856).

### Supervisors applied P-20c's evidence test correctly across many dispatches, but rework requested after acceptance has no category.
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** Q-9 / D-14 (RECORD.md:581-583); RECORD.md:655, 739, 754, 759-760.

A supervisor escalated a third attempt, and the manager answered it under P-20c's own text, "the third attempt belongs to whoever answers", recording the answer as D-14 rather than spending a client turn (RECORD.md:581-582). Supervisors then declined to count a killed dispatch, a contaminated promotion and a deliberately empty dispatch as attempts, deciding each time on evidence rather than on labels (RECORD.md:655, 739, 754). One README step still took four dispatches, and the record says that total "is a real cost on the client's meter", which the correctness of each individual decision should not hide (RECORD.md:759). The fourth dispatch was a framing fix the manager asked for after a verifier had accepted the work, neither a retry nor a planned step: "a shape the task file's step list has no room for" (RECORD.md:760).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The manager asked for the P-20c position to be stated in the closing report (RECORD.md:759); none is recorded for the missing category.
- **For v3.** The supervise skill names a post-acceptance rework step and says how it counts under P-20c (suggested here, not in the record).

### A supervisor parked on a headless worker has nothing to wake it, and the manager's polling-shell substitute was fragile.
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — the manager parked background waits and sent wake messages by hand (RECORD.md:591, 597, 601).
- **Evidence.** F-38 (RECORD.md:590-591, 597, 599, 601); RECORD.md:632, 636-637, 690-691, 726-727.

T-9's supervisor dispatched a worker and ended its turn, and because a headless worker is not a harness child, no notification fires when it finishes (RECORD.md:591). The recovery table's "not stale, leave it alone" row then tells a manager to do nothing (RECORD.md:591). The manager woke supervisors by parking background waits and then messaging them, "a manual act with no step that prescribes it", four times in one task (RECORD.md:597, 601, 632). One wait fired early because `pgrep -f` matched the manager's own shell (RECORD.md:636-637). Another read `meta/pid` once and was right only by luck, because a re-dispatch into the same sandbox rewrites that file (RECORD.md:726-727). The harness also killed the polling shell itself, and from three kills the record infers that the harness picks that kind of process (RECORD.md:690-691).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "either `sandbox.py dispatch` should write a real pid and the supervisor's parking message should name the sandbox id by convention, or the manager's §6 should carry a step for waking a parked supervisor" (RECORD.md:599); later, the dispatch writes the pid where recovery reads it and "the harness should notify on sandbox exit, so nobody needs a polling shell at all" (RECORD.md:691).
- **For v3.** A way to wake a parked supervisor that does not depend on a polling shell held by the manager (RECORD.md:691).

### The .sandbox liveness line never holds a pid, so the recovery route the resume skill documents does not exist.
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-66 (RECORD.md:872-876); RECORD.md:592, 598, 638, 643, 664; C-5 lines 275-281.

At dispatch, `sandbox.py` writes `pid -` as a literal in its format string, before any pid exists; nothing ever updates it, and no script reads a pid from that file (RECORD.md:873). At exit the line is rewritten to `exited 0 at …`, which makes it "a completion record wearing the name of a liveness record" (RECORD.md:598). The real pid is in `meta/pid` under the sandbox root, present while the worker runs and removed at a clean exit (RECORD.md:638, 643). `resume` §3 and its recovery table tell a recovering session to read the `.sandbox` pid, so a session following that prose "re-dispatches over a running worker" (RECORD.md:874). This never bit, because `sandbox.py status` reads `meta/pid` correctly and every recovery in the run found nothing live (RECORD.md:875). Three signals separate a completed worker from a killed one, and of those the record says "None of it is in the recovery table" (RECORD.md:664).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Copy the pid into the documented file, "a one-line copy rather than new plumbing" (RECORD.md:638); C-5 lists this among the `claude-skills` defects left for after the run (C-5 lines 275-281).
- **For v3.** sandbox.py writes the real pid and the resume prose names the file that holds it (RECORD.md:638, 874), with an attempt counter added because sandbox ids are reused across re-dispatches (RECORD.md:727; the counter is suggested here, not in the record).

### Work in a killed worker's overlay had no owner, and each rule meant to protect it had a hole found by losing something.
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — copy the overlay before reading it, later the whole sandbox root (RECORD.md:652, 665, 805); F-50 and F-52 are observations.
- **Evidence.** F-43 (RECORD.md:659-661), F-50 (RECORD.md:744-745), F-51 (RECORD.md:746-749), F-52 (RECORD.md:750-752), F-56 (RECORD.md:791-794); RECORD.md:650-658, 818.

S-9's worker was killed with 115 uncommitted lines in `upper/`; the manager read the file and judged it valuable but did not copy it, and the supervisor's plain `close` then removed it (RECORD.md:650-651, 658). The supervisor had checked only `meta/`, whose summary files a killed process never writes, and "Absence of a summary is not absence of work" (RECORD.md:660-661). The manager's resulting rule, "copy it before you read it", saved a complete README on its first real test (RECORD.md:652, 745). Later that day the same supervisor re-dispatched into a killed sandbox it believed "idle and untouched", which cost attribution rather than work, and the second worker checked the dispatch's `TREE: clean` against the tree and refused to work (RECORD.md:746-748, 751). The copy rule then missed DM-10's evidence, because the blind reader's log lived in the sandbox's `home/`, not in `upper/` (RECORD.md:792-793). The two copies the supervisor later called durable were a gitignored path and `/tmp` (RECORD.md:818).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Before closing a sandbox whose dispatch did not complete normally, inspect the overlay's working tree rather than `meta/`, a rule the record says "belongs in the skill" (RECORD.md:661); copy the whole sandbox root, not `upper/` (RECORD.md:793, 805).
- **For v3.** `sandbox.py close` refuses a plain close after an abnormal exit when the overlay is not empty, and snapshots the whole root first (suggested here, not in the record); the work skill asks workers to verify `TREE:`, which RECORD.md:751 says nothing asks for now.

### A host write while an overlay is open reads through into the worker's tree, and promote then refuses the step and blames the worker.
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — the manager held RECORD.md until each task closed and later drafted outside the repository, and the supervisor committed its title flip before opening any sandbox (RECORD.md:741-742, 887-888, 891).
- **Evidence.** F-49 (RECORD.md:733-743), F-61 (RECORD.md:816-817); RECORD.md:880-888, 891; Q-1 as cited at RECORD.md:735; F-5 and F-26 as cited at RECORD.md:880-883.

`promote --dry-run` refused a clean T-7 step on three findings, all about the manager's uncommitted `RECORD.md`, and this cost a whole dispatch (RECORD.md:734). It takes both halves: the sandbox's lower layer is the live host tree, and `promote`'s uncommitted-remainder check is whole-tree and unscoped, the defect Q-1 met with a stray `.pyc` (RECORD.md:735-736). The refusals stopped once the manager stopped writing, "confirmed by intervention rather than by inference" (RECORD.md:742). The unsafe window runs from `open` through `promote` and the manager cannot see its end, so the record was held back for whole tasks and lost its write-at-observation property for that time (RECORD.md:740-741, 743). F-61 is the same shape caused by the supervisor: the skill says to set the title to `RUNNING` without committing it and then open a sandbox, so every later sandbox inherits the edit and the worker is blamed (RECORD.md:817). At T-10 the manager contaminated two more sandboxes, the second after publishing a wrong "refinement" of F-26, and the measured rule is that the vulnerable window is "the whole time an overlay is OPEN" (RECORD.md:880, 884, 886-888).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Draft `devteam/` writes outside the repository and land them only when no overlay is open (F-26 as applied at RECORD.md:881, 887); commit the `RUNNING` title alone, host-side, before opening any sandbox (RECORD.md:891); the unscoped gate is "charter-deferred to `claude-skills`" (RECORD.md:736).
- **For v3.** Scope promote's uncommitted check to the step's declared paths (RECORD.md:735-736), or base overlays on a commit rather than the live tree (suggested here, not in the record).

### Two worker instructions could not be followed: a citation form the checker refuses, and a SANDBOX-ROOT that does not exist inside the sandbox.
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — the citations were elided and the rules were carried in T-10's brief (RECORD.md:781, 863).
- **Evidence.** F-54 (RECORD.md:780-781), F-55 (RECORD.md:782-784); RECORD.md:863-864, 876; C-5 lines 275-281.

`devteam:work` tells a worker to cite scripts by an absolute home path and calls that form leak-free, while `check_refs` flags every `/home/<user>/…` path, so four citations written the recommended way had to be elided from T-7's record (RECORD.md:780-781). `SANDBOX-ROOT` is a host path the worker cannot use, so two workers found the base commit through `git reflog`, and one said it "happened to work" (RECORD.md:783). A third worker found that the guard refuses a read-only `git stash list` just as it refuses a real `git stash` (RECORD.md:784). The record groups these with F-31, F-65 and F-66 as mechanisms whose documentation is "accurate about intent and wrong about the artifact" (RECORD.md:876). T-10's brief carried the rules, which the record calls "still a brief rather than a mechanism" (RECORD.md:863-864).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** After the run, put the rules in the dispatch template in `claude-skills` (RECORD.md:864).
- **For v3.** Make the work skill's citation guidance match `check_refs` (RECORD.md:781); give workers a route to the base commit from inside the sandbox, and let the guard allow read-only stash inspection (both suggested here, not in the record).

### The environment pin records the plugin commit and binds nothing, because the installed skill is a symlink into the plugin's live tree.
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — before each dispatch, a diff of the plugin's executable directories against the pin (RECORD.md:859-860).
- **Evidence.** F-65 (RECORD.md:848, 855, 859, 865-868); F-42 (RECORD.md:618, 866).

The board pins plugin commit `24533ca` while `claude-skills` was at `777bf03` (RECORD.md:855). `~/.claude/skills/devteam` resolves into that repository's working tree, and the guard hook runs `plugins/devteam/scripts/guard.py` by absolute path, so what runs is whatever is checked out at the time (RECORD.md:855). The run was unharmed only because the diff over `scripts skills agents hooks` was empty, which the record calls "luck rather than containment" (RECORD.md:855). A T-7 verifier had reported the drift a day earlier without being asked; the CLI half of its message became F-42 and the plugin half reached no file (RECORD.md:865-866). The client declined both re-cutting the pin and freezing `claude-skills` (RECORD.md:859). The stale pin was kept because "a re-cut would erase the only surviving evidence that the drift ever happened" (RECORD.md:868).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "either a checkout the run actually executes from or a pin row that says it is a record" (RECORD.md:855); for the rest of this run, `git diff --name-only <pin>..HEAD -- scripts skills agents hooks` had to be empty before each dispatch (RECORD.md:859).
- **For v3.** The run executes the plugin from a pinned checkout, or the pin row says that it only records (RECORD.md:855).

### Adversarial passes found defects verification could not see, but their published reproductions had to be run before relay.
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** observation — F-40 counts as refuted or narrowed later, because F-41 corrected its reasoning (RECORD.md:615).
- **Evidence.** F-40 (RECORD.md:604-606), F-41 (RECORD.md:612-616); RECORD.md:541-542, 574-577, 611, 630-633.

T-6's REFUTE, run by a dispatched verifier, found two individually legal prices whose ratio overflows to `+inf` while the tool exits 0, a defect that is "in neither operand" (RECORD.md:541-542). T-9's first adversarial pass found that a coin whose first price is `1e-305` is bricked; the permanence traced back to T-6's own fix, and "each was right about what it was asked" (RECORD.md:575, 577). T-9's S-7 found a real R-4 violation, but when the manager ran both of its published command lines, each exited 1 with a single stderr line (RECORD.md:605). The manager's verifier found the two live routes, a non-ASCII-digit `amount` reaching an unguarded `print` and a plain `| head -0` giving exit 120 (RECORD.md:613-614). It also showed that the manager's own explanation was right in conclusion but wrong in reason, because `http.client` encodes the request line as ASCII (RECORD.md:615). The same verifier showed that S-7's proof that "a third consecutive refusal is impossible" bounded only overflow refusals, not refusals in general (RECORD.md:611).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The next brief required "publish no reproduction you have not run" and stated the expected yield of an adversarial pass (two of three had found real defects) rather than only its purpose (RECORD.md:630-631); the next supervisor checked every mechanism on the host before writing its briefs (RECORD.md:633).
- **For v3.** Templates for adversarial steps carry "publish no reproduction you have not run" (RECORD.md:630).

### A requirement judged by a person was evidenced by agent readers, and two readers stumbled in different places.
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — a second blind observation was run, C-4 was run to file it, and the client's judgement was recorded as D-19 (RECORD.md:807, 821, 824); F-53 is an observation.
- **Evidence.** F-53 (RECORD.md:778-779), F-62 (RECORD.md:822-824); RECORD.md:720, 764-772, 776-777, 810-813, 821.

DM-10 is "checkable by a person, not by a script", so T-7's REFUTE sent a worker that had not read `cli.py` to follow the README from a clean environment (RECORD.md:720). It left two log lines, the project's only physical DM-10 evidence, and the manager read them before the report arrived (RECORD.md:764-769, 772). The first reader reported four guesses; the second, dispatched after that evidence was lost, reproduced none of them, and the record concludes that "the document's silence is real and its cost is reader-dependent" (RECORD.md:777, 811-813). A separate reader-reaction dispatch found that "0 minutes ago" makes a reader pause, which no test could find: "Every test asserts the age is correct; none asks whether it is sayable" (RECORD.md:779). F-62 records two gaps in R-10's acceptance: it says "a PERSON follows the README" and both observers were agents, and its clause filing the observation "at the closing checkpoint" had not run because no checkpoint had been filed (RECORD.md:823-824). The re-opened T-7 closed without a fresh manager-side verifier, because the manager re-ran R-9's one-command acceptance itself and said so (RECORD.md:810).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Run the checkpoint immediately (RECORD.md:824); state the possible readings of a second observation before it runs (RECORD.md:807).
- **For v3.** Acceptance judged by a person keeps its evidence as a committed artifact and says whether an agent reader counts (suggested here, not in the record; from RECORD.md:792, 823).

### The first audit, run at cycle close at the client's request, broke all four refute claims, and its cost was never measured.
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:900, 917, 921, 923-924; `pricelog-correctness-2026-09-13.md`, `pricelog-hygiene-2026-09-13.md`, `pricelog-safety-2026-09-13.md`, `pricelog-security-2026-09-13.md`; F-33 as cited at RECORD.md:545.

Hygiene replayed check_trace's logic and found F-68, which had hidden the manager's own omission in two charter versions (RECORD.md:923-924). The other three auditors reproduced product defects: one future-dated log line blocks every coin, found in none of the signed documents (safety finding 1); the log path follows symlinks and redirects are followed to any host (security F-1, F-2); and an encode call placed before `_refuse`'s guarded `try` is a fourth uncaught state with no prior citation in the history, found on the day R-4 was marked discharged (correctness finding 1; RECORD.md:900). The record counts "nine substantive defects … three of them reproduced live" without giving its rule (RECORD.md:921), while by the files' own labels there are 19 numbered items, 13 of them neither cosmetic nor informational (count found by this reader, not in the record).
