### check_report gave silent wrong answers on hand-written report fields three times, and never re-runs the checks a report cites.
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — briefs carried the annotation rule and the scoped invocation, and the F-47 citation was corrected (RECORD.md:569, 588, 698).
- **Evidence.** F-34 (RECORD.md:543-544), F-37 (RECORD.md:579-580), F-47 (RECORD.md:693-698); RECORD.md:620, 710, 801.

F-34: the `HEADER` regex allows nothing after the id, so an annotated re-dispatch header is skipped and `parse_report()` validates attempt 1's stale block "with no error or warning of any kind" (RECORD.md:544). F-37: on the same tree, `check_report . T-9` read clean while `check_report . T-9.S-3` failed, because the unscoped form matches the last well-formed report, and `run` §6 tells the manager to run the unscoped form (RECORD.md:580). A step whose report never landed as a block at all is "indistinguishable, to the mandated gate, from a step whose report is fine" (RECORD.md:620). F-47: a `commits:` line reworded after the commit failed to resolve, and the truncated message made the parser look at fault (RECORD.md:694). The record treats all three as one problem, `REPORT` and `commits:` fields typed by hand about commits made earlier (RECORD.md:695). Separately, the `checks:` figures in two closing reports matched no committed tree, and `check_report` "validates a report's form and never re-executes its check lines" (RECORD.md:710, 801).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The cheap fix is to annotate on the line before the header, and the better one is a parser that refuses a `REPORT`-leading line it cannot parse (RECORD.md:544); "The field wants generating, not typing", from `git log -1 --format=%s` at the moment of writing (RECORD.md:695).
- **For v3.** The parser fails loudly on unparseable or missing step blocks (RECORD.md:544, 620) and the commit fields are generated (RECORD.md:695); §6 runs the per-step form (suggested here, not in the record).

### check_trace's amendment check reads past the entry it is checking; the same line was found twice, and the second time it had hidden the manager's omission.
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open — the Version 12 backfill was checked by hand with `comm`, and the checker was not changed (RECORD.md:924).
- **Evidence.** F-36 (RECORD.md:563-565), F-68 (RECORD.md:922-925); RECORD.md:919; `pricelog-hygiene-2026-09-13.md` finding 1; C-5 lines 28-29.

F-36 (2026-09-11): the latest amendment's opener did not match `- **Re-affirmed.**`, and `last = tail[pick:]` runs to the end of the file, so the check scored Version 4's list as if it were Version 5's (RECORD.md:564). F-68 (2026-09-13) is the same line failing the other way: the collector re-fires on every older block and the oldest mention of each row survives, so a row the latest amendment omits reads as present (RECORD.md:923). That hid the manager's own gap, since Versions 10 and 11 re-affirmed only 15 of the 18 constraint rows (RECORD.md:924). C-5 and the D-21 entry both cited the check's silence as evidence, and the record notes that none of GATE 4's rules "defends against a check that answers confidently and wrongly" (RECORD.md:919, 925; C-5 lines 28-29). F-68 does not cite F-36, even though F-36 had already written the same repair, "bound `last` at the next amendment entry" (RECORD.md:565, 923; cross-reference found by this reader, not in the record). F-68's lead says the check "cannot fire on any charter with two or more amendments", which is broader than the audit's condition, "as long as a row was ever mentioned once in history", and F-36 is a case of it firing, for DM-12, a row no older amendment named (RECORD.md:564, 922; hygiene finding 1; this reading is by this reader, not in the record).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** End `last` at the next amendment heading, or refuse an amendment that has no `Re-affirmed.` line (RECORD.md:565; hygiene finding 1); F-36 adds that when the check scores the wrong entry, the fix it prints is the wrong fix (RECORD.md:565).
- **For v3.** Fix the slice and add a test with two or more amendments (suggested here, not in the record), since the hygiene audit calls this checker "shared plugin infrastructure destined for higher-stakes libraries" (hygiene finding 1).

### The permission paperwork was never completed or read: PERMISSIONS.md stayed a proposal and D-1's push count was never taken.
- **Area.** A7 guard & permissions
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** `pricelog-hygiene-2026-09-13.md` findings 3 and 5.

D-1 grants `git push` on the condition that "every push is a measurement … read at every checkpoint", yet none of C-1 to C-5 cites D-1 or holds a push count (hygiene finding 3). `git rev-list --left-right --count HEAD...origin/main` gave `298 0`, so none of the project's commits reached the remote from this tree (hygiene finding 3). The guard refused the auditor's own `git remote` live as an outward-facing command, which the auditor reads as confirmation that the guard works (hygiene finding 3). `PERMISSIONS.md` still reads PROPOSED with unfilled placeholders, and the settings file it promised after GATE 1 does not exist (hygiene finding 5).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "State plainly, once, whether any push occurred this cycle … and either retire the unused permission or record explicitly that 'never exercised' is itself the accepted outcome", and regenerate `PERMISSIONS.md` or mark it superseded (hygiene findings 3, 5).

### The README and the code it describes were owned by different tasks, and the README went stale when the signed text changed.
- **Area.** A8 planning & estimates
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-59 (RECORD.md:799-803); C-5 line 92; `pricelog-hygiene-2026-09-13.md` finding 4; `pricelog-safety-2026-09-13.md` finding 2.

With fd 2 closed at startup, the tool's refusal lands on standard output, so the README's "writes one line … to standard error" is false in that state (RECORD.md:800). The manager left the README alone because no reader following it reaches that state and T-10 owned the behaviour (RECORD.md:802). It recorded that "the sentence and the code it describes are owned by different tasks and cannot be changed in one commit by anybody", and added `README.md` to T-10's scope (RECORD.md:803). T-10's supervisor then chose not to edit the README (C-5 line 92). After D-20, two auditors independently found the README stale: it still names "the one deliberate exception" where the signed text now names two, and it never mentions the silent exit (hygiene finding 4; safety finding 2).

### Every task overran its estimate until the last, which came in at 1.16 times.

### Signed promises, and the gates restating them, kept covering less than the harm they exist to prevent.
- **Area.** A9 signed text
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — D-13 in charter v5, and the stderr re-dispatch under the existing signed text (RECORD.md:561, 670, 678).
- **Evidence.** F-35 (RECORD.md:555-558), F-44 (RECORD.md:668-671); Q-8 / D-13 (RECORD.md:561); RECORD.md:896; `pricelog-safety-2026-09-13.md` finding 1.

F-35: R-6 quantified over failures, but a fetched price of exactly `0` enters the log through the ordinary success path and blocks that coin for good (RECORD.md:557). The manager raised the question's class from REVERSIBLE to CHARTER on the principle "The class is decided by the question", not by the size of the remedy (RECORD.md:556). F-44: T-9's gate, written by the manager, restated DM-13's exit-code rule as "every write to standard output", which left stderr's identical exposure outside it (RECORD.md:669). The record states the pattern itself: Q-3, Q-8 and Q-11 were promises narrower than the harm, and F-44 is "a gate whose quantifier was narrower than the promise it implements" (RECORD.md:670). At T-10, a wrong citation written into the gate at planning time was passed on unchecked by four parties (RECORD.md:896). After the cycle closed, the safety audit found the shape once more: DM-12's letter holds while the harm it exists to prevent recurs through clock skew (safety finding 1).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "a brief states the rule's domain, never the instance that prompted it — and a gate is a brief with a longer life" (RECORD.md:671).
- **For v3.** Plan review checks that each gate covers at least as much as its requirement, and verifies the plan's own citations (suggested here, not in the record; from RECORD.md:671, 896).

### R-4 quantified over every failure but was verified against a list of cases, and one considered exception to it existed only in code comments.
- **Area.** A9 signed text
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — D-18 recorded the `MemoryError` exclusion and D-20 recorded a second (RECORD.md:714, 900).
- **Evidence.** F-48 (RECORD.md:703-710); RECORD.md:609, 613-614, 667, 700-706, 893-900; C-4 lines 35-41; C-5 lines 69-76, 172-173, 250-257.

C-4 names the defect: "a requirement quantifying over a domain was verified against an enumeration", and C-5 repeats it as "a rule verified against an enumeration" (C-4 lines 38-40; C-5 lines 172-173). In this slice R-4 was broken by route A, route B, the stderr mirror and F-48's residuals, and at T-10 by a new silent state that the fix itself created (RECORD.md:613-614, 667, 703-706, 895). F-48's third residual is a deliberate `MemoryError` exclusion, documented three times in `cli.py`, that appeared in no signed document while R-4 said "Every failure" (RECORD.md:704). T-9's closing report understated its own work by carrying forward a residual that was already closed, and it left out of its carried-forward set two residuals S-10 had disclosed, one of them in a narrower form (RECORD.md:702, 705-706). C-4 says each gap was "found by an adversarial pass and not by verification", and C-5 says "by an adversarial pass, never by verification" (C-4 lines 40-41; C-5 lines 73-74). Several were in fact found by task verifiers (route A's real mechanism and route B, F-48's residuals as a set, and T-10's new state, found by a verifier given a refute), so the line that held here is attack-framed checking against acceptance checking, not adversary against verifier (RECORD.md:609, 613-614, 700-706, 893-896; this reading is by this reader, not in the record).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "every failure mode reachable from the CLI appears in the guard's input set or carries a named exclusion" (C-5 lines 254-256); Q-13 kept R-4 open rather than discharge a Statement that was false for an existing state (RECORD.md:898).
- **For v3.** The onboard and plan skills flag acceptance criteria worded as a method ("one case per enumerated cause") rather than as a property (C-5 lines 250-253), and a check looks for code-level exclusions that no signed document names (the check is suggested here, not in the record).

### Part of R-4's promise could not be observed from outside the process where it fails, part could not be kept at all, and the charter said neither.
- **Area.** A9 signed text
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — D-20 named the case that cannot be kept (RECORD.md:900); F-45 is an observation.
- **Evidence.** F-45 (RECORD.md:681-684, 708), F-46 (RECORD.md:686-687); D-20 (RECORD.md:900).

With stderr broken from its first byte, no output can be observed from outside the process in either the fixed or the broken code, so "exactly one line" had to be tested as "exactly one write was attempted, with the right content, and nothing raised" (RECORD.md:682). The record calls this a new class of instrument problem, "a property that genuinely cannot be measured the way it is stated" (RECORD.md:683). A step verifier confirmed, by reading the tests and by mutation, that they used the observable form, and a later verifier used a third mechanism to confirm the reformulation was forced (RECORD.md:684, 708). F-46: when the channel that carries the one line is itself broken, the line cannot be delivered and only the exit code can be kept, so "half of a signed done-means is conditionally unkeepable and the charter does not say so" (RECORD.md:687). D-20 later wrote "standard error unreachable" into DM-4 and R-4 as an accepted exclusion (RECORD.md:900).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Restate the requirement in terms of something observable (RECORD.md:683), and name the residual before the work lands (RECORD.md:687).
- **For v3.** Onboarding asks, for each done-means, how its failure would be observed (suggested here, not in the record).

### The ceiling's manager half could not be attributed to the project, because session_cost is keyed to a session and one session outlived its role.
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run — D-21 replaced it with the sum of per-rotation deltas (RECORD.md:919).

C-5 summed the five manager meters to 517,598,620 tokens against a ceiling of 400,000,000, which is 129.4% (RECORD.md:905). Session `9053d333` rotated out on 2026-09-11 and then did planning work in another repository in the same session, so its 214.5M cannot be split (RECORD.md:907). Leaving it out entirely gives a floor of 303,077,532 (75.8%), and the record adds "Neither number is producible by the instrument the charter names", noting that four checkpoints had reported percentages against it (RECORD.md:907). The worker half re-measured to a floor of $15.7851 across the 8 surviving sandboxes, against a carried $64.97 that C-5 declined to adopt because it could not re-derive it (RECORD.md:908). D-21 set cycle 2 at 150,000,000 manager tokens and $40 for workers, and before it was signed the manager demonstrated a delta of 25,385,363 tokens over 111 requests (RECORD.md:919-920). The figure carried at the fourth rotation, about 290M or 72.5%, is not reconciled with C-5's re-measurement anywhere in this slice beyond F-67's explanation (RECORD.md:858, 905; this reading is by this reader, not in the record).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** D-21's per-rotation deltas (RECORD.md:919).
- **For v3.** The run skill reads the manager meter at lock-take and at handoff and records the difference, so attribution does not depend on what a session does later (suggested here, not in the record; from D-21 at RECORD.md:919-920).
