### The pinned claude CLI moved from 2.1.268 to 2.1.269 under a live claim, and the sandboxed workers ran on the unpinned version.
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform
- **Status at the stop.** open — still unresolved at C-5 (RECORD.md:892; C-5 lines 284-285).
- **Evidence.** F-42 (RECORD.md:618-619, 639); RECORD.md:857, 892.

A verifier disclosed, without being asked, that `claude --version` read 2.1.269 against the pin's 2.1.268 (RECORD.md:619). `run` §5 forbids re-pinning while a claim is in flight, so the drift was only recorded, and every result in that verification was "obtained under 2.1.269 and attributed to a pin naming 2.1.268" (RECORD.md:619). The sandbox's `bwrap` command line binds `versions/2.1.269` explicitly, so the workers ran on it as well (RECORD.md:639). At the fourth rotation the CLI row read 2.1.268 and matched the pin, during T-10 it read 2.1.269 again, and the record does not reconcile the two readings (RECORD.md:857, 892; that last point is by this reader, not in the record). The pin made the drift visible, which is its job under §5, but it could not prevent it (RECORD.md:619).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None, because §5 forbids re-pinning mid-claim (RECORD.md:619).
- **For v3.** The pin records which binary it read, and sandboxes bind the pinned version rather than whatever is installed (suggested here, not in the record).

### The harness's low-memory heuristic killed processes with over 130 GiB free and the kernel OOM counter unchanged, favouring the manager's polling shell.
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:648-649, 688, 690-691, 745.

S-9's worker and the manager's background wait were killed together, with the harness reporting "stopped because the system is running low on memory", while `/proc/vmstat` `oom_kill` stayed at its baseline of 4 with 134 GiB of 157 available (RECORD.md:648-649). The manager's wait on S-12 was killed while the worker survived, again with `oom_kill` at 4 and 136 GiB available (RECORD.md:688, 690). From those kills the record infers that the heuristic chooses among the session's own processes and prefers a long-lived polling shell, an inference anyone can check by repeating the counter reading (RECORD.md:690). T-7's S-2 worker and the manager's wait were then killed together, the fourth confirmation (RECORD.md:745).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Remove the need for a polling shell (RECORD.md:691).
- **For v3.** Nothing in the pipeline needs a long-lived polling shell (RECORD.md:691).

<Cycle 2 opens, 2026-09-13 to 2026-09-14 — RECORD.md lines 930–1190 — written up by a reader of `pricelog`'s record, every sentence cited to it.>

### Host writes to `devteam/` contaminate every live worker overlay, and no gate the manager can observe prevents it
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** F-71 worked around in the run (width dropped to 1, RECORD.md:965); F-72 and F-105 open
- **Evidence.** F-72 (RECORD.md:958–961), F-71 (RECORD.md:962–965), F-105 (RECORD.md:1172); price and mechanism RECORD.md:972–980; C-6 §4 counts "F-5 four more times".
At width 2, T-12's and T-13's overlays each picked up the other supervisor's task-file edit within four minutes, with no manager involved (RECORD.md:963). F-72: `sandbox.py status` flickers, the `.sandbox` lock line's `exited` marker reverses on a re-run, and the heartbeat never changes, so F-26's rule "hold writes until nothing is open" cannot be read (RECORD.md:959–960). The manager tried four cheaper gates and all four let writes in (RECORD.md:973, 975). The timing proves `changed-names.txt` is computed at close against the host tree, so a parked supervisor holds its window open indefinitely (RECORD.md:974). Taken to its end, the sound gate reduces to "write only when nothing is claimed", but `run` §4 step 5 requires record writes while tasks are claimed (RECORD.md:977–978). Measured cost: one discarded tester step at `$0.98`, plus four discarded sandboxes holding correct commits across T-10 and T-12 (RECORD.md:972, 980). F-105 adds that holding a promotion for a concurrent verifier is safe only in the order verifier, then promote, then land, and `supervise` states neither the hold nor the order (RECORD.md:1172).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Exclude `devteam/` from an overlay's `changed-names.txt` and `uncommitted.patch`; a real worker write to `devteam/` is already caught by `check_scope` (RECORD.md:979). Make `status` distinguish a kept-evidence overlay from one belonging to a live claim (RECORD.md:975). A read-only verification should never open an overlay (RECORD.md:982).
- **For v3.** Remove `devteam/` from overlay diffs in `sandbox.py` and label kept overlays in `status`.

### The supervise skill's own ordering and its report convention cause a `promote-uncommitted` refusal on every step
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (a dispatch instruction, RECORD.md:1089); the record later widened the scope to every step (RECORD.md:1103)
- **Evidence.** F-82 (RECORD.md:1055–1056), RECORD.md:1089, 1103, 1168; commit `92b9fc3`.
Step 2.3 sets the title to `RUNNING` before any sandbox opens and step 4.2 forbids committing that edit alone, so the first overlay inherits an uncommitted task-file edit (RECORD.md:1056). T-14's supervisor committed the title alone at `92b9fc3` and avoided the first case, but the refusal came back on S-1 because a worker leaving its report uncommitted, as the convention asks, produces the same refusal (RECORD.md:1089, 1103). In T-17 it appeared as a direct contradiction: `devteam:work` says append the REPORT to the task file while the dispatches forbid writes under `devteam/` (RECORD.md:1168). The recovery (`sandbox.py run <id> -- git checkout -- devteam/tasks/T-n.md`) is "a tax on every promotion" (RECORD.md:1103).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** F-71's fix, excluding `devteam/` from an overlay's diff, "is the only thing that removes it" (RECORD.md:1103).
- **For v3.** The same `sandbox.py` change as the entry above, plus making `supervise` and `work` agree on where a report goes.

### At width 2 a step's "whole suite stays green" gate is unsatisfiable while a sibling task holds committed red tests-first tests
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (it resolved by timing, RECORD.md:991, and width dropped to 1, RECORD.md:965)
- **Evidence.** F-74 (RECORD.md:990–991).
T-12's S-2 got `1 failed, 182 passed`, and the one failure was T-13's deliberate red test, outside T-12's scope (RECORD.md:991). A verifier arriving at a worse moment would have seen a non-zero exit for reasons unrelated to the task.
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A whole-suite gate at width > 1 must exclude another live task's declared scope, or tests-first and concurrency cannot both be project policy" (RECORD.md:991).
- **For v3.** Scope-aware whole-suite gates before width above 1 is advertised again.

### Dispatch templates state rules that roles cannot follow as written, so each supervisor or worker rediscovers the workaround
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** F-75 and F-83 open; F-89 worked around in the run (a supervisor instructed `$REPO`, RECORD.md:1109)
- **Evidence.** F-75 (RECORD.md:992–993), F-83 (RECORD.md:1057–1058, 1065), F-89 (RECORD.md:1108–1109).
F-75: the `~`-relative rule exists so that written reports carry no absolute home path, but two workers also applied it to the commands they ran and could not find `check_scope.py` inside the sandbox (RECORD.md:993). F-83: the step-dispatch template omits `CONTAINMENT`, `SANDBOX-ROOT`, `TREE` and `NOTES`, which `devteam:work` treats as BLOCKED when absent. All three T-11 workers went ahead on a stated cautious reading, and the verifier found they caused no defect, which the record reads as evidence that the rule is unfollowable (RECORD.md:1058, 1065). F-89: verifiers quote absolute paths by default, so landing a verdict verbatim (P-17) trips the leak checker; the first landed verdict added six such paths (RECORD.md:1109).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A rule that is violated by default must not stay a rule each supervisor has to think of — it belongs in the verifier dispatch template" (RECORD.md:1109); F-75: the template must distinguish what is written from what is run (RECORD.md:993).
- **For v3.** Put the four missing fields, the written-versus-run distinction and the `$REPO` instruction into the templates themselves.

### Workers hit `sandbox.py` interface limits the dispatch does not mention: the model name and nesting
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** F-73 open; F-80 observation
- **Evidence.** F-73 (RECORD.md:966–967), F-80 (RECORD.md:1033–1034).
The charter's band `sonnet-5 .. opus-5` is copied verbatim into dispatch templates, but `--model opus-5` fails with `unrecognized_model`; the real identifier is `claude-opus-5`. That costs one dispatch per supervisor (RECORD.md:967). F-80: a worker inside a structural sandbox cannot open another, and `sandbox_probe.py` correctly refuses. The worker built a `git clone` copy and chose it over `git archive` because the tests under examination need a real `.git` (RECORD.md:1034).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none recorded.
- **For v3.** Write real `--model` identifiers into templates and document a sanctioned nested-isolation route (suggested here, not in the record).

### The environment pin has never been read by any sandbox, and the CLI binding goes stale when the CLI updates between open and dispatch
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** F-81 open; F-96 worked around in the run (reopen, and open shortly before dispatch, RECORD.md:1141, 1174)
- **Evidence.** F-81 (RECORD.md:1052–1054, 1063–1064, 1090), F-96 (RECORD.md:1140–1141); CLI drift RECORD.md:1084, 1174.
`read_pin()` globs `devteam/.run/env/*/pin.txt`, but the project writes `containment` and `versions` and no `pin.txt` exists, so "no environment pin found — toolchain resolved from PATH" has fired on every sandbox since 2026-09-10 (RECORD.md:1053). The manager called this "benign by luck". The verifier showed the exposure is real: `/usr/bin/pytest` is 7.4.4 against a pin of 9.0.2 and `/usr/bin/ruff` is absent. The right versions win only because the venv's `bin` comes first on PATH. It labelled this a live reproduction plus inference, not a replay (RECORD.md:1063–1064). F-96: `toolchain_binds` resolves the CLI's versioned target at open but binds the named directory live, so an update at 18:15 made an 18:17 dispatch exit 127 with `claude: not found` (RECORD.md:1141). The CLI moved from 2.1.268 (pinned) to 2.1.270, 2.1.271 and 2.1.272 during the slice (RECORD.md:1084, 1141, 1174).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** F-96: "resolve the toolchain binds at each `run` and `dispatch` rather than at `open`", untested (RECORD.md:1141). F-81: none beyond the diagnosis.
- **For v3.** Make `read_pin` and the pin writer agree on one file, and fail loudly when no pin is found.

### A worker's meter and report do not survive its sandbox or its death, and `check_report` misreads what does survive
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-76 (RECORD.md:1008–1009), F-87 (RECORD.md:1104–1105), F-103 (RECORD.md:1169–1170); floor figures RECORD.md:1142, 1147, 1150, 1155; C-6 §3d and §4.
F-76: T-12's supervisor summed `14,036,156` tokens by reading each meter before close, and `check_report` flagged it against the surviving `5,892,610`. P-17c's "trust the harness's number" is wrong once a sandbox has closed (RECORD.md:1009). F-87: the dispatch extracts report, result and budget inside the process whose death is the case they need to survive, so a killed worker loses exactly those files, while the git-derived files can be recovered with `sandbox.py run` (RECORD.md:1105). F-103: `check_report` finds a step's meter through the task's single `.sandbox` file, which the next dispatch overwrites, and it compared S-2's block against S-4's meter (RECORD.md:1170). Worker spend is therefore always "a floor", for example `$8.9728` of `$40` (RECORD.md:1150).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none recorded beyond "recorded rather than corrected" (RECORD.md:1009).
- **For v3.** Persist each dispatch's meter and report outside the overlay and outside the dispatching process, and key them per step (suggested here, not in the record).

### Closing a task is split across separate commits, and checks read whichever state is current, so verifiers rule inconsistently
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (F-78, F-90, F-98, F-107); plugin unchanged
- **Evidence.** F-78 (RECORD.md:1022–1023, 1046), F-90 (RECORD.md:1115–1116, 1152), F-98 (RECORD.md:1153–1154, 1175), F-107 (RECORD.md:1181–1182, 1186); reopening analogue RECORD.md:1036; commit `c0d1142`.
F-78: `one-sided-link` is red between the supervisor setting the title to `DONE` and the manager moving the requirement. The verifier showed the same pattern for four tasks (RECORD.md:1023). F-90: P-18 bars moving the requirement before the task verifier passes, and T-15's verifier called the resulting red "non-reproducing" while T-14's verifier FAILed on the identical red: "a verification outcome that depends on which verifier happens to be dispatched is not a verification layer, it is a coin" (RECORD.md:1116). F-98: `check_scope` evaluates `misattributed-write` only while the title reads `RUNNING`, so a report's `checks:` line depends on whether it ran before or after the retitle (RECORD.md:1154). F-107: T-12 sat `DONE` on the board with its title still `NEEDS-DECISION` for three days, every check passed, and the record says no party owned the fix (RECORD.md:1182).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Close in one commit ("close the window", RECORD.md:1046); the supervisor's report should describe the pending window so the claim matches (RECORD.md:1116), and it held at T-14's second close (RECORD.md:1152); run `check_scope` both before and after the retitle, which held on T-17 (RECORD.md:1175).
- **For v3.** One close operation that moves the title, board row and requirement together, and a check comparing the board's state with the task title (suggested here, not in the record).

### Several checks read nothing and report clean: `board-drift` matches no rows, and untracked files and wrapped fields are invisible
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-70 (RECORD.md:942–943), RECORD.md:945, 951, 1012, 1013, 1081; C-7 §5.
`BOARD_ROW` expects a bare `T-n` in the first cell, but every row is a markdown link, so `board_states()` returns `{}`. The check "fires on nothing because it reads nothing" (RECORD.md:943). Because of this, stale `BLOCKED` states and a malformed table row went uncaught (RECORD.md:951, 1013). `check_refs` and `check_trace` enumerate files with `git ls-files`, so an untracked task file is invisible, and `FIELD` captures only a value's first physical line (RECORD.md:945). `check_refs` passed an untracked C-6 as clean and found the real defect only after `git add` (RECORD.md:1012). C-7 counts F-70, F-81, F-84 and F-85 as "four instruments that report on nothing" (RECORD.md:1081; C-7 §5).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none specific; filed as a claude-skills defect (RECORD.md:943).
- **For v3.** A check that parses zero rows, or skips untracked files, should fail rather than pass (suggested here, not in the record).

### `check_trace`'s exit code is permanently 1, so the manager committed over red checks; a finding-set baseline gate fixed it in the run
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (a manual baseline diff, RECORD.md:1102, 1112)
- **Evidence.** F-85 (RECORD.md:1075–1077), RECORD.md:1001–1003, 1112–1113, 1121, 1135, 1139; C-7 header.
Two accepted findings (T-1 `missing-field`, T-10 `one-sided-link`) pin the exit code at 1, so `check_trace; git commit` cannot be gated the way `check_refs` can (RECORD.md:1076). Separately, the manager ran `check_refs` with its output sent to `/dev/null` and committed in the same shell invocation, over a red result, a second time; the record calls this worse because the transcript no longer shows the defect (RECORD.md:1001–1002). The manual baseline gate later refused a commit correctly on a real transient state (F-78's window), and again on F-95's self-cycle. When the pattern was wrong, an empty set failed closed (RECORD.md:1112–1113, 1139). Once, the baseline was widened by one named finding with a stated end, and the finding went away earlier than that end (RECORD.md:1121, 1135).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** A recorded baseline of accepted findings, so the exit code means "the accepted set and nothing else" (RECORD.md:1077); a pre-commit hook running `check_refs`, so the gate sits "in the commit path rather than beside it" (RECORD.md:1003).
- **For v3.** Baseline support in `check_trace` and a pre-commit hook for the checks.

### Status vocabularies lack words for ordinary acts, and the report parser fails silently, so honest labels are pushed into prose
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-86 (RECORD.md:1093–1095), F-88 (RECORD.md:1106–1107); N-4 sixth and seventh instances (RECORD.md:938, 970–971); declaration form (RECORD.md:1014, 1059); commit `53326f6`.
F-86: `status: DONE -- RECONSTRUCTED` for a report rebuilt after a worker died was refused, and `53326f6` changed it to plain `DONE`, moving the disclosure into `notes:`. "A check that refuses an honest label and accepts a less informative one is selecting against disclosure" (RECORD.md:1095). F-88: an unindented parenthetical stops the parser, which then reports five or six unrelated `missing-field` findings and does not name the line that broke it (RECORD.md:1107). N-4 split into two causes: four instances where the vocabulary lacked a term (no word for an addition) and three where an author wrote a sentence where a token belongs (RECORD.md:971). The manager wrote the F-n declaration form wrongly six times, the sixth in the same commit that recorded the first five (RECORD.md:1014, 1059).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Either the checker should accept an identifier it can plainly see, or the skill should show the form rather than describe it" (RECORD.md:1014); fix the missing-term class and the sentence-for-token class separately (RECORD.md:971).
- **For v3.** Add `added` and `reconstructed` terms, make the parser name the line it could not read, and put copyable examples of each form in the skills.

### `check_trace` parses prose in `Depends on.` as graph edges, and every cycle-2 dependency was a file collision
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-95 (RECORD.md:1137–1138), RECORD.md:941, 1016, 1100; commit `08014e1`.
`IDS.findall` over the whole field turns any mentioned task id into an edge. The problem showed only because the stray id was T-17 itself, which produced a self-cycle. That an unrelated mention would silently block a task is read from the parser, not run (RECORD.md:1138). The template invites prose in exactly this field. No cycle-2 dependency is logical; "a dependency field that only ever encodes file overlap is a lock, not a plan" (RECORD.md:1016), and cycle 1 carried a stale dependency for four tasks because nothing checks the field (RECORD.md:941).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none recorded.
- **For v3.** Separate a machine-read dependency list from explanatory prose, and name scope-collision locks as locks (suggested here, not in the record).

### The manager loop has no self-continuation and stalled for four hours with nothing to notice it
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-84 (RECORD.md:1069–1074), RECORD.md:1080–1082; C-7 (DRIFTED) §5.
The last commit was at 11:00 and the client asked at 15:03. Nothing had run and nothing was hung: the manager had written "I'm picking it up now" and ended its turn (RECORD.md:1070). The risk sits between one task's close and the next claim, when nothing is in flight to wake the loop. Both of this session's stalls happened there (RECORD.md:1072). The charter has a `Keepalive tick` row that neither cycle ever used, and C-7 filed DRIFTED for that breach, the first checkpoint to judge the pipeline rather than the work (RECORD.md:1074, 1080; C-7 §5). The manager also checked liveness with `ps`, which cannot see in-process subagents; `ListAgents` was the right instrument (RECORD.md:1082).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A keepalive tick … or a background command whose completion re-invokes the manager whenever the board holds an unclaimed task with no live claim" (RECORD.md:1074).
- **For v3.** Make the keepalive the default in `run`, not an optional charter row.

### An escalation window expires only when the manager happens to look
- **Area.** A10 escalation & client
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:953–955; D-24, D-25, D-26; C-6 §3; C-7 §3.
P-27's unreviewed path fired for the first time (Q-15 to Q-17, expired 23:17) as the client's announced experiment (RECORD.md:953). Two of the three untested artifacts worked; the third, the window, "did not exist as a mechanism at all". Nothing computes an expiry, and the manager ran a date calculation by choice (RECORD.md:954). On the second firing, Q-21's window expired at 11:47 and the loop acted at 15:0x (C-7 §3). Two of the decisions had no implementation owner: D-24 waited on T-14's scope, and D-25's `pyproject.toml` was in no task's scope (RECORD.md:956; C-7 §3).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none separate from F-84's keepalive (RECORD.md:1074).
- **For v3.** Tie window expiry to the keepalive tick so it is computed rather than remembered (suggested here, not in the record).

### The harness stopped headless workers "because the system is running low on memory" with over 130Gi free, and the stop kills the worker
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-92 (RECORD.md:1125–1126), RECORD.md:1092, 1105, 1120, 1123–1124; commit `1c89448`.
T-14's S-2 and S-5 dispatches were stopped at 15:50:15 and 17:19:28, and both notifications read "was stopped because the system is running low on memory" (RECORD.md:1123). `free -h` showed 135Gi available of 157Gi, and `oom_kill` stayed at 4 (RECORD.md:1124). The notification was paraphrased before anyone else saw it: the verifier and the manager argued from the supervisor's paraphrase, the supervisor had argued from `kern.log` silence instead of the `oom_kill` counter the record already used, and T-14.md still says "host-level low-memory condition" (RECORD.md:1120, 1123). What the harness measures, and against what threshold, is not established (RECORD.md:1124). F-17 and F-92 are one mechanism: the tracking that wakes the supervisor is what exposes the worker to the stop (RECORD.md:1126).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A disposable watcher": detach the worker and have a harness-tracked poll on its liveness file wake the supervisor; "untested here" (RECORD.md:1126). The T-17 dispatch required quoting a harness stop verbatim (RECORD.md:1156).
- **For v3.** The watcher shape, plus a rule to quote harness notifications verbatim into reports.

### A supervisor passed a killed attempt's fix to a retry as "confirmed correct" after one test file, and a relayed cause travelled as fact
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-93 (RECORD.md:1129–1131), RECORD.md:1128, 1173; D-31 (RECORD.md:1122).
The supervisor ran only `tests/test_fetch.py` (22 passed) against the dead worker's diff, then ran plain `close`. `PROTOCOL.md`, `run` and `supervise` all say `close --keep`, so the patch and its meter were lost (RECORD.md:1128). The retry worker ran the whole suite anyway. It found both `serve()` helpers bypassed, with failures and a hang, and refused to commit red, widen scope or reintroduce `install_opener`; this cost `$2.1440` and 6,716,266 tokens (RECORD.md:1130). The manager's sweep found a third interception point that neither the worker nor the supervisor had named; this was found by reading, not by running (RECORD.md:1131). In T-17, a worker's guessed cause of a hang ("pytest's fd capture") was repeated in two briefs until a faulthandler dump showed the real cause (RECORD.md:1173).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A step that changes what a shared name resolves to must sweep every user of that name across the test tree" (RECORD.md:1130); "a relayed cause needs 'reproduced' or 'reasoned' beside it" (RECORD.md:1173).
- **For v3.** Put both rules into `supervise`, and require `close --keep` for a killed attempt.

### Supervisors tick a step before its verifier returns, and verdicts that are not landed read as steps nobody verified
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (T-17's dispatch, RECORD.md:1156; held, RECORD.md:1175)
- **Evidence.** F-97 (RECORD.md:1145–1146, 1149), RECORD.md:1096–1099, 1110; commits `ce21ac0`, `dc3c721`, `7b92526`, `f6aacb5`.
All five of T-14's steps and all four of T-11's were ticked when the report landed, with the verdict recorded 5 to 13 minutes later. Other steps ticked in the verdict commit, so "both orders are in use and nothing distinguishes them in the file" (RECORD.md:1146). F-30's land-the-report-first rule is how the tick gets there (RECORD.md:1146). In T-14, S-1's and S-2's verifiers passed but their verdicts were not written down, and the manager concluded from the file that a step with a dead worker had gone unverified. That was wrong, and it nearly landed as a false finding (RECORD.md:1096–1098).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "`check_report` flags a ticked step with no verdict block", untested (RECORD.md:1146); state "land it or it did not happen" as its own line in the skill (RECORD.md:1110).
- **For v3.** That `check_report` rule and the separate checklist line.

### The verify skill's fallback copy has no `.git`, so tests that run git fail there by construction
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (the brief changed to `git clone`, RECORD.md:1133)
- **Evidence.** F-94 (RECORD.md:1132–1133), RECORD.md:982; commit `7442808`.
`git archive HEAD | tar -x` is commented "the committed tree, exactly", but two R-16 tests fail in such a copy with exit 128 and pass in a `git clone` (219 passed, 1 xfailed). A retry worker blamed the wrong mechanism for those failures (RECORD.md:1133). The manager's own T-14 brief made the fallback the only route (RECORD.md:1133). Earlier, a verifier's no-sandbox `git archive` extraction had been praised as the pattern for avoiding F-5 (RECORD.md:982).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Use `git clone` rather than archive (RECORD.md:1133, 1156).
- **For v3.** Change the verify fallback to a clone.

### Nothing checks that every audit finding gets a disposition, and the audit's heading form collides with task files
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-99 (RECORD.md:1157–1158), F-104 (RECORD.md:1171); Q-22 (RECORD.md:1160, 1187).
The correctness audit's Finding 3 (`change._age()` shows "60 minutes" from 3570 to 3599 seconds) appeared in no decision, question, requirement or task. T-13, T-15 and T-17 each declined it as out of scope, and it became Q-22 only when T-17's supervisor escalated it as ownerless (RECORD.md:1158, 1160). F-104: the audit skill prescribes `## COR-n` headings, but an adversarial step's answer lands verbatim in a task file, where such a heading declares a finding id outside `audits/` (RECORD.md:1171).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The audit triage writes one disposition line per finding, and a check refuses a finding without one", untested (RECORD.md:1158).
- **For v3.** The disposition check, and a heading form that is safe to land outside `audits/`.

### Agent definitions defeat the qualified-name workaround for the cross-plugin skill collision
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-101 (RECORD.md:1164–1165), F-102 (RECORD.md:1166–1167), RECORD.md:944.
The planner confirmed that `devteam:plan` by qualified name resolves correctly while a bare `research` resolved to `npk`'s skill (RECORD.md:944). But `devteam:verifier` has no Skill tool, so every brief saying "invoke `devteam:verify`" asked for the impossible; the content arrives with the agent definition (RECORD.md:1165). The `devteam:auditor` definition lists unqualified `skills: [audit, research]`, which injected `npk:audit` and `npk:research`. "A dispatch can qualify a name; it cannot un-inject what the agent definition already loaded" (RECORD.md:1167).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none recorded beyond the qualified-name workaround (RECORD.md:944).
- **For v3.** Qualify every skill name in the `agents/` frontmatter and remove the "invoke" instruction from verifier briefs.

### A dispatch that forbids sandboxes and a skill that prescribes them do not say which wins, and roles resolve it differently
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-108 (RECORD.md:1184–1185), RECORD.md:1168.
T-17's task verifier, briefed to open no sandbox, ran six `sandbox.py exec` overlays because `verify` names that route first. T-17's contained workers, facing the reverse conflict, followed the dispatch (RECORD.md:1184, 1168). It did no harm only because the manager wrote nothing while the verifier ran: "circumstance rather than design" (RECORD.md:1185).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none recorded.
- **For v3.** A stated precedence rule between dispatch and skill (suggested here, not in the record).

### Verifiers caught product defects whose tests could not fail; the record's lesson is that a pin is trustworthy only once shown to flip
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** observation (all three fixed in pricelog: RECORD.md:1029, 1040, 1152)
- **Evidence.** F-77 (RECORD.md:1019–1021), F-79 (RECORD.md:1030–1032), F-91 (RECORD.md:1117–1119); RECORD.md:1027.
F-77: T-15's `strict=True` xfail could never flip, because `grep` given one file prints no filename. The verifier applied the documented fix, still got `xfailed`, then proved the cause (RECORD.md:1020). The manager had praised the pin in writing after reading the decorator (RECORD.md:1021). F-79: an xfail with no `raises=` absorbed a `CalledProcessError`; reproduced separately by two parties (RECORD.md:1031). F-91: `fetch.py`'s module-level `install_opener` silently removes redirect-following for every other `urlopen` caller once it is imported, which was proved by construction (RECORD.md:1118). The record ties F-77 and F-79 to P-35 for the sixth time (RECORD.md:1032).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** A refute that names the specific thing to attack (RECORD.md:1027); test both directions rather than reading the decorator (RECORD.md:1021).
- **For v3.** Verifier briefs require a demonstrated flip for every pin or trip-wire.

### The manager's own bookkeeping: a stale invariant copied forward, a dispatch with no model, and a draft landed twice
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (corrected by the supervisor at `45cf603`, RECORD.md:1162)
- **Evidence.** F-100 (RECORD.md:1161–1163), RECORD.md:997.
The manager copied `main`'s boundary block into T-17's dispatch as 244 lines at `8d3d5d86…`, stale since T-11.S-3; the supervisor re-derived 274 lines at `0536112c…` before dispatching (RECORD.md:1162). The manager dispatched T-17 with no `model`, so it ran on Opus 5 while the board said Sonnet 5 for four hours (RECORD.md:1163). The held P-27 batch was landed twice, because the draft was never consumed. `duplicate-id` caught the three decisions, but the prose duplicated silently (RECORD.md:997).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Copy an invariant forward "as the command that produces it" rather than as a number (RECORD.md:1162); make a draft's landing idempotent or consume the draft (RECORD.md:997).
- **For v3.** A draft-and-land helper that consumes the draft, and a `model` field required at dispatch (suggested here, not in the record).

### A weekly-quota stop leaves the record describing live work that is not running
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-106 (RECORD.md:1176–1179), RECORD.md:1085.
The limit stopped every session on 2026-09-14 at about 23:35. T-17 read as claimed with its task verifier in flight for two and a half days, although that verifier had died with "You've hit your weekly limit" (RECORD.md:1176, 1179). `resume`'s closest row, "never verified", is right about the state and silent about the cause. It was cheap only because the killed agent was a read-only verifier (RECORD.md:1179). The session id survived both the restart and the resume, so the writer lock held (RECORD.md:1085, 1176).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none recorded.
- **For v3.** A `resume` row for a quota stop, and a way to record it (suggested here, not in the record).

### The write guard matches command text, not targets, and refuses clean-up of self-created `/tmp` paths
- **Area.** A7 guard & permissions
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:968, 994, 1035 (no F-n declared).
A `git checkout` in a fresh scratch clone under `/tmp` was refused (RECORD.md:968). `rm -rf` and `chmod` on self-created `/tmp` paths containing a task-ID-shaped substring were refused three times (RECORD.md:994), and a second worker could not clean up at all, so the guard "is now measurably producing litter" (RECORD.md:1035). The refusal message's own workaround worked in the first case (RECORD.md:968).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** none; "recorded rather than raised" (RECORD.md:968).
- **For v3.** Match the target path against scopes and structural containment instead of the command text (suggested here, not in the record).

### Estimates were not checked against their own step lists, and overruns stayed large
- **Area.** A8 planning & estimates
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:946, 1015; C-6 §4; C-7 §4 (no F-n declared).
T-12 and T-16 each had four steps but were estimated for three, understating by 783,200 tokens apiece. Only an arithmetic check caught it, because `check_trace` reads `Estimate.` and does not count `S-` lines (RECORD.md:946). T-12 metered `14,036,156` against `3,282,800` estimated, 4.3× (C-6 §4). T-11 came in at about 1.03× on dispatched agents alone (C-7 §4). T-17 was quoted at about 2 step-units and planned at 3, with the increase stated (RECORD.md:1015).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "a fourth thing a check could catch and does not" (RECORD.md:946).
- **For v3.** `check_trace` compares the estimate formula's step count with the task's `S-` lines.

### Manager per-request cost more than doubled across the session, and off-meter agents spent 2.7 times the manager half
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1142–1144, 1159, 1188–1189; C-6 §3d; C-7 §3d (no F-n declared).
The manager half read 37.5% of 250,000,000 at C-6 and 61% at C-7 (C-6 §3d; C-7 §3d), then 80.0% at 18:25 and 87.2% at 20:30 on 2026-09-14 (RECORD.md:1142, 1159). A request grew from about 202,000 tokens over the first 138 to about 470,000 (RECORD.md:1142, 1159). Summed from in-process agent transcripts, 40 Sonnet supervisors and verifiers used 546,345,463 tokens over 2,171 requests, 2.7 times the manager half and counted in neither half of the ceiling; this is a floor (RECORD.md:1143). C-8 filed DRIFTED partly because C-5 to C-7 passed without the rotation `run` §7b requires; the delta at handoff was 231,151,044 (RECORD.md:1188–1189).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Rotate at T-14's close, keeping the ceiling (RECORD.md:1144); "a rotation does not reset the sum" but lowers per-request cost (RECORD.md:1159).
- **For v3.** Enforce §7b rotation mechanically and meter Agent-tool subagents against a ceiling (suggested here, not in the record).

<Cycle 2, 2026-09-17 to 2026-09-18 — RECORD.md lines 1191–1634 — written up by a reader of `pricelog`'s record, every sentence cited to it.>

### check_report's model-mismatch fires on a correct report whenever the last worker's model differs from the supervisor's
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-109 (RECORD.md:1236, 1237); recurrences at RECORD.md:1305, 1563, 1593; "the other end" at RECORD.md:1460; workaround in NOTES R7 (RECORD.md:1277), C9 (RECORD.md:1422, 1532)
`check_report` finds the harness meter through the task's single `.sandbox` file (F-103), so it compares the supervisor's `model:` line with the last step worker's model, and `run` §6 prescribes a re-dispatch for that finding (RECORD.md:1236). On T-18 the supervisor's transcript held 187 model fields, all `claude-sonnet-5`, while `T-18.sandbox` named S-3's Opus 5 implementer; "following §6 literally would have discarded a correct report and re-run three verified steps" (RECORD.md:1237). It recurred on T-18's task verification (RECORD.md:1305) and on T-16, where the task verifier confirmed it as a false positive from 300 model fields, all `claude-sonnet-5` (RECORD.md:1563, 1593). The reverse also happens: once a supervisor closes a step's sandbox plain, `.sandbox` names a root that no longer exists and the budget comparison goes silent for every step (RECORD.md:1460).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None for the script. The run's workaround: every supervisor dispatch says the `model:` line names the supervisor's own model and `notes:` names each step worker's model (RECORD.md:1277, 1422, 1532), and the manager judged each instance "not a re-dispatch" (RECORD.md:1563).
- **For v3.** `check_report` should compare each report block against that block's own run, not against one `.sandbox` pointer per task (suggested here, not in the record).

### The ceiling never counts a successor's resume, and eight resumes totalled 25,648,745 uncounted tokens
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-110 (RECORD.md:1249, 1250); re-measured at RECORD.md:1261, 1334, 1380, 1485, 1582, 1624; C-9 §3d
D-21's per-rotation delta runs from a manager's lock-take reading to its second reading, so the successor's whole resume before its lock-take falls in no rotation's delta (RECORD.md:1249). First measured at 1,988,326 and 2,947,944 tokens, "about 2–3M tokens a rotation … invisible to the ceiling — F-33's shape, for the one role the ceiling does measure", so "a rotation looks cheaper to the governor than to the client's weekly gauge" (RECORD.md:1250). By the twelfth lock move the eight lock-take readings summed to 25,648,745 (RECORD.md:1624). C-9 §3d found the first four (9,240,078) larger than two of the four rotation deltas. The tenth lock move said the trend "follows the record's own length" (RECORD.md:1485); the eleventh called that reasoned, not measured, and said what a successor chooses to read moves it as much (RECORD.md:1582).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded beyond measuring it at each lock-take.
- **For v3.** Count a rotation as its resume plus its tenure, so the ceiling sums what the client's gauge sees (suggested here, not in the record).

### A rotation's delta closes before its last work, and the uncounted tail reached 22.7% of a rotation
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-116 (RECORD.md:1365, 1366); remedy at RECORD.md:1472; measured at RECORD.md:1486, 1491, 1583, 1625; notice timing at RECORD.md:1565, 1591
D-21 takes the second reading when `handoff-ready` is written, so the manager's later writes fall outside every ceiling; the first measurement was 1,750,800 tokens, 13% of the rotation, spent landing Q-34 and charter Version 20 (RECORD.md:1365). The record calls it "F-110's shape from the other end" (RECORD.md:1365). Later tails: 2,704,848 (16%, RECORD.md:1491), 2,762,988 (12.5%, RECORD.md:1583) and 3,001,726 (22.7%, "the largest share yet, because this was the shortest of the four tenures", RECORD.md:1625). The idle notice the successor subscribes to arrives only between the successor's own turns, so it "cannot gate a reading taken mid-turn" (RECORD.md:1565, 1591).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The successor takes the predecessor's final reading (RECORD.md:1472), refined to "after the predecessor's last turn rather than at the lock-take", with `ListAgents`' idle state saying when that is (RECORD.md:1491); applied at RECORD.md:1583 and 1625.
- **For v3.** `resume` would take and record the predecessor's closing reading as a step, by the rule at RECORD.md:1491 (placing it in `resume` is suggested here, not in the record).

### Free-text step names from sandbox.py open are refused by promote's numeric subject gate
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-111 (RECORD.md:1306); RECORD.md:1325 item (3), 1330; NOTES C11 (RECORD.md:1424, 1534); RECORD.md:1564
`sandbox.py open --step` accepts free text, but `promote`'s subject gate accepts only `T-n:` or `T-n.S-<digits>:`, so a supervisor that named a step `S-4-pre` had its worker's correct commit refused at promotion: one wasted dispatch, `$2.0671` (RECORD.md:1306). `check_scope`'s attribution regex has the same numeric-only grammar (RECORD.md:1306). The refused overlay, `T-18-S-4-pre-094004`, was the only unpromoted overlay in the project; its content was redone as `baeabb2`, with identical token multisets (RECORD.md:1330). Under C11, T-16's retry opened as `--step S-1-retry` but committed under `T-16.S-1:`, and promote passed (RECORD.md:1564).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The dispatch rule: "Name every step S-<digits>, or commit it under a bare `T-18:` subject" (RECORD.md:1424), carried into T-16's brief (RECORD.md:1534).
- **For v3.** `sandbox.py open --step` should accept only the grammar `promote` and `check_scope` accept (suggested here, not in the record).

### F-104's verbatim-audit rule and check_refs' declaration grammar conflict
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (F-112's first remedy, an "accepted red set" of seven, was withdrawn minutes later at RECORD.md:1315)
- **Evidence.** F-112 (RECORD.md:1307); correction at RECORD.md:1315; `audits/pricelog-T-18-S-4-2026-09-17.md`
F-104 says an audit is landed verbatim in a task file with no `## COR-n` headings, while `check_refs` declares a `COR-n` only by a heading under `devteam/audits/`, so T-18's landed audit produced `cited-undefined` findings that neither rule allows fixing (RECORD.md:1307). The manager first recorded seven of them as an accepted red set (RECORD.md:1307), then withdrew it: its own filing cited all ten `COR` ids, so `check_refs` reported ten, plus a `bad-status` on Q-31, "both the manager's", and the command meant to stop at them did not stop because `head -1` exits 0 — "F-24's trap, walked into one entry after writing it down" (RECORD.md:1315). The fix was a declarations file: one heading and one disposition per `COR`, pointing at the verbatim evidence in `T-18.md`, which leaves F-104 intact for task files (RECORD.md:1315; the audit file's opening paragraph).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The declarations file under `devteam/audits/` (RECORD.md:1315).
- **For v3.** Make the audit-landing rule and `check_refs`' declaration grammar agree, for example a declarations file the `audit` skill writes as standard (suggested here, not in the record).

### The report a manager receives, the report in the task file, and the check_report line inside it can all disagree
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** open (F-113's commit-before-reporting rule was worked around in the run as NOTES C12; F-127 was then measured on a dispatch that carried C12, and its remedy is untested)
- **Evidence.** F-113 (RECORD.md:1308); RECORD.md:1303, 1453; F-127 (RECORD.md:1596); `verify T-16 FAIL` (RECORD.md:1593); C12 (RECORD.md:1425, 1535)
Both of T-18's supervisors retitled the task as their last action; the first committed its report and the second did not, and "nothing compares the report a manager receives with the report in the file" (RECORD.md:1308). Meanwhile `check_report` silently read the last worker's block and reported a `status-mismatch` about the wrong report until the manager landed the closing report itself at `ab29fc7` (RECORD.md:1303). When the next report was committed, the received copy and the filed copy still differed in citation form and in condensed `checks:` lines; nothing of substance differed "as far as it was read" (RECORD.md:1453). F-127: a supervisor runs `check_report` and then commits the block that cites it, so the committed line describes the previous block; T-16's task verification FAILed on exactly that line (RECORD.md:1593, 1596).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Commit the closing report before reporting up (C12, RECORD.md:1425, 1535). For F-127, untested: "a report cites `check_report` only as a post-commit check against its own block, or `check_report` skips the line that cites it" (RECORD.md:1596).
- **For v3.** The manager's intake compares the received report with the committed block mechanically, and `check_report` handles self-citation (suggested here, not in the record).

### A verifier brief that states the expected result turns an observed mismatch into a reported match
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-115 (RECORD.md:1338); RECORD.md:1305; C-9 §3c item 3; applied at RECORD.md:1454
T-18's task verifier was told `check_report` gives "an advisory `unknown-commit`"; it ran the check, observed `[exit 1]`, and wrote "matches exactly what the prompt said this gives" (RECORD.md:1338, citing `tasks/T-18.md:1561`). Its raw observation was right and its reading inherited the brief's error (RECORD.md:1338). C-9 calls this "the cheapest way to turn an independent check into a confirmation" (C-9 §3c item 3). The next task verifier was given two premises "with no expected result stated (F-115)" and tested both with self-tested AST comparisons (RECORD.md:1454).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "name the command and what to report, never what it will say" (RECORD.md:1338).
- **For v3.** The verifier dispatch template forbids expected results in `checks:` handed to a verifier (suggested here, not in the record).

### A pre-commit gate cannot see a check that reads committed history
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-114 (RECORD.md:1340); RECORD.md:1339, 1341, 1359, 1360; C-9 §1
`check_trace`'s `re-litigated-requirement` counts a requirement's changes over committed `REQUIREMENTS.md` blobs (`check_trace.py:243-253`), so the gate run on the working tree before `e569eff` passed, and the commit itself turned the check red (RECORD.md:1339). "A pre-commit gate needs a post-commit re-run", and the project's standing instruction to gate before every commit does not say so (RECORD.md:1340). The third finding stood from `e569eff` to `a216140`, when a committed `Shape reviewed.` line reset the counter (RECORD.md:1360); getting there needed a client question (RECORD.md:1341), so it was not a manager repair.
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Re-run history-derived checks after the commit; applied at RECORD.md:1359–1360.
- **For v3.** The check skill's gate order includes a post-commit re-run of history-derived checks (suggested here, not in the record).

### Check exit codes were read through pipes or never tested, and the board once advanced on a report run §6 sends back
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-117 (RECORD.md:1368); RECORD.md:1315, 1336, 1337; C-9 §4 and §5 item 4; C7 (RECORD.md:1420, 1530); RECORD.md:1373, 1601
The T-18 second-stop entry said `check_report … --blocking-only` exits 0, so no re-dispatch; replayed at every relevant commit it exits 1, because `$?` was `tail`'s (RECORD.md:1336). With that exit code gone, `run` §6's "a malformed report is a re-dispatch" applied, and "the board moved on a report §6 would have sent back" (RECORD.md:1337). No answer rested on that report: the task verifier had reproduced the escalation mechanisms from scratch (RECORD.md:1337). The record counts F-24 "three times in one day": the predecessor's `check_refs` at the same stop (the `head -1` case, RECORD.md:1315), this `check_report` reading, and the incoming manager's own first check run (RECORD.md:1336). F-117 is the other side: `check_refs` reported an absolute-path leak and the commit in the same shell command ran anyway, because the check's result was printed rather than tested (RECORD.md:1368).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The remedy is structural, not attentiveness: the commit belongs in a command that runs only if the checks exit 0" (RECORD.md:1368), carried as C7 (RECORD.md:1420, 1530). From the ninth lock move every check was run with output to a file and the exit code read from the check itself (RECORD.md:1373), and at RECORD.md:1601 "the gate held".
- **For v3.** Ship a wrapper or hook that runs the checks and commits only on exit 0, so reading an exit code is never a manual step (suggested here, not in the record).

### resume asks for the session join at every rotation although a session can read its own name
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-118 (RECORD.md:1378); RECORD.md:1377, 1483, 1580, 1622
`resume` §0 assumes "a session cannot read its own name", but on this CLI `ListAgents` opens with "This session is `claude-skills-devTeam_s19` [fc0c5f]", and the `/rename` notice names the session inside its own context (RECORD.md:1378). Every `resumed` line since the fifth lock move has carried both name and id (RECORD.md:1378). Three later rotations record that the remedy "is still a plugin change not made, so the question stays mandatory" (RECORD.md:1483, 1580, 1622).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: `handoff-ready` carries a third line, `name <its name>`, so the join is written rather than asked, "a plugin change between iterations, not this project's" (RECORD.md:1378).
- **For v3.** Add the `name` line to `handoff-ready` and let `resume` §0 read it (RECORD.md:1378).

### Briefs and reasoning kept in a transcript, a scratch directory or a diff needed a live predecessor to recover
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-119 (RECORD.md:1383); RECORD.md:1227, 1263, 1382, 1585, 1599, 1631; applied at RECORD.md:1267, 1391, 1489 item (iv), 1500, 1558
T-18's first dispatch NOTES existed only in the predecessor's transcript; with the predecessor gone they would have been rebuilt from finding numbers, and half the notes carry none (RECORD.md:1263). The C-9 entry then landed the next brief as a diff ("start from T18-refute-0928's" plus five items), and four inherited lines were false as written, so it needed the same rescue (RECORD.md:1382, 1383). The same shape appeared as a planner's pre-correction `T-18.md` in a scratchpad (RECORD.md:1227), T-16's drafted step dispatches in the supervisor's host scratch "with no path" (RECORD.md:1585), and two pieces of reasoning about D-53's task "in no file" (RECORD.md:1631). Once briefs were landed whole, a successor could answer "which lines carry" by naming lines (RECORD.md:1489 item (iv)). The T-16 brief was also generated from the committed record with its md5 recorded, and `$REPO` was expanded only in the field values, because expanding it everywhere would have told each verifier to write the absolute path its own instruction forbids (RECORD.md:1558).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Land a dispatch's NOTES whole" (RECORD.md:1383). Drafts were copied to the git-ignored `devteam/.run/scratch/T-16/`, which outlives both sessions' scratch (RECORD.md:1599).
- **For v3.** `run` lands every dispatch brief whole in the record before sending, and sends it generated from that text (RECORD.md:1383, 1558).

### A diff-grep for 'assert' certified an unchanged condition it could not see
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-120 (RECORD.md:1455); RECORD.md:1454, 1456
S-6's step verifier certified "no assert condition touched" with `grep '^[+-].*assert '` over the diff, but all five of S-6's edits were on continuation lines, so the certificate "could not have come out any other way — F-24's family" (RECORD.md:1455). The claim was true; the task verifier showed it with AST comparisons, self-tested by planting a defect first (RECORD.md:1454). The same closing dispatch's supervisor reduced C4 to an argument that holds only on those two premises, so D-46's falsifier did not fire "on evidence one layer above where it was asked for" (RECORD.md:1456).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A diff-grep for a statement's keyword is not a check that the statement is unchanged" (RECORD.md:1455); no rule was carried into later briefs in this slice.
- **For v3.** The `verify` skill asks for a structural comparison, such as an AST, when a claim is about unchanged code, and for a planted defect first (suggested here, not in the record; the practice is at RECORD.md:1454).

### The charter's model band was passed as a model id, and sandbox.py reports the refusal as an ordinary exit 1
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-121 (RECORD.md:1457); RECORD.md:1479, 1489 item (iii); C15 (RECORD.md:1538)
T-18's closing supervisor passed `MODEL-BAND`'s `sonnet-5` to `sandbox.py run --model`; the CLI refused it as `unrecognized_model` in 689 ms at `$0`, where every earlier dispatch had passed `claude-sonnet-5` (RECORD.md:1457). `sandbox.py` requires `--model` and does not check it, so the refusal surfaces as a worker's `exit 1`, outside the standing rule for F-96's `exit 127` (RECORD.md:1457). The record rules out the CLI update as the cause: "nothing shows 2.1.274 accepting `sonnet-5` either" (RECORD.md:1457). The dead overlay held nothing when read directly: zero commits, a zero-byte patch, `$0` (RECORD.md:1479).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: "`sandbox.py` checks the model name before it opens the overlay" (RECORD.md:1457). In the run, C15 tells supervisors to pass the CLI id, never the band's shorthand (RECORD.md:1538).
- **For v3.** `sandbox.py` validates `--model` before opening an overlay (RECORD.md:1457), and the dispatch template gives CLI ids rather than a band (suggested here, not in the record).

### A worker cannot amend its own unpromoted commit, so a missing trailer became permanent
- **Area.** A7 guard & permissions
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-122 (RECORD.md:1458); RECORD.md:1489 item (ii); C4 (RECORD.md:1527)
S-5's worker left out both trailers, and the guard refused its `git commit --amend` and `git reset --soft HEAD^` inside the sandbox while T-18 was claimed, because it does not tell a sandbox's own commits above its base apart from shared history (RECORD.md:1458). So `55c0eb7` carries no trailer, "disclosed rather than rewritten", the project's second trailer gap after D-6's (RECORD.md:1458). T-16's D-6 draft then had to extend its evidence to cover that commit (RECORD.md:1489 item (ii), 1527).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: "commits above `meta/base.sha` are private until promotion, and the guard could let their author amend them" (RECORD.md:1458).
- **For v3.** The guard lets a sandbox's author rewrite commits above `meta/base.sha` before promotion (RECORD.md:1458).

### A manager commit under a task's subject prefix becomes that task's permanent undeclared write
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-123 (RECORD.md:1459); RECORD.md:1309; C-9 §2
`92dc849`, subject `T-18: gate point 8 …`, landed a gate amendment and three record lines in one commit, so `check_scope . T-18` carries a permanent `undeclared-write` on `devteam/RECORD.md` that no retitle clears, while whole-project `check_scope .` stays clean (RECORD.md:1459). A related attribution finding: `d70c4d6`, an `answers:` commit touching `tasks/T-18.md`, raised `misattributed-write`, which is "visible only while the title reads `RUNNING`" (RECORD.md:1309; C-9 §2 records it as "recorded, not decided").
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Practice: "a manager commit under a `T-n:` subject touches that task's file and nothing else, and record and board lines go in their own commit" (RECORD.md:1459).
- **For v3.** Write that commit rule into `run`, or have `check_scope` attribute by author session rather than subject prefix (the second option is suggested here, not in the record).

### sandbox.py status cannot say why a sandbox is kept, or that it did nothing
- **Area.** A4 worker & sandbox
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-124 (RECORD.md:1480); RECORD.md:1194, 1195, 1256, 1330, 1479, 1578
`status` prints `work in upper/` the same way for promoted work, a superseded attempt, F-5 contamination and deliberate evidence, which is exactly the distinction `resume` §3 asks a successor to draw; sorting out six sandboxes took `promoted.json`, two task files and two record entries (RECORD.md:1194, 1195). F-124 is sharper: `held` is true for any file under `upper/` (`sandbox.py:1327`), and the harness itself writes `.git/config` before every run and `.git/index` after it (`sandbox.py:615`, `:732`), so a refused dispatch with zero commits and a zero-byte patch is labelled like real work (RECORD.md:1480). Two more traps: the obvious promotion check, `merge-base --is-ancestor`, reports promoted overlays as unreachable because promotion re-applies commits onto a moved base, and `git patch-id --stable` is the check that answers the question (RECORD.md:1256).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: "`close --keep` takes a reason, and `status` prints it" (RECORD.md:1195); "`status` reads the extract's own accounting, `meta/commits.count` and `meta/uncommitted.patch`, instead of walking `upper/`" (RECORD.md:1480). In the run, successors read each overlay's own accounting and patch-ids (RECORD.md:1330, 1479, 1578).
- **For v3.** Both `status` changes (RECORD.md:1195, 1480), plus a patch-id promotion check inside `status` (suggested here, not in the record).

### A declared scope can hand a worker any devteam/ file, and nothing mechanical refuses it
- **Area.** A7 guard & permissions
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-125 (RECORD.md:1490); RECORD.md:1489 item (i), 1497; C2 (RECORD.md:1525)
P-13 lets supervisors and workers write the product tree and their own task file's execution record "and nowhere else under `devteam/`" (RECORD.md:1490). Inside a sandbox the guard judges every write as the dispatching manager's own session (`guard.py:728-756`), promote's scope gate checks only that each path is declared (`sandbox.py:1641-1645`), and `check_trace` and `check_scope` take a declared scope as valid (RECORD.md:1490). T-16 passed GATE 2 with `devteam/PERMISSIONS.md` in its Scope, two paragraphs after its own prose argued P-13 for the other two `devteam/` files (RECORD.md:1489 item (i), 1490). It was caught by the predecessor's reading of T-16, relayed at the handoff, not by a check (RECORD.md:1489 item (i)).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: "the plan gate and promote's scope gate both refuse a `devteam/` path other than `devteam/tasks/<task>.md`" (RECORD.md:1490). In the run: D-50 dropped the file from T-16's Scope and made S-2 a draft for the manager to land (RECORD.md:1497, 1525).
- **For v3.** That refusal in both gates (RECORD.md:1490).

### A negative phrase check written at planning passed with the false text still present
- **Area.** A8 planning & estimates
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-126 (RECORD.md:1498); RECORD.md:1497; C4 (RECORD.md:1527)
T-16's `! grep -qF "R-11's own Statement warns" pricelog/store.py` exited 0 at HEAD with the false text still there, because the docstring breaks the phrase after "R-11's own" and `grep` reads lines (RECORD.md:1498). It was written at planning on 2026-09-13 and never run until the re-read before T-16's first dispatch (RECORD.md:1498). The record ties it to F-120: "a grep that cannot see a statement split across lines", this time in a task's own Verify (RECORD.md:1498). It was found by running each negative check against the unfixed tree first, "since a check that already passes proves nothing when it passes again" (RECORD.md:1498).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Applied: phrase checks collapse whitespace first, and each composite check exits 1 on the unfixed tree (RECORD.md:1498), made a dispatch rule in C4 (RECORD.md:1527). Untested, for the protocol: "the plan gate runs every `! grep` in a Verify against the base tree and refuses one that already passes" (RECORD.md:1498).
- **For v3.** That plan-gate check (RECORD.md:1498).

### R-6's acceptance test used a mock that cannot produce a short write's side effects, and it testified for two cycles
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** open (the product defect became D-53's task, T-19; the protocol remedy is untested)
- **Evidence.** F-128 (RECORD.md:1597); RECORD.md:1562, 1566, 1594, 1595, 1603; Q-37 → D-53
`test_short_write_raises_rather_than_reporting_success` replaces `os.write` with a mock that "write[s] nothing at all" and then asserts the log byte-identical; the mock answers whether `append()` checks the count, and "the assertion answers a question the mock cannot" (RECORD.md:1597). `tests/test_failures.py:708` also cites "a real short write" as reproduced in `tests/test_store.py`, which has no short-write test (RECORD.md:1597). It surfaced when T-16's README wording failed step verification (RECORD.md:1562); the manager flagged it as possibly package behaviour, marked "reasoned, not exhaustively searched" (RECORD.md:1566), and the task verifier reproduced it on tmpfs and ext4: a valid record missing only its newline takes the next run's record at exit 0, so "DM-5, R-6 and R-2 are false at HEAD" (RECORD.md:1594, 1595). The record calls it "`run` §8's stale test that testifies, in a discharged requirement's own acceptance file, for two cycles" (RECORD.md:1597).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: "the plan gate asks of every acceptance case that injects a failure whether the injection produces the failure's real side effects, and a mock that does less than the failure it models is named as such in the discharge" (RECORD.md:1597).
- **For v3.** That plan-gate question (RECORD.md:1597).

### An off-cadence rotation parked the loop for 4 hours 13 minutes beside an idle successor
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-129 (RECORD.md:1627); RECORD.md:1626
Between the outgoing manager's last act at 07:29:57 and the successor's resume at 11:43:35, nothing was in flight for 4 hours 13 minutes, against about 38 and 10 minutes at the two previous handoffs, and the successor session was open and idle throughout (RECORD.md:1626). D-47's condition 1 requires nothing in flight, so no agent is running to wake anyone, and `run` §7b step 3 addresses the announcement to the client, whose `/devteam:resume` is the only wake (RECORD.md:1627). The cost is wall-clock only, so no ceiling, delta or D-47 falsifier sees it; "D-47's 'What would make this wrong' is written in tokens" (RECORD.md:1627).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: when `handoff-ready` names a successor the client has already opened, the outgoing manager sends it the resume instruction directly and the announcement becomes a notice, "at the price of the one act that has so far been each succession's only confirmation" (RECORD.md:1627).
- **For v3.** That direct handoff, with the succession confirmed some other way (RECORD.md:1627).

### A stopped claim has no row in the recovery table, and nothing counts its in-flight row against width
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (a manager decision at RECORD.md:1629; carrying it out falls after this slice)
- **Evidence.** F-130 (RECORD.md:1630); RECORD.md:1265, 1389, 1629
Twice a restart found T-18's title reading `NEEDS-DECISION`, "a state §3's recovery table has no row for", and had to be governed by §9a and the decisions' own text instead (RECORD.md:1265, 1389). When D-53's task was about to be claimed beside T-16's stopped row, the predecessor had kept that row "stopped" on T-18's precedent and "never checked it against width" (RECORD.md:1629). `check_scope` reads liveness from the title line (`RUNNING`), which is right for scope overlap and wrong for width, so a second claim beside a stopped row passes every check at width 1; the width rule exists only as `run` §4 prose, and no script reads the in-flight rows against the header's width (RECORD.md:1630).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** For this case: the new claim's commit releases T-16's row, and T-16's heartbeat is rewritten, never deleted, to name the release and D-52 (RECORD.md:1629). Untested, for the protocol: "`check_scope` counts the board's in-flight rows against the header's width and reports a width finding, and a stopped task's row either leaves the table at its stop or is counted by a written rule" (RECORD.md:1630).
- **For v3.** That width check, and a `NEEDS-DECISION` row in the recovery table (RECORD.md:1265, 1630).

### Off-cadence rotations ran on a manager's private trigger until the client wrote it down as D-47
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1216, 1251, 1324, 1357, 1470, 1473; C-9 §4; Q-33 → D-47
Three rotations running were off-cadence, each justified by rising cost per request, nothing in flight, and a reason about the next act (RECORD.md:1216, 1251, 1324). `run` §7b's `handoff-ready` pointer "has no field for an off-cadence rotation" (RECORD.md:1216); after D-47 its second line read `checkpoint none -- off-cadence under D-47`, and the record notes that the guard reads only the `session` line (RECORD.md:1473). C-9 found that all four rotations since C-8 happened before any compaction, which the skill warns "is usually spending a rotation for nothing", while the doubling cost is "real and measured", and "no manager should keep deciding it privately" (C-9 §4). The client chose "Write the trigger down" (RECORD.md:1357); the first rotation under it followed at RECORD.md:1470. One order had a stated cost: sending the checkpoint to a fresh successor meant two resumes instead of one (RECORD.md:1324).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** D-47: rotate after every checkpoint, and off-cadence when all three conditions hold, with all three readings recorded (RECORD.md:1357).
- **For v3.** Put a written off-cadence trigger into `run` §7b, and give `handoff-ready` a field for it (suggested here, not in the record; the need is stated at RECORD.md:1216).

### D-47's condition 2 reads 'roughly doubled' within a few dozen requests of any heavy resume
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1492, 1569, 1611
Condition 2 compares a tenure's cost per request with the stretch that loaded it, and a resume is where the context gets built, so "with resumes this heavy it reads 'roughly doubled' within a few dozen requests of every lock-take" (RECORD.md:1492). Measured: 1.63× before anything had been dispatched, not acted on (RECORD.md:1492); 2.03×, acted on (RECORD.md:1569); and 1.84× over the tenure against 2.21× over its last 15 requests, where "the last stretch decided it" (RECORD.md:1611). The record wrote both numbers down "for the next checkpoint to read" rather than have a manager decide it privately (RECORD.md:1492, 1611).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond recording the readings for the next checkpoint (RECORD.md:1492, 1611).
- **For v3.** Define the condition-2 baseline so that a heavy resume cannot trigger it by itself (suggested here, not in the record).

### Anything the client says after an outgoing manager's last write has no file to land in, and prose succession questions drew no reply
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (the succession is put as a selection; the missing file is still open)
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1199, 1200, 1202, 1226, 1262, 1335, 1344, 1381, 1388, 1488, 1494, 1584, 1605, 1628
From the sixth lock move to the twelfth, the incoming manager had to ask what the client had said since the predecessor's last write (RECORD.md:1226, 1262, 1335, 1381, 1488, 1584, 1628). At the fifth lock move the record had not said that the ceiling half of a question went unanswered, or that C-8 reached the client as a terminal summary rather than as the file (RECORD.md:1199, 1200). The sixth lock move called this "structural": an outgoing manager writes nothing after its last intended write, so "only a live predecessor can carry it" (RECORD.md:1226). The eighth lock move measured a different cause for the unconfirmed successions: prose in a report drew no reply three times running, while "every question put by selection in this project has been answered within minutes", and "the record should stop calling his silence structural when the channel is what differs" (RECORD.md:1344). That confirmation was itself a selection, and the ninth to eleventh successions were confirmed by selection within minutes (RECORD.md:1344, 1388, 1494, 1605); the twelfth's confirmation falls after this slice (RECORD.md:1628). A client decision given on the outgoing terminal was relayed to the successor and put back to the client to confirm (RECORD.md:1202).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Put the succession as a selection rather than a line in a report (RECORD.md:1344).
- **For v3.** `resume` puts the succession as a selection, and a place outside the writer lock where client words heard during a handoff can be recorded (suggested here, not in the record).

### The successor had to ask for the predecessor's last write until the rotation entry named its own commit
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1260, 1333, 1379, 1484, 1487, 1572, 1581, 1623
The tenth lock move had to ask whether `d64bec9` was the predecessor's last intended write, because that entry listed what came after its reading and not whether any of it wrote (RECORD.md:1487). Once the rotation entry named its own commit as the last intended write (RECORD.md:1572), the next successor gated its lock commit without asking, "the first rotation where step 5's question was answered by the record" (RECORD.md:1581), and so did the one after (RECORD.md:1623). Lock commits were made by commands written to abort unless HEAD was the named last write and the tree was clean, and from the tenth lock move also unless `BOARD.md` was the only change (RECORD.md:1379, 1484, 1581, 1623). One lock commit was made as `git add` then a bare `git commit`, the form `run` §4.5 forbids; it had no effect and was not repeated (RECORD.md:1260, 1333).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "the rotation entry names its own commit as the last intended write, so a successor can gate its lock commit without a question" (RECORD.md:1487).
- **For v3.** That rule in `run` §7b, and a lock-take script that aborts on those conditions (the script is suggested here, not in the record).

### promote.lock keeps naming the last promotion's dead process

### Instructions not to leak absolute paths kept failing, and check_refs caught every recorded leak
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1238, 1368, 1369, 1558, 1601; C-9 §4

In T-16's verification the leaked path was the verifier's own scratch, because the brief's rule named the instance (`$REPO`) rather than the domain: "F-89's family, and this manager's" (RECORD.md:1601). Q-32 → D-46 restated DM-4 and R-4 as one rule with two conditions, with every listed state kept as a test: "the first time this project has restated a requirement rather than extended it" (RECORD.md:1356).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Restate as a rule with its cases as tests (C-9 recommendation 1; RECORD.md:1356).
- **For v3.** `onboard` and `plan` flag a requirement that lists cases under a goal that quantifies, before it is signed (suggested here, not in the record).

### A planner-drafted charter amendment carried four claims that did not survive checking, and two unsourced figures
- **Area.** A9 signed text
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1209
A `devteam:planner` on Sonnet 5 drafted Version 15 (421,999 tokens in-process, in neither half of the ceiling, F-33), and "four of its claims did not survive checking against the question texts and the tree" (RECORD.md:1209). Among them: form (c) was worded "open" though one member's stream is closed; T-18's gate claimed D-35 was kept "by construction" when D-35's falsifier is exactly a state those tests leave green (RECORD.md:1209). Unsourced figures ("R-4 broken six times", where Q-14 counted five) were cut rather than carried; the anchors it did verify held when the manager re-ran them (RECORD.md:1209).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None as a rule; the manager checked every claim before landing (RECORD.md:1209).

### The charter's size-triggered checkpoint never said which meter, and run §7 states a different threshold
- **Area.** A12 iterate & cycles

### Leaving the keepalive tick off after C-7 was nobody's decision

- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1201, 1208, 1357
C-7 named the tick as F-84's fix, but it never ran: "neither the manager nor the client decided it, and nobody proposed starting it" (RECORD.md:1201). The predecessor's substitute was a rule in its own memory files, never end a turn on an announcement, "which is not in this project's record, binds no successor, and could not have acted on the usage-limit stop (F-106)" (RECORD.md:1201). ### The manager ceiling was raised five times in two days, and the run adopted a rule to ask before a claim rather than spend through it
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1215, 1242, 1264, 1311, 1313, 1319, 1363, 1471, 1493, 1495, 1570, 1602, 1604; D-37, D-44, D-48, D-51, D-54
The ceiling rose through D-37, D-44, D-48, D-51 and D-54 (RECORD.md:1215, 1319, 1363, 1495, 1604). D-37 was asked with 9.4M left because recent requests averaged about 256,000 tokens (RECORD.md:1215), and its own falsifier ("a re-dispatch of T-18") fired (RECORD.md:1242, 1311). The seventh lock move found that its predecessor had weighed asking and left that unwritten, and adopted a written rule: measure cost per request, then put the ceiling to the client before claiming T-16 if the remaining work plus about 3M for one incident exceeds the headroom (RECORD.md:1264); it fired at RECORD.md:1313. D-51 priced a whole tenure at 13M–20M, and 19.4M was spent reaching T-16's first stop (RECORD.md:1570). Q-37 and Q-38 were put as one selection because each ceiling depended on the other answer, making the answers "consistent by construction" (RECORD.md:1602, 1604).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The rule at RECORD.md:1264; ceilings sized "above the failure path" (RECORD.md:1363); re-derive rather than carry forward (RECORD.md:1493).
- **For v3.** Put the ask-before-claim rule and the one-selection-for-linked-answers practice in `run` (suggested here, not in the record).

### T-18 ran about 4.6 times its token estimate, and the overrun was in what the adversarial passes found
- **Area.** A8 planning & estimates
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1212, 1214, 1235, 1463; C-9 §4
Q-23's option was quoted at about 2.2 step-units and T-18 was planned at 4; the client chose 4 over dropping the adversarial pass (3) (RECORD.md:1212, 1214). The first run's three steps' workers and verifiers metered about 15.0M tokens against 3,282,800 estimated for four steps, about 4.6 times; S-1's tester self-reported 700,000 tokens against a metered 7,761,296 (P-17c), while S-3's Opus implementer came within 7% (RECORD.md:1235). T-18's lifetime workers cost `$21.476559` (RECORD.md:1463). At C-9, still unclosed, T-18 had run two supervisors, seven worker dispatches, one auditor, five verifiers, seven client questions and three charter versions, and C-9 says "the overrun is not in the code it wrote — it is in what each adversarial pass found" (C-9 §4).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded.

### The environment pin's claude row stayed stale for five readings because run §1.5 forbids re-pinning while a claim is in flight
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1196, 1323, 1376, 1385, 1466, 1482; C-8 §4; C-9 §4 and recommendation 6
The pin read CLI 2.1.268 while the machine read 2.1.274 and then 2.1.276 (RECORD.md:1196, 1376). `run` §1.5 forbids a re-pin while a claim is in flight, and T-18's claim stayed in place through both of its stops, so the repair waited for the window between T-18's close and T-16's claim (RECORD.md:1323, 1385). Re-pinned as `20260918-0417`, with F-65's plugin re-check in the same command (RECORD.md:1466). "The new pin goes stale the next time the CLI moves, and nothing here can stop that" (RECORD.md:1466).

### Checks read the index, and every R-4 amendment forced another after-close edit to T-10's gate
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1213, 1245, 1359
`check_refs` could not see an untracked `tasks/T-18.md`, because the checks read the index, so a new task file must be staged before they can see it (RECORD.md:1213). `check_trace`'s `gate-omits-decision` fired each time R-4 cited a new decision, and each time it was fixed by editing closed T-10's gate under a sentence marking it amended after close, the fifth such marking at RECORD.md:1359 (also RECORD.md:1213, 1245).
