<Cycle 2, T-19 to the stop, 2026-09-18 to 2026-09-19 — RECORD.md lines 1635–2145 — written up by a reader of `pricelog`'s record, every sentence cited to it.>

### T-19 grew from three planned steps to six and stopped three times, and each task verifier refuted the supervisor's recommended resolution
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** Plan and quote: RECORD.md:1637, 1646, 1718; T-19.md:203–222 (Q-37, D-53, `cfee116`). Stops: RECORD.md:1748, 1911, 2077 (`3347800`, `2ab92b2`, `98b468f`). Task verifications: RECORD.md:1784–1786, 1951–1953, 2117–2118 (`T19-verify-1447`, `T19-verify-0015`, `T19-verify-1145`). Batches: RECORD.md:1789, 1957, 2121 (Q-40, Q-42, Q-45). Selections: RECORD.md:1795, 1963, 2127 (D-57, D-60, D-64). The manager's P-20c decisions: RECORD.md:1956, 2120 (D-59, D-63). The stop: RECORD.md:2140 (D-67).

T-19 was planned as three steps (red tests first, using real short writes; then the separator and the fragment; then S-3, the adversarial pass) at 3 step-units and 2,499,600 worker tokens by the plan's model. That figure was Q-37's quote: workers `$6`–`$16`, manager 7M–11M (RECORD.md:1637, 1646; T-19.md:203–206). When the client stopped the run it had six steps. S-1 was accepted and S-2 was never ticked. S-4 and S-5 each failed two attempts under P-20c. S-6 was decided (D-63, D-64) but never planned. S-3 ran once, in the second dispatch, after its first dispatch there died to a server-side API timeout (RECORD.md:1748, 1824, 1911, 1994, 2077, 2120, 2127, 2140). Getting there took three supervisor dispatches, seven metered worker attempts, eight in-process step verifiers and three in-process task verifiers (RECORD.md:1683, 1835, 2000, 1750, 1914, 2080, 1784, 1951, 2117).

Each break was first found below the task verifier: by S-2's step verifier, by S-3, and by S-5's second step verifier (RECORD.md:1748, 1828, 1911, 2077). What the task verifiers added was a refutation of every resolution the supervisors recommended, plus 18 false sentences and one incomplete one that nobody had named (count computed here from RECORD.md:1785, 1953, 2118). Each time, the manager put to the client the option the verifier's evidence supported rather than the supervisor's, and the client selected it (RECORD.md:1789, 1795, 1957, 1963, 2121, 2127). Two things limit how much of this can be credited to the layer itself. At stops 2 and 3 the manager doubted the recommendation before dispatch, from the code or from research, and aimed the verifier's R2 and R3 at it (RECORD.md:1912, 2085). And every supervisor ran on Sonnet 5 while every task verifier ran on Opus 5 (RECORD.md:1683, 1752, 1835, 1919, 2000, 2086). So the record cannot separate the layer's effect from the brief's aim or from the model (reasoned here, not in the record).

- **Stop 1:** `T19-append-1225`, NEEDS-DECISION at `3347800` (RECORD.md:1748).
  - Workers spent `$14.798342` against Q-37's `$6`–`$16` for the whole task, with S-3 and the landing not yet run (RECORD.md:1718, 1750).
  - The supervisor recommended widening the disclosure to cover the part left when the operating system refuses the removal, redrafted by property (RECORD.md:1748).
  - `T19-verify-1447` failed the report on the report's own `check_report` line (F-133). It refuted R2 by finding states that no signed text describes, most of them created by T-19's own lock and removal: an unbounded lock wait; a write to an orphaned file that returns success; and (E), the removal cutting a record written by a writer outside the lock, which is D-53's own falsifier. It also found that the drafted disclosure covered none of states B to G, and that six more sentences in scope were false (RECORD.md:1784–1785).
  - The client selected D-57: stop removing, and fix the lock (RECORD.md:1795).
- **Stop 2:** `T19-disclose-1934`, NEEDS-DECISION at `2ab92b2` (RECORD.md:1911).
  - Workers spent `$19.668271` against Q-40's `$10`–`$20` for S-4 and S-3. S-3 ran in-process, off the meter (RECORD.md:1871, 1914).
  - The supervisor recommended checking `pricelog/` one level up, against the anchor directory's descriptor (RECORD.md:1912).
  - `T19-verify-0015` measured that close refusing the four `pricelog/` cases and none of the four data-directory ones. It showed a CLI run exiting 0 with a percentage computed against an orphaned record. It measured that T-19's bounded wait widened an older microsecond window to up to five seconds, and it named eight false sentences nobody had named (RECORD.md:1952–1953).
  - The client selected D-60: re-resolve the path from its anchor after taking the lock (RECORD.md:1963).
- **Stop 3:** `T19-reresolve-0905`, NEEDS-DECISION at `98b468f` (RECORD.md:2077).
  - Workers spent `$23.7007645` against D-60's `$10`–`$24` for S-5 and S-3 with one retry. S-3 was not run (RECORD.md:2038, 2080).
  - The supervisor recommended an absolute working-directory anchor, "unbuilt and untested, as it says itself" (RECORD.md:2077).
  - `T19-verify-1145` found that this close refuses honest runs the tool serves today, with `latest()` answering "no previous record" for a log that has records. It found that a relative `HOME` also reaches the class, a source the report's framing missed. It named four more false sentences, one incomplete sentence and two false drafts (RECORD.md:2118).
  - The client selected D-64: refuse any relative data directory (RECORD.md:2127).

Across the three dispatches, workers metered `$58.1673775` and 68,548,278 tokens, about 27 times the plan's 2,499,600 (computed here from RECORD.md:1683, 1750, 1914, 2080). The record's own ratios were 6.3 times the original figure after S-2, and 8.1 times the six-unit figure after S-4 (T-19.md:211–212, 216, 219–220). Each dispatch's spend fell inside its own quote, at 92%, 98% and 99% of the top of the range, yet none reached the close its quote had priced (computed here from the same lines). Between T-19's plan and the stop, the manager ceiling rose four times, from 360,000,000 to 520,000,000, and the workers' ceiling rose three times, from `$80` to `$135` (RECORD.md:1646, 1650, 1801, 1971, 2128). The four manager tenures that held T-19 had deltas of 17,338,420, 21,164,427, 45,918,234 and at least 31,608,818 tokens. Together that is 116,029,899, and s22's figure includes its own lock move. Q-37 had quoted 7M–11M for the manager's whole share (computed here from RECORD.md:1646, 1669, 1905, 2003, 2144).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None as a protocol change. The record's practice was to put each supervisor recommendation to the task verifier as REFUTE lines rather than decide it (RECORD.md:1912, 2085), and to carry each FAIL verbatim into the restart brief (RECORD.md:1786, 1836, 2001).
- **For v3.** Keep `run` §6's fresh task verifier on every NEEDS-DECISION report, with REFUTE lines aimed at the supervisor's recommended resolution. Give the plan skill's model a way to price a task whose gate can stop it more than once (suggested here, not in the record).

### A new task file is invisible to all three plan checks until it is staged
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (the file was staged by hand and the checks re-run; plugin unchanged)
- **Evidence.** F-131 (RECORD.md:1642); RECORD.md:1641.

`check_trace`, `check_scope` and `check_refs` read tracked task files only. With T-19.md untracked, the first run counted 18 tasks and reported T-16's dependency on T-19 as a reference no task declares; the checks read correctly only after `git add` (RECORD.md:1641, 1642). The miss was loud only because two other files already cited T-19. A task that nothing cites yet "is not checked at all, and the three checks report clean", which the record says is reasoned from the measured 18-versus-19 count and was not built (RECORD.md:1642). The `plan` skill runs these checks "before you show anyone" and fills the board's row after they pass (RECORD.md:1642).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Either the checks read `devteam/tasks/*.md` from the working tree as well as the index and report an untracked task file as a finding of its own, or the `plan` skill stages each task file before its checks. Untested (RECORD.md:1642).
- **For v3.** The first of the two remedies, because it turns the miss into a finding rather than a step someone must remember (suggested here, not in the record).

### `check_trace` reads a `Discharges.` field two ways, and the measurement behind D-55 ran on a stub
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (T-19.md's field split at `4fb7680`; plugin unchanged)
- **Evidence.** F-132 (RECORD.md:1675); D-55 (RECORD.md:1639); RECORD.md:1679, 1683; commits `cfee116`, `4fb7680`.

`check_trace` keeps only a field's first line. The `unmotivated-task` check then reads that line by regex, but the `one-sided-link` and owner checks cut it at commas and compare each piece whole (`check_trace.py:530`, `:618`, `:757`, `:768`–`:774`, `:778`, as RECORD.md:1675 cites them). T-19.md's first line carried prose after "R-6, R-2", so the cut yielded `R-2 — **completed here` and never `R-2`. Measured against the file itself, every state from the claim on reported that T-19 does not list R-2: three findings in the claim, running and close states, and six between the DONE retitle and the close (RECORD.md:1675). D-55's measurement had used a stub carrying a bare `R-6, R-2`, so it was true of the stub and not of the file. The record puts this in F-128's family: an instrument doing less than the thing it stands in for (RECORD.md:1639, 1675). The prose was moved to its own bullet at `4fb7680`, before the claim, and re-measurement left only the two permanent findings (RECORD.md:1675, 1679, 1683). The same D-55 measurement found that one of the two "permanent" findings clears with R-4 at `discharged (T-6, T-10)`. That was left to a cycle close that never came (RECORD.md:1639, 2140).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** `check_trace` reads a `Discharges.` field one way: either ids by regex over the whole field, or a finding for any comma-separated piece that is not a bare id. And a measurement of a check against a planned task runs on the task file, not on a stub of it. Untested (RECORD.md:1675).
- **For v3.** The record's remedy: one parse of the field in `check_trace`, and a plan-skill rule that checks are measured on the real task file (RECORD.md:1675).

### A brief line did not stop a stale `check_report` reading, and the report sent was not the report filed
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (brief line C18 and a manager-side comparison; plugin unchanged)
- **Evidence.** F-133 (RECORD.md:1787), with F-113 and F-127; RECORD.md:1748, 1784, 1879, 1913, 2079; commits `3347800`, `67d144d`.

The first supervisor ran `check_report` after committing its block, as brief line C16 asked. At `67d144d` it corrected the block's `commits:` line from that run, but it left the `checks:` line on a pre-commit reading of the worker block before it. The message it sent the manager carried a third reading (F-133, RECORD.md:1748, 1787). `T19-verify-1447`'s FAIL rested on exactly that line: the block said one advisory finding and exit 0, while HEAD gave three findings and exit 1 (RECORD.md:1784). The record's reading is that "a written instruction did not hold where no check exists" (RECORD.md:1787). The next brief's C18 said to re-run `check_report` until block and run agree, then send up the block copied from the file. At the second stop the manager's script found the block received and the block filed identical, 272 lines each (RECORD.md:1879, 1913). At the third stop the block's reading was the post-commit run's (RECORD.md:2079).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** `check_report` compares a block's own `check_report` line with the run it is making and reports any disagreement, and `run` §6 compares the block the manager received with the block in the file before anything else. Untested (RECORD.md:1787).
- **For v3.** Both halves of that remedy, as checks rather than brief lines (RECORD.md:1787).

### Probes lived where no tracked file could name them, and rotations and sandbox closes lost them
- **Area.** A13 record & evidence
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (for verifiers and auditors, via brief line C22; the worker form was proposed and never applied)
- **Evidence.** F-134 (RECORD.md:1817); RECORD.md:1985, 2050, 2082, 2119, 2134.

An in-process verifier's probes sit in the dispatching manager's session scratchpad. `check_refs` forbids a tracked file to name that scratchpad, and a rotation leaves it behind. `T19-verify-1447`'s twelve probe scripts were found only by listing the predecessor's scratchpad, then copied into the git-ignored `devteam/.run/scratch/T-19/probes/` with md5s matching at both ends (F-134, RECORD.md:1817). The problem recurred at every verdict of the first restart. S-3's probes ran inline through `sandbox.py exec` and exist nowhere. The manager's own brief had told `T19-verify-0015` to build its probes outside `$REPO`, the opposite of F-134's first remedy (RECORD.md:1985). Brief line C22 then told every verifier and auditor to keep each probe as a named file. `T19-verify-1145`'s 16 probes were "the first verdict of T-19 whose instruments were found from the record" (RECORD.md:2050, 2119). The S-5 workers' probes died with their sandboxes' private `/tmp`, which left one `checks:` line unrunnable for its verifier. This is a worker form of the problem that C22 did not reach (RECORD.md:2082).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** A verifier brief names `devteam/.run/scratch/<T-n>/` as its probe directory, or the manager copies the probes there in the commit that lands the verdict (RECORD.md:1817). Workers keep their probes under the repository tree, git-ignored, where the kept overlay's `upper/` preserves them for the manager to copy; untested (RECORD.md:2134).
- **For v3.** Every dispatch template names a probe directory the record can cite, for workers as well as verifiers and auditors, and landing a verdict copies it (suggested here, not in the record; it combines the record's remedies at RECORD.md:1817, 2050, 2134).

### `check_scope` and `check_report` read a restarted task as though it had never restarted
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (brief line C21 for F-135, the step-scoped id for F-136; plugin unchanged)
- **Evidence.** F-135 (RECORD.md:1916); F-136 (RECORD.md:1917); RECORD.md:1972, 2049, 2083; F-109 (RECORD.md:1717, 1784, 1934, 1951, 2117).

`check_scope` takes a restarted task's claim window from its first claim. So the manager's own commits between that claim and the restart, made before the dispatch as `run` §4.3 asks, read as `misattributed-write` while the task is RUNNING. There were three such findings at the first restart and five at the second, and none after the retitle (F-135, RECORD.md:1916, 2049). `check_report . T-n` on a restarted task reads the last `REPORT` block. Until the new report lands, that block is the previous supervisor's, so mid-task the check compares a stale block with the current meter (F-136, RECORD.md:1917). S-4's two step verifiers found both; F-135 was traced to the source and reproduced (RECORD.md:1916, 1917). F-109's `model-mismatch` also fired at every stop, and each task verifier confirmed it false. That check compares the report's model with the last worker's through the task's single `.sandbox` pointer (RECORD.md:1717, 1934, 1784, 1951, 2117).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None for the plugin. The run's workaround for F-135 was a brief line listing the expected findings, C21, and it held: five findings and no others (RECORD.md:1972, 2049, 2083). For F-136, "the step-scoped id is the working form" (RECORD.md:1917).
- **For v3.** Both checks learn a task's restarts from the board or the record, and scope their window and their block to the current claim (suggested here, not in the record).

### Guards refused five harmless commands outside the project tree, each refusal aborting its whole command
- **Area.** A7 guard & permissions
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (brief lines C20 and N2; no guard changed)
- **Evidence.** F-137 (RECORD.md:1955); finding (v) (RECORD.md:1829); RECORD.md:1918; C20 (RECORD.md:1881, 2048); N2 (RECORD.md:2102).

The `claude-skills` repository's own root-allowlist guard polices every in-process agent the manager dispatches into pricelog, because the manager session is launched in `claude-skills`. It resolved a relative clone target against the `claude-skills` root rather than the command's own `cd`, and refused a harmless scratch clone twice (F-137, RECORD.md:1955). The record counts this with four refusals by "the guard" that went against P-10b, under which the guard does not police paths outside the project (RECORD.md:1829, 1918, 1955). The four were:
- `chmod +x` on a `/tmp` scratch file;
- a `cp` of an in-scope file to `/tmp`;
- `git stash list`, read as `git stash`;
- a relative `rm -rf` in a worker's own `/tmp` scratch.

Each refusal aborted the whole compound command, and twice it took earlier work with it: two heredocs that never ran, and an in-scope edit (RECORD.md:1829, 1918). The record calls F-137 "the launch convention's side effect, not a devteam rule" (RECORD.md:1955).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** No guard change. Brief lines C20 and N2 tell every agent to run probes as `python3 <file>`, to give every clone target and scratch path as an absolute path, and to confirm that every part of a compound command ran (RECORD.md:1881, 2048, 2102).
- **For v3.** The write guard honours P-10b for paths outside the project, and a manager launched from another repository is warned that that repository's guards reach its agents (suggested here, not in the record).

### The keepalive tick was not running when a REVERSIBLE window expired, and `run` §8 never starts it
- **Area.** A10 escalation & client
- **Status at the stop.** open (the successor was told to start it, but no later batch held a REVERSIBLE question)
- **Evidence.** F-138 (RECORD.md:1968); Q-43, D-61 (RECORD.md:1957, 1965); RECORD.md:1794, 1962, 1972, 2122, 2126.

The batch holding Q-43, a REVERSIBLE question with a one-hour window, went out at 01:01:27 with nothing running. The window expired at 02:01 and the client answered at 08:11:30. No `/loop /devteam:run tick` had been started to notice (F-138, RECORD.md:1962, 1965, 1968). It did no harm here: T-19 was stopped on Q-42 whatever Q-43 said, and the client selected the recommendation (RECORD.md:1968). The record names the cause. `run` §8 sends the batch and ends the turn, and the charter's `Keepalive tick` row is read at startup, hours before the phase it governs (RECORD.md:1968). The successor was told to start the tick for any such batch, but the only later batch held no REVERSIBLE question (RECORD.md:1972, 2122). The three batches that followed T-19's stops waited 3 h 41 min, 7 h 10 min and about two minutes (RECORD.md:1794, 1962, 2126).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** `run` §8's batch step starts the tick whenever the batch holds a REVERSIBLE question and nothing is running, and stops it when the batch is answered. Untested (RECORD.md:1968).
- **For v3.** That remedy, in `run` §8 (RECORD.md:1968).

### Items in adversaries' and workers' reports reached no owner, because nothing carries them to a disposition
- **Area.** A5 verification & adversaries
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (both F-139 items given dispositions by hand; plugin unchanged)
- **Evidence.** F-139 (RECORD.md:1987); RECORD.md:1828, 1829, 1986; Q-46, D-65 (RECORD.md:2121, 2129); T-19.md:168–175.

Two of S-3's six open items reached no owner. One was item 8: Ctrl-C leaves a 14-line traceback, which DM-4's "no traceback" rule forbids. The other was a "refusing <log>" line printed by a run whose record then landed. s24 had found (L)/(M) in S-3's section by grep and never read the open-items list whole (F-139, RECORD.md:1987). The same drop happened at two other relays. The manager's stop entry left out the first supervisor's findings-for-protocol, where `run` §6 puts them, and they landed late. And three findings from S-2's workers never reached the supervisor's report at all (RECORD.md:1828, 1829). Both F-139 items got dispositions once a successor asked about them. Item 8 became Q-46 and D-65, and the message was treated as a sentence in scope under Gate point 5 (RECORD.md:1986, 1987, 2121, 2129; T-19.md:168–175).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "An auditor's open items travel as fixed-form lines a check can count, and the landing that relays them gives each a disposition line", which the record calls "the join nothing makes today". Untested (RECORD.md:1987).
- **For v3.** Auditor, worker and supervisor reports carry open items and findings as countable lines, and a check fails any landing that leaves one without a disposition (close to the record's remedy, RECORD.md:1987).

### Plan and gate wording carried into a restart was false of the landed design or narrower than its rule
- **Area.** A8 planning & estimates
- **Status at the stop.** open (F-140's remedy was carried into the instructions for the next plan, which was never written)
- **Evidence.** F-140 (RECORD.md:2084); RECORD.md:1826, 1828, 1830, 1995, 2077, 2083, 2134, 2140; T-19.md:131–142.

S-5's plan and its brief both said that a substitution "during the check itself" is followed. That wording came from a task verifier's prototype. S-5's own check refuses a component it has not yet reached, so its first step verdict failed on the clause the plan had supplied (F-140, RECORD.md:2077, 2084). S-5's second worker met the same trap from the other side: "a brief that tells a worker to delete a clause because a verifier found it false can be half right". Reproducing both halves before editing kept a real window in the disclosure (RECORD.md:2077, 2083). Earlier, Gate point 2 had tied its disclosure branch to "the adversarial pass", but a step verifier found the break first. The record generalises this as "a gate that names which party may find a break is narrower than the rule it serves" (RECORD.md:1828). D-57 also left Gate point 3 standing in words that its disclosure made false. This was caught before the restart by reading the client's answer against the gate (RECORD.md:1830; T-19.md:131–142). Both plan amendments went ahead without a second GATE 2, recorded as decisions because the client's selections named the content (RECORD.md:1826, 1995).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "A plan cites a prototype's measurement as the prototype's, and leaves the disclosure's wording to be measured against the design that lands". Untested (RECORD.md:2084). The successor's instructions for S-6 carried it (RECORD.md:2134), but S-6 was never planned (RECORD.md:2140).
- **For v3.** The plan skill gets rules for amending a restarted task's plan: provenance on every measured figure, every gate point re-read against each new decision, and a stated rule on whether an amended plan goes back to GATE 2 (suggested here, not in the record).

### The protocol has no way to stop a run early, so the client's stop was recorded by hand
- **Area.** A12 iterate & cycles
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (recorded by hand on the pattern of a close)
- **Evidence.** F-141 (RECORD.md:2142); D-67 (RECORD.md:2140); RECORD.md:1676, 2141; commit `a0a0e71`.

`run`, `checkpoint`, `iterate` and `resume` each assume that the loop either continues or closes a cycle. Nothing says what a client's stop does to open claims, heartbeats, an announced rotation, or signed text left false (F-141, RECORD.md:2142). At D-67 the manager did the following by hand (RECORD.md:2141, 2142):
- rewrote T-19's and T-16's heartbeats to stop lines;
- marked the board `STOPPED (D-67)`, with a new legend value;
- added a `cancelled` line to `handoff-ready`, by analogy with `completed`. The record says this is "untested against the guard's reading of that file".

D-67 lists every signed sentence known to be false at HEAD, disclosed there because none will now be amended (RECORD.md:2140). The checkpoint-cadence question, which the run had put off until T-19's close, was never decided (RECORD.md:1676, 2140).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond the by-hand pattern it records (RECORD.md:2141, 2142).
- **For v3.** A stop procedure covering claims, heartbeats, a pending rotation and a list of signed text left false, with `handoff-ready`'s stop value defined where the guard reads it (suggested here, not in the record).

### The Agent tool delivers a prompt without its final newline
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** F-142 (RECORD.md:2143); F-119 (RECORD.md:2001).

This was measured at `T19-reresolve-0905`'s dispatch. The prompt in the transcript hashes to `0558931dcd139290b7fabe637e5e5843`. With a newline appended it hashes to `81b840047bc3c741562c01377fa81a03`, the md5 recorded for the sent file (RECORD.md:2001, 2143). F-119's landed-against-sent check holds once this is known. A check comparing raw bytes would report a difference that is not one (RECORD.md:2143).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond that reading (RECORD.md:2143).
- **For v3.** Any check that compares a sent brief with its transcript normalises the trailing newline (suggested here, not in the record).

### Each rotation in the slice cost more to take over and to hand off than the one before
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** F-110 (RECORD.md:1668, 1812, 1982); F-116 (RECORD.md:1656, 1669, 1801, 1905, 1971, 2003); D-47 (RECORD.md:1647, 1800, 1958, 1970, 2122); RECORD.md:1646, 1906, 2141, 2144.

The incoming manager's resume reading, which falls in no rotation's delta (F-110), was 4,448,218, 6,579,078 and 7,017,113 tokens at the thirteenth, fourteenth and fifteenth lock moves. Across all eleven lock moves it totals 43,693,154 (RECORD.md:1668, 1812, 1982). The outgoing manager's tail after its last intended write (F-116) was 1,966,865, then 3,391,424, then 7,210,395 tokens. Those were 11.3%, 16.0% and 15.7% of each rotation, against 2.5M–3.5M, 2.5M–3.5M and 3M–4M priced (RECORD.md:1656, 1669, 1801, 1905, 1971, 2003). The record puts most of the last tail on the handoff exchange itself: "the successor's four questions and the lock notice arrived after the predecessor's last intended write, and each answer re-read a context near 600,000 tokens" (RECORD.md:2003). D-47's cost-per-request readings ran from 1.76× to 3.24× each tenure's baseline. So a rotation followed every batch in the slice: three were taken, and the fourth was announced and then cancelled at the stop (RECORD.md:1647, 1800, 1958, 1970, 2122, 2141). Plans and restart briefs were written at those rates. T-19's first dispatch was preceded by 12,017,880 tokens of manager work, against Q-37's 7M–11M for the whole task, and the first restart's dispatch by 13,645,461 (RECORD.md:1646, 1906). At the stop, cycle 2's manager tokens stood at about 439,623,144 of 520,000,000, with resumes and in-process agents outside both meters (RECORD.md:2144).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None in this slice.
- **For v3.** The rotation model prices the successor's takeover read and the predecessor's handoff exchange, both of which grew with context here, and the plan skill prices a plan written at a rotation's cost per request (suggested here, not in the record).

### Every rotation in the slice needed the live predecessor to answer questions its record did not
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** Thirteenth lock move: RECORD.md:1666, 1670–1674. Fourteenth: RECORD.md:1810, 1813–1816. Fifteenth: RECORD.md:1980, 1983–1987. F-118, F-129, F-134, F-139.

At each of the three lock moves, the incoming manager recorded defects it could close only by asking the predecessor (RECORD.md:1671–1674, 1814–1816, 1984–1986). It asked every time what the client had said since the last write. At the fifteenth lock move the record calls this "the sixth rotation running with this defect", and that time the answer was a typed message the record never minuted (RECORD.md:1671, 1814, 1984). The other questions were about reasoning or material that existed only in the predecessor's session:
- whether anything had been drafted outside the tree (nothing had), and the empty-line probe's shape and the supervisor-model choice (RECORD.md:1672, 1674);
- what brief item 16 should become, and what "the accepted set it changes" meant, each settled by measuring in a clone (RECORD.md:1673, 1815);
- a `Retires.` line that missed R-6's Acceptance, volunteered by the manager who wrote it (RECORD.md:1816);
- where a verifier's probes were (F-134, RECORD.md:1985);
- two adversary items with no owner (F-139, RECORD.md:1986–1987).

The session-join question stayed mandatory, because F-118's remedy is "a plugin change not made" (RECORD.md:1666, 1810, 1980). The takeover gaps were 4 min 13 s, 7 min 9 s and 26 min 29 s. The record reads the first two as sampling the client's schedule rather than the mechanism (F-129, RECORD.md:1670, 1813, 1983).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Nothing new in this slice for the recurring question about what the client said. F-118's remedy is cited as not made (RECORD.md:1666), and F-134 and F-139 carry their own remedies (RECORD.md:1817, 1987).
- **For v3.** `handoff-ready`, or a check on it, requires what these successors had to ask for: client input since the last write, anything drafted outside the tree, where probes live, and open items without a disposition. Then a predecessor that has died does not take them with it (suggested here, not in the record).

### At each of T-19's three stops the heartbeat still named an agent the supervisor no longer awaited
- **Area.** A3 supervisor & steps
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1749, 1913, 2078; RECORD.md:1828 (iv).

At the first stop T-19's heartbeat still named the supervisor's last verifier dispatch. At the second it named S-3 as awaited, and at the third it named S-5's second verifier as awaited. The manager rewrote each one to a stop line (RECORD.md:1749, 1913, 2078). The third supervisor also disclosed four process errors of its own, each corrected (RECORD.md:2078):
- a step verifier dispatched before its sandbox was promoted;
- both step verdicts landed late and together, against brief item 5;
- a pathspec-commit trap;
- a `check_refs` leak.

The first supervisor owned two dispatch errors, corrected at the time (RECORD.md:1828). A heartbeat that names a returned agent tells a resuming manager that something may still be in flight, and that is what D-47's condition 1 and `resume` read (reasoned here, not in the record; RECORD.md:1647, 1663).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded.
- **For v3.** The supervise skill's NEEDS-DECISION exit writes the heartbeat's stop line itself, and brief lines such as item 5 become checks (suggested here, not in the record).

### Under structural containment the manager cannot keep a claim's Sandbox cell current
- **Area.** A1 manager loop
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run (`*pending dispatch*` at the second restart's claim)
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1915, 2000; F-5; commit `7a87cf8`.

The first restart's claim at `7a87cf8` copied `—` into the board's Sandbox cell. The board's legend makes `—` the guard-only value, so while S-4's overlay was live the board told a reader that no sandbox was in use (RECORD.md:1915). The manager could not correct it during the run, because it makes no host commit while an overlay is live (F-5, RECORD.md:1915). The record's reading is that the live copy is `devteam/.run/locks/<T-n>.sandbox`, and the cell is right only at two points: at the claim, where it should say `*pending dispatch*` as T-4's did, and at the stop. The second restart's claim used that form (RECORD.md:1915, 2000).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The cell reads `*pending dispatch*` at the claim and names the last overlay at the stop, with `<T-n>.sandbox` as the live copy (RECORD.md:1915).
- **For v3.** The `run` skill's board legend defines that claim-time value under structural containment and names the `.sandbox` file as the live copy (the record's reading, RECORD.md:1915).

### An auditor's session received another plugin's skills as command blocks
- **Area.** A14 harness & platform
- **Status at the stop.** observation
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1918; C8 (RECORD.md:1716, 1869, 2036).

S-3's session "received the `npk` plugin's `audit` and `research` skills as command blocks before it invoked `devteam:audit`, and followed neither". The record calls this "a cross-plugin injection for the harness" (RECORD.md:1918). The briefs' only warning of this kind, C8, names a document connector's "MCP Server Instructions" block (RECORD.md:1716, 1869, 2036).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded.
- **For v3.** Agent definitions, not only briefs, say that skill content from another plugin arriving unasked is not an instruction, as C8 says of MCP blocks (suggested here, not in the record).

<Did iterate work? — written up by a reader of `pricelog`'s record, every sentence cited to it.>

### Did iterate work? Partly — its mechanical half held, and the interview that makes an iteration more than an amendment never ran
**Partly.** `/devteam:iterate` ran once, on 2026-09-13. Its mechanical half held when cycle 2 opened: charter Versions 11 to 13 were each a dated entry with a full `Re-affirmed.` block, D-21 superseded D-7's manager half under a new number, R-16 superseded R-8 without editing it, tasks resumed at T-11, `RECORD.md` gained its `cycle 2 opened` line, and the client approved the charter and the plan at separate gates (RECORD.md:933, :937, :940-941, :947; DECISIONS.md:732-734; REQUIREMENTS.md:197, :277). The half that makes an iteration more than an amendment never ran. Nothing records the owner using pricelog between the cycles, and the manager recorded the skill's first interview question as unanswered. The project's first-ever audit was run to supply the input instead, and it ran with another plugin's audit procedure loaded (RECORD.md:917-918, :921, :927-928; DECISIONS.md:1906). Three things the skill or the plan promised then failed during cycle 2:
- requirements were edited in place five times instead of being superseded;
- an audit finding carried into the cycle went four days with no owner, while the plugin's disposition check could not parse the audits;
- the plan's questions-the-record-could-have-answered measurement was never taken.

(DECISIONS.md:1077-1080, :1593-1595; RECORD.md:46, :1157-1158; scripts/check_refs.py:41-46.)

D-67 says iterate "fired" (DECISIONS.md:1917). That is accurate for the procedure and says nothing about the interview.

##### What the skill and the plan said should happen

**The skill, read whole.**
- **§1: is this an iteration?** An addition inside the existing goals is an amendment and gets "No new cycle". An iteration is for when "now that I've used it" shows the goals were right but something under them was wrong or missing. Changed goals mean a new project. "The distinguishing question is whether the goals survive." (SKILL.md:18-27)
- **§2: read before asking.** Read the charter, then the checkpoints (last first), then the decisions, then `audits/`, where declined findings "are the ones that come back", then the record, then open questions. "Never re-ask something the record answers." (SKILL.md:34-45)
- **§3: a short interview in three areas.**
  - What using it taught: "the whole point", and "the only question the record cannot answer".
  - Which decisions to revisit, showing the `proceeded-unreviewed` ones first and saying which have become expensive to reverse.
  - What moved into or out of scope.

  (SKILL.md:52-66)
- **§4: amend, never rewrite.** The charter gets a new `Version.`, a dated amendment and a `Re-affirmed.` block covering every `DM-n` and constraint row (P-48), and the old text stays. A changed requirement "is superseded by a new one … it is not edited". A reversed decision gets a new number. Discharged requirements stay discharged. (SKILL.md:70-87)
- **§5: then the ordinary loop.** Tasks number onward, and `RECORD.md` gets a `cycle 2 opened` line and continues. "The client approves the amended charter, then the plan." (SKILL.md:91-97)
- **§6: what iteration is not.** It is not a way to avoid saying a cycle failed, and it is not a fresh start. (SKILL.md:104-112)

**The plan (0.2.9).**
- **Why iterate is the point.** L-9.7 makes firing `iterate` "the single thing this subcycle exists for" (0.2.9.md:69-72). L-9.8 chose pricelog because it "cannot demonstrate its main feature on a single run". That makes repeated use between cycles "forced by the tool", which in turn is "what makes cycle 2's first interview area answerable at all" (0.2.9.md:75-81). That was reasoned at planning, not measured.
- **The interval.** Between the cycles "the owner runs the tool, repeatedly, for real". No duration is fixed, and "if he has nothing, that is a recorded result rather than a delay" (0.2.9.md:348-353).
- **The opening's expected outputs** (0.2.9.md:355-368):
  - iterate is run "exactly as the skill says";
  - the new charter version is "`amendment-omits-condition`'s first meeting with a real charter";
  - every open audit finding "is shown to the client with its `Needs.` and gets a disposition; `undispositioned-finding` is then clean";
  - "whether any requirement is edited rather than superseded is recorded either way" for PAIRS row 18;
  - `cycle 2 opened` is written, and task numbers continue.
- **The measurements.**
  - Every checkpoint carries a `## Cycle measurements` heading, including a "requirement edits" row for PAIRS row 18 (0.2.9.md:372-373, :386).
  - Questions the record could have answered are "logged as a defect in the skill" (0.2.9.md:320-322).
  - §5 extends that logging to iterate (0.2.9.md:451).
- **Do not.** "Do not let a session play the client", and "do not pre-empt the interview's answers" (0.2.9.md:459-460).

##### What happened, in order

**1. Cycle 1's close.**
- T-10, cycle 1's last task, closed DONE and was verified PASS (RECORD.md:885, :893). Before that, its verifier broke the task's REFUTE claim, and Q-13 went to the client as `CHARTER` (RECORD.md:895-898).
- The client took the recommendation inside the hour. D-20 and charter Version 10 followed, and all twelve requirements stood discharged for the first time (RECORD.md:899-900).
- C-5 was filed as the final checkpoint under GATE 4, with the verdict `DRIFTED`. The verdict was about the budget ceiling, not the product: the manager half of D-7's ceiling could not be measured (F-67) (RECORD.md:904-907; C-5:197-228).
- C-5 §8 offered accept, amend or iterate. It described iterate as "the goals held, and using the tool taught something the specification could not", and as carrying "the audits" forward (C-5:287-302). No audit had been run at that point (RECORD.md:921).

**2. The choice at the gate.**
- The client chose iterate: *"well, we actually have never tested iterate and i don't see why now isn't as good a time as any"* (RECORD.md:915).
- The manager recorded that this settled the accept/amend/iterate question and not C-5's separate ceiling question, which it carried forward as open (RECORD.md:916).
- The manager later recorded something about this moment. By iterate §1's test, everything outstanding was then an amendment: R-4's shape, the ceiling, and two cosmetic comments. The audit is what made it a real iteration (RECORD.md:934). The skill's table routes an amendment to "No new cycle" (SKILL.md:20).

**3. What iterate carried forward.** The `cycle 2 opened` line lists what came across: "charter v12, twenty-one decisions, thirteen struck questions, five checkpoints, four audits and this record" (RECORD.md:933). It adds that requirements keep their numbers and that "C-5's `DRIFTED` verdict stands as filed" (RECORD.md:933). C-5's list of work left undone also came across. The re-litigated R-4 (C-5:273-274) became Q-14 and was settled by D-23 at the plan gate (RECORD.md:947-949). The client set one standing instruction for the cycle: any further finding "joins the list taken back to the plugin … rather than being fixed here" (RECORD.md:932).

**4. What was re-interviewed, and how the client answered.**
- **Area one, what using the tool taught: not asked.** The manager recorded that the client "did not say pricelog had been used and taught him something". So the question "is still unanswered, which is why the audit runs first rather than an interview being pressed" (RECORD.md:918).
- **The audit in its place.**
  - The client gave two answers at the gate: `/devteam:audit` runs first, and the ceiling's manager half is replaced with a measurable instrument (RECORD.md:917).
  - Four audits were filed, the project's first. They broke all four refute claims and found nine substantive defects, three of them reproduced live (RECORD.md:921).
  - One auditor disclosed that the skill loaded for it was `npk:audit`. The manager verified that three devteam agents' unqualified skill names collide with another plugin's (F-69, RECORD.md:926-927).
- **Area two, decisions to revisit.** There were no `proceeded-unreviewed` decisions to show: C-5 found that table empty across thirteen questions (C-5:97-111). One decision was revisited, D-7's manager half, and D-21 superseded it. That came from C-5's `DRIFTED` question, not from an area-two review (RECORD.md:916-919; DECISIONS.md:726-734). The entries from the gate to the first dispatch show no review of cycle 1's other decisions (RECORD.md:913-950).
- **Area three, scope.**
  - After cycle 2 opened, the client raised the cycle-2 manager ceiling from 150,000,000 to 250,000,000 (RECORD.md:935).
  - The client sent all four decision-class audit findings to cycle 2: R-4's shape, a response body-size ceiling, a security lint ruleset, and D-1's never-exercised push grant (RECORD.md:935).
  - The client chose which findings cycle 2 settles, not what their answers are (RECORD.md:935; DECISIONS.md:783-786).
  - The manager put its reading in writing so the client could correct it in one line. Three `REVERSIBLE` questions arriving together form one batch, and so count as one "thing that pops up" under C-4's standing instruction to let it sit (RECORD.md:936).
- **Unprompted.** The client added that their weekly meter blends model families, so the non-Fable remainder is what governs an Opus manager (RECORD.md:932).

**5. The amendments.**
- **Version 11 (D-21).** The manager half of the ceiling became the sum of per-rotation deltas, superseding D-7's manager half only. The manager set cycle 2 at 150,000,000 manager tokens and `$40` of workers (RECORD.md:919; DECISIONS.md:732-741; CHARTER.md:1329-1362). The instrument was demonstrated before it was signed: a delta of 25,385,363 over 111 requests (RECORD.md:920).
- **Version 12 (a backfill).** The hygiene audit found F-68: `amendment-omits-condition` slices to the end of the file, so it had not seen Versions 10 and 11 drop the Licence, Repository and Public? rows. Version 12 restored all 31 entries, checked with `comm` (RECORD.md:922-924; CHARTER.md:1262-1292).
- **Version 13 (D-22).**
  - It added DM-14 (a future-dated record must not block every coin) and DM-15 (writing goes where the log path names), both written as rules with no list of causes. It also raised the manager ceiling to 250,000,000 (RECORD.md:937; CHARTER.md:1184-1222; DECISIONS.md:771-809).
  - Its `Re-affirmed.` block first listed DM-14 and DM-15 as `added (this entry)`. `check_trace` refused that, N-4's sixth instance, and they were entered as `amended (this entry)` (RECORD.md:938-939; CHARTER.md:1241-1242).
  - The client approved: *"i have read the charter. looks good to me. I approve."* (RECORD.md:940).
- **Requirements.** Four new requirements, R-13 to R-16, went to four of the six new tasks (RECORD.md:941). R-16 superseded R-8, and R-8 was left unedited (REQUIREMENTS.md:197, :277).

**6. Cycle 2's first task.**
- **The plan.** The planner was told to invoke `devteam:plan` by its qualified name. It produced T-11 to T-16, "exactly the approved scope" (RECORD.md:941, :944).
- **The plan gate.**
  - The client answered Q-14 and approved the plan: *"lets go with your recommendation for q-14. the plan looks fine. please proceed."* (RECORD.md:947).
  - The manager did not treat "please proceed" as an answer to Q-15, Q-16 or Q-17, whose window had twenty minutes left at 22:57 (RECORD.md:948).
  - D-23 recorded `Shape reviewed.` on R-4 (RECORD.md:949; DECISIONS.md:816-843).
- **The first claims.**
  - T-12 and T-13 were claimed together at width 2, with their scopes checked for overlap and found disjoint (RECORD.md:950).
  - At 23:17 the Q-15 to Q-17 window expired with no answer. P-27's unreviewed path fired for the first time in the project, producing D-24, D-25 and D-26 (RECORD.md:953-955).
- **The first close.**
  - T-13 was the first cycle-2 task to close, on a fresh verifier's PASS (RECORD.md:981, :987, :998).
  - T-12 stopped on a `CHARTER` question about a hardlink residual (Q-19), and closed after the client answered (RECORD.md:988-989, :1004-1005).
  - C-6 was then filed `ON-COURSE` (RECORD.md:1010; C-6:166-173).

##### What worked

- **Carrying forward without restarting.** Cycle 2 opened with everything carried and "nothing restarts". Tasks resumed at T-11, and the record continued under a `cycle 2 opened` line, as SKILL.md:91-94 asks — RECORD.md:933, :941.
- **The failed verdict stayed failed.** C-5's `DRIFTED` "stands as filed", and opening cycle 2 "does not discharge anything cycle 1 left undone", as SKILL.md:104-107 requires — RECORD.md:933. Cycle 1's ten tasks stayed DONE and its twelve requirements stayed discharged — DECISIONS.md:811-814 (D-22).
- **Amending, never rewriting, at the opening.** Versions 11, 12 and 13 are each a dated entry with a `Re-affirmed.` block, and the earlier text stays — CHARTER.md:1184-1392. Version 13's 33 entries were checked with `comm` against the live Constraints table, because F-68 showed the script could not do it — RECORD.md:937 (D-22).

##### The questions the record could have answered

A search of `RECORD.md` for "record could have answered" and "could-have-answered" returns that line alone. **What was actually asked, from C-5's gate to the first dispatch**, for any reader who wants to take the count now (listed here, not in the record):
1. Accept, amend or iterate? The client chose iterate (C-5:287-302; RECORD.md:915).

4. The cycle-2 manager ceiling: keep 150,000,000 or raise it? Raised to 250,000,000 (RECORD.md:935).

6. Approve charter Version 13? Approved (RECORD.md:940).
7. Q-14, R-4's shape, and GATE 2's plan? The recommendation was accepted and the plan approved (RECORD.md:947).
8. Q-15, Q-16 and Q-17? Left unanswered on purpose, and they proceeded unreviewed (RECORD.md:948, :953-955).

(suggested here, not in the record) Each of these asks for a choice or an approval that only the client could give, and none repeats a question the record had already settled. The count would probably have been zero. But a zero here would measure a gate, not the iterate interview, whose first area was never put (RECORD.md:918).

### The interview's central question, what using the tool taught, was never asked, and nothing records the owner using pricelog between the cycles
- **Area.** A12 iterate & cycles
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:918; RECORD.md:913, :930, :934; DECISIONS.md:648, :658-660 (D-19); DECISIONS.md:1906 (D-67); 0.2.9.md:75-81, :348-353; SKILL.md:20, :52-53

The manager recorded that the client "did not say pricelog had been used and taught him something", so the question stayed unanswered (RECORD.md:918). Cycle 1 closed and cycle 2 opened on the same day, 2026-09-13 (RECORD.md:913, :930). R-10 asks for "a person" to follow the README; it was met by two dispatched agents, and the client knew that (DECISIONS.md:648, :658-660). At the stop, the client called pricelog "a tool with no user" (DECISIONS.md:1906). The plan's reasoned premise, that the tool would force repeated use (0.2.9.md:75-81), did not hold. - **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded. The run used an audit in place of use (RECORD.md:917).
- **For v3.** Iterate needs a stated path for "no use yet". One option: record whether use happened as the opening's first line, and name the substitute input. Another: route the request to an amendment under §1 (suggested here, not in the record).

### The plugin's `undispositioned-finding` check exists, but it could not see any of the four gate audits, because they did not use its finding namespace
- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** scripts/check_refs.py:41-46, :54, :498-508; audits/pricelog-correctness-2026-09-13.md:9, :44, :54, :66; audits/pricelog-hygiene-2026-09-13.md:12, :24, :39, :53, :59; audits/pricelog-security-2026-09-13.md:10, :28, :42, :50; audits/pricelog-safety-2026-09-13.md:7, :30, :44; C-6:16; C-7:17; RECORD.md:1157; docs/CONSOLIDATION.md:157-158

`check_refs.py` recognises an audit finding only by a heading in the form `COR-n`, `SEC-n`, `HYG-n`, `REV-n` or `CNV-n`. It reports `undispositioned-finding` for such a finding that has no non-open `Disposition.` line (scripts/check_refs.py:41-46, :54, :498-508). The four gate audits head their findings "## Finding 1", "### 1." and "## F-1 — HIGH —", and none carries a `Disposition.` line (the audit lines listed above). So `check_refs` reported "devteam: clean" at C-6 and C-7 while Finding 3 had no owner (C-6:16; C-7:17).

The plan's "`undispositioned-finding` is then clean" was therefore met without the check reading anything (0.2.9.md:363). CONSOLIDATION item 7's "the disposition half has never been exercised on a live audit" remains true of these four audits (docs/CONSOLIDATION.md:157-158). F-99's "nothing checks" (RECORD.md:1157) is narrower in fact: a check exists and read none of these findings (suggested here, not in the record, which never mentions the check). F-69 may explain the format mismatch, since the auditors ran with `npk:audit` loaded, but that is unverified (RECORD.md:927) (suggested here, not in the record). The security audit also numbers its findings `F-1` to `F-4`, the same prefix the record uses for its own findings (audits/pricelog-security-2026-09-13.md:10; RECORD.md:62) (suggested here, not in the record).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None recorded.
- **For v3.** Make the audit skill's output format and `check_refs`'s audit namespace one contract. When `audits/` holds findings the check cannot parse, it should report that instead of reporting clean (suggested here, not in the record).

- **Area.** A6 mechanical checks

Notes on the table.
- Rows 1 to 4 take their time from `git log` of the lock commit because those entries give none. Rows 5 to 15 give the time in the writer-handoff line, and it matches `git log` of the lock commit.
- All fifteen were planned and every predecessor was alive, so `resume` §1's reconstruction path was never exercised by a rotation. The one other resume was the same manager, `cacdf5d0`, after the client's weekly usage limit stopped every session from about 23:35 on 2026-09-14 until 2026-09-17 (1176; F-106 at 1178). s14's tenure therefore ran from 2026-09-13 16:41 to 2026-09-17 04:55 across that stop.
- A sixteenth lock move, the seventh under D-47, was announced at `a0a0e71` (2135) and cancelled when the client stopped the run (D-67, DECISIONS.md:1904). `handoff-ready` gained a `cancelled` line and s26 never ran `/devteam:resume` (2141; F-141 at 2142).
- From the outgoing manager's second reading to the lock commit took about six minutes at the sixth to eighth lock moves (07:01:18 → 07:07:23, 09:18:31 → 09:24:08, 12:47:27 → 12:53:20; 1216/1224, 1251/1259, 1324/1333; computed here) and about fourteen at the ninth (02:14:30 → 02:28:49; 1364, 1379). The record puts announcement to lock at about 38 and 10 minutes at the tenth and eleventh (1626); at the twelfth, 4 h 13 min from the predecessor's last act to the resume (1626); and at the thirteenth to fifteenth, 4 min 13 s, 7 min 9 s and 26 min 29 s from the announcement's commit to the resume (1670, 1813, 1983).

##### What the handoffs measured

The first rotation defined the measurement: "Not one was something the outgoing manager withheld — every one was something no file was shaped to carry" (134). `grep -c "record defect"` gives 62 lines; 51 of them are record-defect entries and 11 only mention the phrase (293, 502, 509, 842, 1208, 1231, 1329, 1344, 1572, 1581, 1599). Of the 51, 47 were raised at a rotation by the successor's questions (four of those volunteered by the predecessor rather than asked: 1585, 1631, 1674, 1816), two were filed by an outgoing manager against itself before the lock moved (504, 506), one was the incoming manager's own misstep (275), and one came from the post-usage-limit resume's title sweep, not a rotation (1182; F-107).

Per rotation the count ran 5, 5, 2, 3, 3, 2, 3, 3, 3, 3, 2, 3, 4, 3, 3 (table above). D-47 read the flat run of 3, 2, 3, 3 at the fifth to eighth lock moves as the record "carried its weight each time" (DECISIONS.md:1605). The count never fell to zero.

| group | record-defect lines | count | rotations | status at the stop |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. What the client said since the outgoing manager's last write, or whether the client answered | 139, 257, 504, 520, 852, 1199, 1200, 1226, 1262, 1335, 1381, 1488, 1584, 1628, 1671, 1814, 1984 | 17 | all fifteen | Recurred to the end. Called structural from the sixth lock move: "an outgoing manager writes nothing after its last intended write, so whatever the client says between that write and the lock move has no file to land in, and only a live predecessor can carry it" (1226); "which is what a structural defect looks like from inside" (1335). The fifteenth found one typed client message the record never minuted (1984). |
| B. Which live session is which (id to name) | 136, 256 | 2 | 1, 2 | Worked around in the run. Solved at the third lock move by reading another session's transcript (509–510, with the `head -1` trap at 511); F-118 found a session can read its own name and proposed a `name` line in `handoff-ready` as "a plugin change between iterations" (1378). The question stayed mandatory to the end (1580, 1622, 1666, 1810, 1980). |
| C. A budget figure no file carried | 137, 506, 521 | 3 | 1, 3 | Worked around in the run: F-14 became D-7 (147), the worker figure was carried forward rather than recomputed from T-6's report on (547), the budget was read at the start of a tenure from the fourth lock move (858), and D-21's two readings were recorded at every rotation from the fifth. After the third lock move no record defect asked what the budget stood at; the budget items that remained concerned the client's answer (1199), a weighed but unrecorded decision (1264) and a recorded delta that was a floor (1384). |
| D. Work or reasoning a successor needed that sat only outside the tree (a message, a transcript, a scratchpad) | 259, 260, 1227, 1263, 1382, 1489, 1585, 1631, 1672, 1674, 1985 | 11 | 2, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15 | Recurred to the end (1985). Partly worked around: briefs landed whole (F-119, 1383), after which "which lines carry" could be answered by naming lines (1489); drafts and probes copied to `devteam/.run/scratch/<T-n>/` (1599, 1817, 1985, 2119). S-3's probes "exist nowhere" (1985). |

The record's own rules for it: "A debt recorded without an owner is not a plan" (138); "A held decision whose reasons expire silently becomes a decision nobody made" (140); "A decision recorded without its expiry condition reads as standing" (854). |

Piped exit codes (F-24, three times in one day at 1336): from the ninth lock move every resume ran the checks with output to a file and read the exit code from the check (1373, 1478, 1577, 1619, 1663, 1808, 1978). The last-write sha: the tenth lock move's defect produced "the rotation entry names its own commit as the last intended write" (1487), and the eleventh to fifteenth took the lock without asking (1581, 1623, 1667, 1811, 1981). |

Other things the handoffs measured.
- **The last-write sha.** At the third lock move it took three values in under fifteen minutes, each right when given (519). The fourth lock move's predecessor diagnosed why: "the sha is unstable while the predecessor still owes writes it has not made" (851). The remedy in group F ended the question from the eleventh lock move on (1581).
- **Taking the lock.** The guard refused the whole lock-taking command at the second, third and twelfth lock moves, because `BOARD.md` and `.run/session/manager` were written in one command (262, 523–524, 1623). The rule it enforces, "BOARD.md is always writable" only when written alone and in its own command, is stated in neither `resume` §0 step 4 nor `run` §7b (526, 856). The seventh lock move also committed the lock with `git add` and a bare `git commit`, which `run` §4.5 forbids (1260).
- **The guard's messages.** A replaced manager gets the right message (142). The incoming manager at a planned rotation gets "the GENERIC takeover recipe — 'if that session is gone, take the lock'", written for a successor whose predecessor is gone (263).
- **Reconciliation.** Every resume found tree and record agreeing ("Nothing was reconciled because nothing disagreed": 517, 849, 1193, 1220, 1255, 1373, 1478, 1577, 1619, 1663, 1808, 1978), except the eighth lock move, where the record's account of two check results was wrong (1329).
- **Provenance.** The second lock move's `promote.lock` answer (259) was corrected by its own author as "a source reading presented as a fact ... a strong inference, untested" (293).

##### Cost per request inside a tenure

**What D-47 rests on.** D-47 cites three off-cadence rotations: "about 120–200K to 260K (s15), 117K to 314.6K (s16), 171K to 345K (s17)" (DECISIONS.md:1605; the readings are at 1216, 1251, 1324). s18's tenure, which ended at the ninth lock move, went from about 107,000 to about 262,000 (1364). The case without rotation is s14's tenure, which passed C-5, C-6 and C-7 without rotating (1188): its first 138 requests averaged about 202,000 (1142; D-21 gives 27,896,558 over 138, DECISIONS.md:724), a request cost about 361,000 at 434 requests, "the size of this session's context after one compaction" (1142), and about 470,000 by 473 (1159). D-32 records the manager half moving from 61% at C-7 to 91.6% at C-8 over that stretch (DECISIONS.md:1172). s14's whole tenure delta, 231,151,044, which spans cycle 1's close and cycle 2 through T-17 (1189), averages 493,912 a request over its 468 requests after lock-take (495 less 27; computed here from 858 and 1189).

**The readings each D-47 rotation recorded.** The baseline from the ninth lock move on is the resume's own average (lock-take tokens over lock-take requests).

**Why condition 2 fires early.** The tenth lock move recorded that "a resume is where a successor builds the context it then works from", so condition 2, which "compares a tenure against the stretch that loaded it", reads "roughly doubled" within a few dozen requests of every lock-take (1492). s20, rotating out at the eleventh lock move, gave the mechanism for its own cost: 285,204 a request "because its resume loaded 5.3M of context before the lock and every request after it re-read that" (1570; reasoned). The tenth lock move wrote its reading down "so the next checkpoint reads it" (1492). No checkpoint ran after C-9 (1348 is the last `checkpoint C-n` verdict line; D-67 stopped the run), so neither that reading nor D-47's falsifier — "Two more rotations whose deltas are small enough that the resumes cost more than the doubling saves" (DECISIONS.md:1605) — was ever read.

**The numbers D-47's falsifier needs, assembled here (computed here from the lock-take and close readings in the rotation table; the tenures did different work, so this is not a controlled comparison).**

Three things follow from these, all computed here. Every rotated tenure averaged 1.9 to 2.5 times its own resume's rate (tail included from s18 on), which is comparable to the rates at which predecessors rotated (232,224 to 394,978 over a tenure; 1470, 1569, 1611, 1647, 1800, 1970). Tenure averages rose over the run, from 251,000–271,000 (s15 to s18, and s22) to 417,000–421,000 (s24, s25), while resumes rose from 1.8M–2.9M to 6.6M–7.0M. Each rotation's overhead — the predecessor's tail plus the successor's resume — was 5,657,157 (ninth), 8,037,181 (tenth), 6,627,803 (eleventh), 6,306,888 (twelfth), 6,415,083 (thirteenth), 9,970,502 (fourteenth) and 14,227,508 (fifteenth). Whether a rotation saved more than its overhead cannot be read from these figures, because each tenure did different work; that is the comparison D-47's falsifier left to a checkpoint that never ran. The record's own late view is that rotating earlier would have been cheaper, not later: Q-44 names "rotating more often, since this tenure's second half ran at 2.6 times its first half's cost per request" as what would beat its figure (QUESTIONS.md:636), and Q-47 names "rotating at the doubling rather than after the batch — this tenure crossed 2× before its report arrived" (QUESTIONS.md:687).

**What the meters leave out.** Resumes sit outside every ceiling: 43,693,154 across the fifth to fifteenth lock-takes (1982, 2144; F-110 at 1249). The tails from the ninth lock move on sum to 22,789,046 (computed here) and are inside the sum, because the predecessor's true close was read (the ninth's last "few hundred thousand" excepted, 1380). In-process supervisors, verifiers and auditors are outside both halves: 546,345,463 tokens over 2,171 requests by 2026-09-14, "2.7 times the manager half" (1143; F-33 at 478).

In cycle 2 the ceiling rose ten times, from 150,000,000 and $40 to 520,000,000 and $135 (CHARTER.md:260). The counted manager spend stopped at about 439.6M (2144), with 43,693,154 of resumes outside it (2144) and in-process agents outside it as well (F-33). Cycle 1's manager figure is a floor of 303,077,532 with an unrecoverable true value (CHARTER.md:260; D-21).

**The estimate pattern.** From D-37 on, every raise was sized to the high end of a remaining-work band plus one incident, and every one was overtaken before the first item on its list was finished. The next question came 12.1M to 38.4M later (computed here), each time because a task stopped again or work was added: T-18 stopped twice (1232, 1302), T-19 three times (1746, 1909, 2075), T-16's first dispatch once and it never restarted (1562; D-67), and D-53 added T-19 itself, where each estimate carried one incident. D-48, D-54, D-56, D-58, D-62 and D-66 each name as their falsifier only the low-end case, headroom nobody spends (D-51 adds a drift case for its one-pass half). From D-37's question to the stop, 198,993,459 was spent (computed here) against the 11M–15M D-37 priced for T-18, T-16, a checkpoint and the cycle close: T-18 closed (1462), C-9 ran as the extra checkpoint D-38's falsifier required (1348), and T-16's restart and the cycle close never ran (D-67).

### What the client said after the outgoing manager's last write reached no file, at all fifteen rotations
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** Group A above, 17 record-defect lines (RECORD.md:139, 257, 504, 520, 852, 1199, 1200, 1226, 1262, 1335, 1381, 1488, 1584, 1628, 1671, 1814, 1984); F-21 (RECORD.md:239).
Early answers found real losses: four escalations spoken and never filed (F-21; 239, 257), a "great. lets continue." the record read as silence (504, 520), the C-4 reply (852). From the sixth lock move the answer was "Nothing, in any form" every time (1226, 1262, 1335, 1381, 1488, 1584, 1628, 1671, 1814), until the fifteenth found one typed message the record never minuted (1984). The succession was unconfirmed at lock-take from the sixth lock move on; it was confirmed afterwards once in words (1231) and seven times by a selection (1344, 1388, 1494, 1605, 1651, 1797, 1966), and no `client confirmed` line appears for s17 or s25.
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Ask it at every rotation: "the highest-yield of the five, and it is the one no checker can ever replace" (139). Leave the announcement entry open until the reply exists or a line records that none came (852).
- **For v3.** Make "what has the client said since <sha>" a fixed `resume` §0 question whose answer is a fixed line of the `resumed` entry, so it stops being filed as a defect (suggested here, not in the record).

### A rotation's resume is outside every ceiling, and it grew from 1.8M to 7.0M tokens
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-110 (RECORD.md:1249–1250); lock-take readings at RECORD.md:1198, 1225, 1261, 1334, 1380, 1485, 1582, 1624, 1668, 1812, 1982; the disclosures in D-44, D-48, D-54, D-56, D-58, D-62 and D-66.
D-21 opens each tenure's delta at the lock-take, so the resume before it is counted nowhere: 43,693,154 across eleven lock-takes (1982, 2144). The tenth lock move attributed the growth to "the record's own length" (1485); the eleventh called that not refuted by one reading, "what a successor chooses to read moves it as much" (1582; reasoned, not measured).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None beyond disclosing it with every ceiling decision.
- **For v3.** Open the delta at the successor's first request rather than at its lock-take, so the ceiling sees what the client's weekly gauge sees (suggested here, not in the record).

### The outgoing manager's tail after its last reading was 11–23% of a rotation and reached 7.2M
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** F-116 (RECORD.md:1365); tails at RECORD.md:1491, 1583, 1625, 1669, 1905, 2003.
The last tail was twice what the predecessor priced, and "the handoff's own exchange is most of it: the successor's four questions and the lock notice arrived after the predecessor's last intended write, and each answer re-read a context near 600,000 tokens" (2003).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** The successor takes the predecessor's final reading (1472), after its last turn as shown by `ListAgents`' idle state (1491).
- **For v3.** Put the true-close reading into `resume` §0, and send the successor's standing questions in one message so the predecessor re-reads its largest context once (suggested here, not in the record).

### Cost per request doubles inside every tenure, and D-47's test for it fires on the resume's own weight
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1492, 1570, 1600, 1751, 1837, 2002; D-47 (DECISIONS.md:1605); the tenure table above.
Condition 2 compares a tenure with its resume, so it read 1.63×, 1.71× and 1.67× before a tenure's first dispatch (1492, 1837, 2002) and 1.97× while a verifier was about to be dispatched (1751). D-47's falsifier was written to be read at the next checkpoint, and no checkpoint ran after C-9 (1348).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None; the reading was "written down so the next checkpoint reads it" (1492).
- **For v3.** State condition 2's baseline explicitly (for example the first stretch after lock-take, not the resume), and record resume plus tail beside each tenure's delta in the rotation entry so D-47's falsifier can be read without a checkpoint (suggested here, not in the record).

### Every ceiling raise was overtaken before the first item it priced was finished
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** The ceiling table above; Q-31, Q-34, Q-35, Q-38, Q-39, Q-41, Q-44, Q-47 (QUESTIONS.md:412–691); D-37 to D-66 (DECISIONS.md:1379–1903); stops at RECORD.md:1232, 1302, 1562, 1746, 1909, 2075.
Ten raises in cycle 2. Each estimate carried one incident, while T-18 stopped twice and T-19 three times (1232, 1302, 1746, 1909, 2075). D-37 and D-44 named "a second incident in one task" as what would make them wrong; from D-48 on the falsifiers named only the low-end case, headroom nobody spends.
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "Rotating more often" (QUESTIONS.md:636) and "rotating at the doubling rather than after the batch" (QUESTIONS.md:687), each named as what would beat the recommended figure.
- **For v3.** Price an adversarial task at its observed stops per task rather than one incident, and price each remaining tenure at the measured tenure average plus resume and tail (suggested here, not in the record).

### The cycle-1 manager ceiling could not be read, and nobody read it across two rotations
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:522, 832, 905–907 (F-67); D-21 (DECISIONS.md:724).
The ceiling was scoped to a session, a session outlived its role, and four checkpoints reported percentages against a governor that could not be read (907).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** D-21's per-rotation delta, bounded by the window in which the session held the lock (DECISIONS.md:724).
- **For v3.** Make the per-rotation delta, with the successor-read true close, the plugin's ceiling method rather than one project's charter row (suggested here, not in the record).

### In-process agents spent 2.7 times the manager half, outside both halves
- **Area.** A11 budget & cost
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-33 (RECORD.md:478–479); RECORD.md:1143.
546,345,463 tokens over 2,171 requests across 40 supervisors and verifiers by 2026-09-14, "counted in neither half of the ceiling" (1143).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** None; "Not the manager's to fix -- the ceiling row is charter-owned" (479).
- **For v3.** Meter in-process agents from their transcripts, as 1143 did by hand (suggested here, not in the record).

### Three checkpoints passed without a rotation, and that one tenure cost 231M tokens
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:1188–1189, 1142, 1159; D-32 (DECISIONS.md:1172).
C-5, C-6 and C-7 passed without the rotation `run` §7b requires, "and nothing records a decision to skip them" (1188). D-32 fixed the rule by client decision, but "Enforcement is by record, not mechanism: nothing refuses a manager that files a checkpoint and keeps the lock" (D-32).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** D-32; D-47 for off-cadence rotations.
- **For v3.** A check that refuses the manager's next write after a filed checkpoint unless `handoff-ready` exists or a recorded decision names the skip (suggested here, not in the record).

### An off-cadence rotation parks the loop until the client acts
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** open
- **Evidence.** F-129 (RECORD.md:1626–1627); gaps at RECORD.md:1670, 1813, 1983.
4 h 13 min at the twelfth lock move with the successor open and idle the whole time (1626); the later gaps were 4 min, 7 min and 26 min (1670, 1813, 1983), which the record reads as sampling "the client's schedule rather than the mechanism" (1670, 1813).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Untested: when `handoff-ready` names a successor the client has already opened, the outgoing manager sends it the resume instruction directly and the announcement becomes a notice, "at the price of the one act that has so far been each succession's only confirmation" (1627).
- **For v3.** The record's remedy.

### Taking the lock in one command was refused three times
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** RECORD.md:262, 523–526, 856, 1623; RECORD.md:263; RECORD.md:1260.
The guard judges `BOARD.md` from disk when the hook fires, so a single command that writes the board and `.run/session/manager` is refused whole (525, confirmed at 856).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** "The fix is one clause in two skills": write `BOARD.md` alone, in its own command (526, 856).
- **For v3.** That clause in `resume` §0 step 4 and `run` §7b, and a distinct guard message for a successor whose predecessor is alive (263) (the message change suggested here, not in the record).

### Work a successor needed kept surfacing only outside the tree, to the last rotation
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff
- **Status at the stop.** worked around in the run
- **Evidence.** Group D above, 11 lines (RECORD.md:259, 260, 1227, 1263, 1382, 1489, 1585, 1631, 1672, 1674, 1985); F-119 (RECORD.md:1383); F-134 (RECORD.md:1817).
NOTES that existed only in a transcript (1263), drafts with no path (1585), reasoning volunteered at two handoffs (1631, 1674), and verifier probes found only by listing a predecessor's scratchpad (1817, 1985).
- **Remedy the record proposes.** Land a dispatch's NOTES whole (1383); a verifier brief names `devteam/.run/scratch/<T-n>/` as its probe directory, or the manager copies the probes there when it lands the verdict (1817).
- **For v3.** Both of the record's remedies in the dispatch templates.

### An announced rotation had no way to be cancelled
- **Area.** A2 rotation & handoff

The stop was recorded by hand with a `cancelled` line added to `handoff-ready` "by analogy with `completed`", "untested against the guard's reading of that file" (2142).
